# Pinch — Technical Design Document

Built against this app's `README.md` and `UX.md`, and against the conventions in `.github/copilot-instructions.md` and `CLAUDE.md`. A-List's `TECHNICAL.md` is the format reference. Like A-List, **every byte of Pinch data is private to one signed-in person**, so there is no invite flow, no roles and no cross-user concurrency. The design weight sits on four things instead: a static ingredient catalog merged with the person's own records, the quantity and cost math, the calendar read model, and an AI reader that fills recipe forms from text, a file or a web page.

## Decisions made in this document

These shape everything below. Each is also listed under [Open questions](#open-questions-for-the-roadmap) where it needs the owner's confirmation.

1. **The catalog is a bundled constant, not a Firestore collection.** About 200 everyday ingredients, their families, nutrition per 100 g, serving sizes and unit conversions ship in the app's own lazy chunk. A person's data refers to them by id (`cat-chicken-breast`). Nothing is copied into a person's account until they change or attach something to a catalog ingredient.
2. **A person's `ingredients` documents are records of what they did, not a copy of the catalog.** A document exists for a custom ingredient, or for a catalog ingredient the person adjusted (nutrition, serving) or attached a product to (the default-product pointer). The client merges catalog and record into one resolved `Ingredient` at read time.
3. **Three layers, one level of grouping.** `family → ingredient → product`. Families don't nest. Recipes and meals refer to an ingredient id only; products carry store, price and size.
4. **Money is integer cents.** Quantities are numbers in a typed unit. Nutrition is stored per 100 g on ingredients and as optional per-serving overrides on recipes. The ingredient form takes it on the label's basis (per an amount of g, oz or servings) and converts before the write, so per 100 g stays the only stored shape; per serving is a display.
5. **Planned meals are copies.** Planning a meal copies its items into a `plannedMeals` document that keeps `sourceMealId`. Editing a saved meal never touches planned days. Items point at recipes and ingredients by id, so a recipe edit reaches every upcoming day.
6. **Past days are frozen once, and priced again only when the person asks.** A planned meal whose day has passed gets a `snapshot` of its cost and nutrition that the app's own freezing writes once and never replaces. Edits to prices, recipes or nutrition first freeze any unfrozen past meals, so history doesn't drift. The one thing that replaces a snapshot is the person's own "Price this day with today's prices" (decision 22).
7. **Anything in use is archived, never removed.** A recipe, custom ingredient or store that anything still points at gets `isArchived: true`. Whether a recipe or ingredient is used on a planned day is answered by one `array-contains` read on `plannedMeals.refIds`, so planned days outside the loaded month window are covered, and whether a recipe still has food in the fridge or freezer by one count of its `IN_STOCK` containers (two equality filters, no index): a recipe whose only use is boxes in the fridge or freezer is archived, and the confirm says "is used in 1 box in your fridge or freezer". A saved meal stays always deletable; its containers keep their own title and stay in stock. Products are deleted outright (their ingredient's default pointer is re-pointed or cleared in the same transaction), a saved meal is always deletable, a catalog ingredient is only ever reset to its built-in values, and the MVP has no way to delete a family.
8. **Cost and nutrition are computed, never stored**, except the snapshot of a past meal. Every derived number returns what it left out, so the UI can say so.
9. **Cost exists only for comparable amounts.** A recipe amount and a product size must share a dimension (mass, volume, count) or be bridged by a conversion the catalog or the person has supplied. Anything else is left out of totals and surfaced as a gap.
10. **Gaps are derived, not stored.** A "nudge" is a pure function of the data (a missing price, size, nutrition value or conversion on an ingredient that something uses). Nothing new is stored for them: "Not now" quiets a nudge until reload, and only the closings older versions recorded (`dismissedNudges`) are still read.
11. **Dates are date-only.** A planned meal's `date` is UTC midnight of the day picked (`fromDateInputValue`), shown with `formatDateUTC`, and keyed on the calendar by `toDateInputValue`. The month grid and the wide week's columns run Sunday to Saturday (open question 4); below `lg` the week is seven days from its anchor, today when it opens (`getPeriodRange(view, anchorKey, isRollingWeek)`), and this week's passed days come from `getEarlierThisWeekKeys`, folded under "Earlier, Oct 11–14" (`formatDayShort` of the first and last day, so the row never reads as the whole week).
12. **One shared Cloud Function, no Pinch-specific ones.** The AI reader is the repo's shared client-side Firebase AI setup and store logos use a publishable browser token, so nothing needs a server secret. Reading a recipe from a link needs a server to fetch the page, so it calls the shared, app-neutral `fetchPageContent` callable (`functions/src/linkMetadata/fetchPageContent.ts`, listed in `functions/README.md`), which returns only the kinds of content the caller asks for; nothing under `functions/` is Pinch's own.
13. **Per-person data nests under `apps/pinch/kitchens/{uid}/…`.** A `kitchens` root (not `users/{uid}`) leaves room for a future shared kitchen to be a read grant on this document, not a data move.
14. **How a list looks is a per-device choice, not data.** Each list's View options (show prices, show calories and macros, the nutrition basis of ingredient rows, and on Recipes and Saved meals the fridge and freezer line) live in the browser's `localStorage` per person and list, so no field, rule or listener is involved and nothing syncs between devices. Prices and the stock line start on and calories and macros start off (`DEFAULT_LIST_VIEW_OPTIONS`); a stored choice always wins over the default, and a stored object missing a key reads that key's default (`parseListViewOptions`, checked by `check:pinch-math`), so a choice stored before the stock switch existed shows the line. Hiding a line only skips drawing it; no total reads these options. The calendar's view (Month or Week) is the same kind of choice, under `pinch:{uid}:calendar-view`: a `?view=` link wins, then the stored choice, then Week below `lg` and Month from `lg` (`DEFAULT_CALENDAR_VIEW`).
15. **Where a recipe came from is its own small collection, named by `sourceId`.** A `Source` is just an id and a label the person typed ("Serious Eats", "Mom's recipe cards"); a recipe points at one with `sourceId`, which is not `Recipe.source` (that field says how the draft was made: by hand, or read from text, a file or a link). Deleting a source clears the pointer on the recipes that name it and leaves the recipes alone; a recipe that names a source that is gone, or has no `sourceId` key at all, simply reads as having none.
16. **The shopping list is one document per ingredient and unit, merged on add.** A `shoppingItems` document is `{ ingredientId, amount, unit, isChecked, sources }` with the id `{ingredientId}__{unit}`, so adding the same thing again lands on the same document and the list never holds two lines for it. Units are never converted (a cup and a tablespoon of one ingredient are two lines). Lines come from a pure function over recipes, meals, planned meals, days and a range of days (`utils/shopping.ts`), and nothing is derived from the calendar afterwards: the list is plain data the person edits, and changing a meal later leaves it as it was. `sources` is what put the item there (planned meal ids, `recipe:<id>:<servings>`, `meal:<id>`). The list stays a snapshot: sources steer an add sheet's defaults, the day drawer's row, the row's "for …" line and Meals on your list, whose Remove takes one meal's share off by working it out again from the meal as it is now (a recipe at the servings its adds kept, the only per-source amount stored), and a deleted planned meal, recipe or saved meal is dropped quietly when names are read. A recipe line with no amount becomes a name-only line (`{ amount: null, unit: 'EACH' }`): null + x = x when it meets an amount on the same item, a batch drops it when another line names the same ingredient with an amount (in any unit), and the list draws a stored name-only item inside that ingredient's amount row on the same side (`getItemRowIds`), so "Parsley" never sits beside "Parsley · 30 g".
17. **A recipe idea is its own small collection, and becoming a recipe is a link, not a move.** An `Idea` is `{ kind, title, note, url, siteName, imageUrl, photoPath, recipeId, … }`: a link, a photo or a few words. It points at the recipe it became with `recipeId`, set by its own write right after the recipe has committed (a failure there is logged and never loses the recipe); nothing is copied back, and deleting the recipe leaves a dangling `recipeId` that every reader treats as "not made yet". A link idea keeps only what a page gives away in its `meta` (title, site name, picture address), fetched through the shared `fetchPageContent` and never read by the AI model.

18. **Cooking mode is a screen, not data.** It reads one recipe, or a planned plate's recipes, from the store, keeps each recipe's step, ticks and servings and the session's timers in component state and memory, and writes nothing of its own: no document, rule, index, Storage path, listener, `localStorage` key or Function. The one write that starts there is the cooks Finish records (decision 19: one per recipe, a plate's in one transaction), then what is put away (decision 23) and a note about that cook (decision 20), the last two only when the person taps them. It opens as an overlay `Subview` (`{ kind: 'cook', recipeId, servings?, plannedMealId?, plate? }`, where `plate` lists a planned plate's recipes to cook with each one's planned servings, `recipeId` first), so the header's back, Escape and the back gesture share one `onBack`, which first puts a timer view in the background (one layer per press), then leaves at once or, while a timer is running or paused, asks "Stop timers and leave?" first; Finish on the last step opens a final panel (§5d) that leaves through the same path. Timers run to a clock deadline, so they are accurate but only while the screen is open: a browser can't ring a web page in the background, and a notification that outlives the page would need push, which this doesn't use.

19. **A cook is a quiet record in its own collection, one document each.** A `Cook` is `{ recipeId, plannedMealId, servings, cookedAt, … }`: that a recipe was made, when (an instant, shown in local time) and at how many servings. It is data the person can correct, so it lives at `apps/pinch/kitchens/{uid}/cooks/{id}` beside `ideas` and `shoppingItems`, owner only. A cook marked from a planned meal has the deterministic id `{plannedMealId}__{recipeId}`, so marking the same meal twice finds the first cook and writes nothing; every other cook (marked from a recipe, from cooking mode started on a recipe, or added by hand for something remembered) has a fresh `generateUuid()` id, and cooking mode started from a planned meal finishes into that meal's own cook. Nothing is derived from cooks beyond a per-recipe count and last date; no total, filter or sort reads them, and a recipe that is deleted takes its cooks with it (§5d).

20. **A recipe's notes are one small collection: what the person wrote, and the record of each edit.** A `RecipeNote` is `{ recipeId, kind: 'NOTE' | 'TWEAK', text, changes, cookId, … }` at `apps/pinch/kitchens/{uid}/notes/{id}`, owner only, one document each, beside `cooks`. A NOTE is the person's own words, optionally tied to the cook it follows up (a cook that is deleted later leaves the note, which then reads as a plain one). A TWEAK is written by the recipe's own save, in the same `writeBatch` as the recipe update, when the recipe has at least one cook and the save changed something the form edits: its `changes` are short plain-word lines computed at save time by the pure `getRecipeChanges` and are never rewritten (the rules hold them), while its `text`, the optional reason, can be edited. `Recipe` gets no new field: the recipe's last change is the newest TWEAK's `createdAt`, read by `selectNoteSummaries` (the drawer's status line adds "· changed Oct 20" only when it is newer than the last cook), and a NOTE older than it is labelled "Written before your Oct 20 tweak", so it is always clear which version of the recipe a note was about. Nothing else reads notes; no total, filter or sort does.

21. **A recipe's care is one nullable map on the recipe, not a collection.** `Recipe.care` is `{ fridgeDays, freezerDays, reheat, tip } | null`: how long what the recipe makes keeps in the fridge and the freezer, how to reheat it, and a tip. It is something the recipe form edits, so the recipe's own save writes it (a required key, `null` when all four parts are empty) and a recipe written before it has no key, which reads as no care. Days are whole numbers from 1 to 365; weeks and months exist only in how days are typed and shown (a month is 30 days, so 90 shows as "3 months"), never in the data. `reheat` and `tip` are trimmed text of at most 300 characters. The three readings fill it for review only, from what the source says and nothing else. Only the form, the drawer and cooking mode's last screen read it; no total, filter or sort does, and a change to it never records a tweak.

22. **A past day is priced again by one explicit action that replaces its snapshots, in place.** Decided by the owner: "Price this day with today's prices", one tap and a confirm, with no per-product price history. It keeps every planned meal's `id` (a cook marked from a meal is `{plannedMealId}__{recipeId}`, so a changed id would orphan "Cooked ✓") and every other field, and replaces only `snapshot` and `updatedAt`, in one `runTransaction` per day (§3). The snapshot gains `repricedAt: number | null` (null when the app froze the day, an instant when the person asked) and the rules allow replacing a snapshot only with one that carries it. Rejected: deleting and re-creating each meal in a batch, which needs no rules change but leaves the rules unable to tell a re-price from any other rewrite of a meal, so the marker and the "meal otherwise untouched" checks (criterion 6) would have nothing to hold on to; it was reasoned about and not tried on the emulator.


23. **A fridge or freezer box is one document per physical container, owner only, with a short history of its own.** A `Container` at `apps/pinch/kitchens/{uid}/containers/{id}` is food made ahead and kept in the fridge or freezer: `title`, `recipeId` / `mealId` / `cookId` (all `T | null`, immutable; a recipe and a saved meal never both), `servings` (whole portions, 1 to 100), `status` (`IN_STOCK` | `DONE`), `place` (`FRIDGE` | `FREEZER`), `eatBy` and `history`. It is never split: eating some lowers `servings` and adds an EAT; the last portion, or a toss, sets `status: DONE` and the document stays as history (a finished container keeps the portions it ended with, so `servings` is never 0); Delete is a separate action that erases the history and says so. `eatBy` is a date-only value (UTC midnight, written with `fromDateInputValue`, shown through the planned-day helpers that read UTC) meaning "eat by the latest", never an instant; "Eat soon" (a tinted "Eat today" / "Eat by tomorrow") is the viewer's local today and tomorrow against that stored day (`getLocalTodayDate`), "Past its date" is any day before it. Event `at` values are instants shown in local time. `history` holds at most 10 events `{ kind, at, servings, place, note }` of kind MADE, FRIDGE, FREEZE, THAW, EAT, TOSS or EDIT, oldest first: the rules can only walk a list by index and the evaluator stops at 1,000 expressions per request (criterion 13), and past the cap the oldest EAT goes first, then the oldest EDIT, then the oldest move after the first place event that is not a FREEZE (the first place event says how many went in and the FREEZEs are what "Frozen twice" counts, so only a long run of moves reaches the oldest thaws). A cook's container starts with MADE (what the cook made) then where it went; one added by hand starts with where it went alone. Every change that depends on the document's own state is a `runTransaction` that re-reads it and applies a pure plan (`planEat`, `planMove`, `planEatBy`, `planToss`, `planCount`, `planUndoEnd` in `utils/containers.ts`), so another device's change is never written over and an impossible change stops with one plain sentence; "Put some away" is one `writeBatch` of one to twenty containers (a person who puts portions in separate boxes says how many boxes; the servings split evenly), accepted locally without waiting for the server. The default eat-by is the person's own call and says so: from the recipe's `care` (`fridgeDays` / `freezerDays`) when it has them, else a named default (`CONTAINER_FRIDGE_DAYS` 4, `CONTAINER_FREEZER_DAYS` 90), and moving out of the freezer starts at the shorter of the thawed window (`CONTAINER_THAWED_DAYS` 2), the recipe's fridge days and the date the container already had; a container already past its date keeps that date through a move, and one that has been frozen before says so; nothing in the app says food is safe. What is in stock is one listener on `status == 'IN_STOCK'` (a lone equality, no index) in the usual optional-extra shape; Finished containers are history, read on demand (§5g). Rejected: a document per portion by default (a bulk meal would be fifteen writes and fifteen rows; a box is the unit, and "how many boxes" is there for the person who does portion it), a `Recipe.servingsInStock` counter (no history, and a meal or a typed name would need its own), and keeping every container on one always-open listener (finished ones grow without end).

---

## Data Schema

Namespace root: `apps/pinch/`

**Per-person private tree.** Everything lives under the person's own kitchen, keyed by their `uid`:

```
apps/pinch/kitchens/{uid}                         → KitchenSettings
apps/pinch/kitchens/{uid}/ingredients/{id}        → IngredientRecord
apps/pinch/kitchens/{uid}/families/{id}           → Family
apps/pinch/kitchens/{uid}/stores/{id}             → Store
apps/pinch/kitchens/{uid}/products/{id}           → Product
apps/pinch/kitchens/{uid}/recipes/{id}            → Recipe
apps/pinch/kitchens/{uid}/sources/{id}            → Source
apps/pinch/kitchens/{uid}/collections/{id}        → Collection
apps/pinch/kitchens/{uid}/shoppingItems/{id}      → ShoppingItem
apps/pinch/kitchens/{uid}/ideas/{id}              → Idea
apps/pinch/kitchens/{uid}/cooks/{id}              → Cook
apps/pinch/kitchens/{uid}/notes/{id}              → RecipeNote
apps/pinch/kitchens/{uid}/containers/{id}         → Container
apps/pinch/kitchens/{uid}/meals/{id}              → Meal
apps/pinch/kitchens/{uid}/plannedMeals/{id}       → PlannedMeal

Storage:
apps/pinch/kitchens/{uid}/recipes/{recipeId}/cover.webp
apps/pinch/kitchens/{uid}/stores/{storeId}/logo.webp
apps/pinch/kitchens/{uid}/ideas/{ideaId}/photo.webp
```

Why nested under the `uid`: every rule is the same one-line path check, every listener is a plain collection listener, and a mis-scoped query can't leak across people. Products are a top-level collection, not nested under an ingredient, so Stores & prices and the cost math read them with one listener.

**Every field is a required key typed `T | null`, never optional.** Absent values are written as explicit `null`; arrays default to `[]`. Timestamps are millisecond numbers. Ids come from `generateUuid` (`@/utils`); catalog ids are the fixed strings `cat-…` and are never generated.

**Enums and constants** (fixed lists only the developer extends; option arrays are derived from `constants.ts` at render time, never redeclared):

```typescript
export type MealType = 'BREAKFAST' | 'LUNCH' | 'DINNER' | 'SNACK' | 'DESSERT' | 'DRINK';
export type ItemKind = 'RECIPE' | 'INGREDIENT';
export type Unit =
  | 'G' | 'KG' | 'OZ' | 'LB'                        // mass
  | 'ML' | 'L' | 'TSP' | 'TBSP' | 'CUP' | 'FL_OZ' | 'PINCH' // volume
  | 'EACH';                                          // count
export type Dimension = 'MASS' | 'VOLUME' | 'COUNT';
export type NutritionMetric = 'calories' | 'protein' | 'carbs' | 'fat';
export type RecipeSource = 'MANUAL' | 'AI_TEXT' | 'AI_FILE' | 'AI_LINK';   // how a recipe draft was made; where it came from is a `Source`, named by `Recipe.sourceId`
export type RecipeStartMethod = 'text' | 'file' | 'link' | 'manual';   // UI only: how a new recipe begins, never stored
export type IdeaKind = 'LINK' | 'PHOTO' | 'TEXT';
export type IdeaStartMethod = 'link' | 'photo' | 'text';              // UI only: how a new idea begins, never stored
export type NoteKind = 'NOTE' | 'TWEAK';                              // a journal entry, or the record of an edit to the recipe
export type ContainerPlace = 'FRIDGE' | 'FREEZER';
export type ContainerStatus = 'IN_STOCK' | 'DONE';
export type ContainerEventKind = 'MADE' | 'FRIDGE' | 'FREEZE' | 'THAW' | 'EAT' | 'TOSS' | 'EDIT';
export type GapKind = 'NO_PRODUCT' | 'NOT_COMPARABLE' | 'NO_NUTRITION';
```

```typescript
// constants.ts (runtime values live here, never in types.ts)
export const MEAL_TYPE_ORDER: MealType[] = ['BREAKFAST', 'LUNCH', 'DINNER', 'SNACK', 'DESSERT', 'DRINK'];
export const MEAL_TYPE_LABELS: Record<MealType, string> = {
  BREAKFAST: 'Breakfast', LUNCH: 'Lunch', DINNER: 'Dinner', SNACK: 'Snack', DESSERT: 'Dessert', DRINK: 'Drink',
};
export const MEAL_TYPE_EMOJIS: Record<MealType, string> = {
  BREAKFAST: '🍳', LUNCH: '🥪', DINNER: '🍝', SNACK: '🍿', DESSERT: '🍰', DRINK: '🥤',
};
export const UNIT_DIMENSION: Record<Unit, Dimension> = {
  G: 'MASS', KG: 'MASS', OZ: 'MASS', LB: 'MASS',
  ML: 'VOLUME', L: 'VOLUME', TSP: 'VOLUME', TBSP: 'VOLUME', CUP: 'VOLUME', FL_OZ: 'VOLUME', PINCH: 'VOLUME',
  EACH: 'COUNT',
};
export const UNIT_TO_BASE: Record<Unit, number> = {   // grams for MASS, millilitres for VOLUME, 1 for COUNT
  G: 1, KG: 1000, OZ: 28.3495, LB: 453.592,
  ML: 1, L: 1000, TSP: 4.92892, TBSP: 14.7868, CUP: 236.588, FL_OZ: 29.5735, PINCH: 0.308,
  EACH: 1,
};
export const UNIT_LABELS: Record<Unit, string> = { /* 'g', 'kg', 'oz', 'lb', 'ml', 'l', 'tsp', 'tbsp', 'cup', 'fl oz', 'pinch', 'each' */ };
export const NUTRITION_METRICS: NutritionMetric[] = ['calories', 'protein', 'carbs', 'fat'];
export const CALENDAR_VIEWS = ['month', 'week'] as const;
export const DEFAULT_CALENDAR_VIEW = { wide: 'month', narrow: 'week' };   // Month from lg, Week (from today) below it
export const MONTH_CELL_MEAL_ORDER: MealType[] = ['DINNER', 'LUNCH', 'BREAKFAST', 'SNACK', 'DESSERT', 'DRINK'];   // dinner is never the meal behind "+N"
export const MAX_RECIPE_INGREDIENTS = 60;
export const MAX_RECIPE_STEPS = 60;
export const MAX_CARE_DAYS = 365;                // a recipe's fridge or freezer days: the cap the rules enforce
export const MAX_CARE_REHEAT_LENGTH = 300;        // a recipe's reheating line and its tip: the caps the rules enforce
export const MAX_CARE_TIP_LENGTH = 300;
export const MAX_MEAL_ITEMS = 10;                // the most items a rule can check by index (a 12-item check exceeds the rules evaluator's limit)
export const MAX_PASTE_CHARS = 20_000;            // text the AI reader accepts
export const MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES = 10 * 1024 * 1024; // photo, screenshot or PDF
export const LIST_SEARCH_THRESHOLD = 8;           // a list gets a search from this many rows
export const MAX_NUTRITION_VALUE = 100_000;       // per 100 g, the cap the rules enforce
export const NUTRITION_ENTRY_UNITS = [...];       // what a typed label is "per": g, oz, serving
export const NUTRITION_BASIS_OPTIONS = [...];     // what a shown number is per: SERVING, PER_100G
export const RECIPE_START_METHODS = [...];        // text, file, link, manual: id, emoji, title, description (the one list the drawer, the page and each reader's title read)
export const MAX_DISMISSED_NUDGES = 500;
export const MAX_SOURCE_LABEL_LENGTH = 80;       // a source's label, the cap the rules enforce
export const SOURCE_BATCH_SIZE = 400;             // writes per batch when removing a source reaches many recipes
export const SOURCE_FILTER_ANY = 'any';           // the Recipes filter's two answers that aren't a source id
export const SOURCE_FILTER_NONE = 'none';
export const MONTH_WINDOW_PADDING = 2;            // months loaded on each side of the window's centre
export const MAX_SHOPPING_LINES_PER_ADD = 200;    // distinct lines one add takes: the action and the UI, not a rule
export const SOFT_MAX_SHOPPING_ITEMS = 300;       // items before adding asks to clear what is checked off: the UI, not a rule
export const SHOPPING_GROUP_PAGE_SIZE = 100;      // rows a shop section draws before "Show N more"
export const SHOP_SECTIONS = ['produce', 'meat', 'dairy', 'bakery', 'pantry', 'spices', 'snacks', 'frozen', 'other'] as const;   // the order a shop is walked
export const SHOP_SECTION_BY_FAMILY / SHOP_SECTION_BY_INGREDIENT / USUALLY_AT_HOME_IDS;   // static maps: a catalog family (or one ingredient shelved elsewhere) to its section; the person's own families are Other
export const SHOP_SECTION_DIVIDER_MIN_ROWS = 8;   // under this many rows the list is one run in shop order, with no dividers
export const SHOPPING_ADD_MODES = ['one', 'plan'] as const;   // the Shopping tab's add sheet: "One thing" and "From your plan"
export const SECTION_COUNT_THRESHOLD = 10;       // a section heading counts its rows only past this many
export const SHOPPING_MOVE_DELAY_MS = 1200;       // how long a ticked or un-ticked row stays where it was drawn before it moves
export const SHOPPING_BATCH_SIZE = 250;           // items per transaction when clearing what is checked off (a read and a delete each, under 500)
export const MAX_IDEA_TITLE_LENGTH = 120;         // an idea's title, note, link and site name: the caps the rules enforce
export const MAX_IDEA_NOTE_LENGTH = 1000;
export const MAX_IDEA_URL_LENGTH = 2000;
export const MAX_IDEA_SITE_NAME_LENGTH = 120;
export const SOFT_MAX_IDEAS = 200;                // ideas before adding asks to delete one: the action and the screens, not a rule
export const IDEA_LOOKUP_TIMEOUT_MS = 6000;       // how long a link's page may take to look up
export const IDEA_PHOTO_MAX_DIMENSION = 1280;     // the longest edge of an idea's photo
export const IDEA_KIND_EMOJIS = { LINK: '🔗', PHOTO: '📷', TEXT: '📝' };
export const IDEA_START_METHODS = [...];          // link, photo, text: id, emoji, title, description (the one list the picker reads)
export const MAX_SHOPPING_AMOUNT = 1_000_000;     // an item's amount, the cap the rules enforce
export const MAX_RECIPE_NOTE_LENGTH = 2000;       // a note's text, the cap the rules enforce
export const MAX_TWEAK_CHANGES = 8;               // a tweak's lines, "and N more" included: the cap the rules enforce
export const MAX_TWEAK_CHANGE_LENGTH = 200;       // one line of a tweak, the cap the rules enforce
export const SOFT_MAX_RECIPE_NOTES = 300;         // notes before adding asks to delete one: the action and the screens, not a rule
export const RECIPE_NOTE_BATCH_SIZE = 400;        // writes per batch when a recipe's removal deletes its notes
export const RECIPE_NOTES_FIRST_PAGE = 5;         // entries the journal draws before "Show N more"
export const RECIPE_NOTES_PAGE_SIZE = 50;
export const NOTE_COOK_CHOICES = 4;               // a recipe's latest cooks offered as answers in the note sheet
export const MAX_CONTAINER_SERVINGS = 100;        // a container's portions, and an event's: the caps the rules enforce
export const MAX_CONTAINER_HISTORY = 10;          // events a container keeps: the cap the rules enforce (the evaluator's budget, criterion 13)
export const MAX_CONTAINER_NOTE_LENGTH = 80;      // an EDIT's note, the cap the rules enforce
export const MAX_CONTAINER_TITLE_LENGTH = 120;    // a container's title (a recipe's can be 200): the cap the rules enforce, applied when the container is built
export const MAX_CONTAINERS_PER_PUT_AWAY = 20;    // containers one put-away writes in one batch
export const MIN_EAT_BY_DATE = 946_684_800_000;   // 2000-01-01 and 2100-01-01 (UTC midnight): the bounds the rules enforce on `eatBy`
export const MAX_EAT_BY_DATE = 4_102_444_800_000;
export const CONTAINER_FRIDGE_DAYS = 4;           // eat-by starting points when the recipe has none: the person's own call
export const CONTAINER_FREEZER_DAYS = 90;
export const CONTAINER_THAWED_DAYS = 2;
export const CONTAINER_EAT_SOON_DAYS = 1;         // today and tomorrow are tinted; a wider window tints most of a box's life
export const CONTAINER_FREEZER_DATE_DAYS = 7;     // a freezer row names its eat-by only this close or past
export const BULK_MEAL_SERVINGS = 5;              // a cooking session at or above this asks about putting some away
export const CONTAINER_SITTING_SERVINGS = 2;      // what one sitting is taken to use, for the put-away default
export const SOFT_MAX_CONTAINERS = 500;           // containers in stock before adding asks to finish one: the action and the screens, not a rule
export const CONTAINER_LIST_PAGE_SIZE = 20;       // rows a place draws before "Show N more"
export const CONTAINER_FINISHED_PAGE_SIZE = 25;   // finished containers read per page
export const CONTAINER_QUERY_LIMIT = 200;         // containers read for one recipe or meal (finished ones are never purged)
export const CONTAINER_PLACE_EMOJIS = { FRIDGE: '🥛', FREEZER: '❄️' };   // two that don't both read "frozen" (the recipe's Keeping it section draws the same two)
export const CONTAINER_EMOJI = '🍱';
```

### 1. `KitchenSettings` — `apps/pinch/kitchens/{uid}`

```typescript
interface NutritionSwitches { calories: boolean; protein: boolean; carbs: boolean; fat: boolean }

interface KitchenSettings {
  uid: string;                                  // immutable
  showNutrition: NutritionSwitches;             // all false until switched on
  dismissedNudges: Record<string, number>;      // "NO_PRODUCT:cat-soy-sauce" → dismissedAt (ms)
  createdAt: number;                            // immutable
  updatedAt: number;
}
```

The document is created the first time the person changes a setting (older documents may also have been created by a dismissal, which the app no longer writes); until then the client uses defaults (`isLoaded` with `settings === null` means defaults). There is no setup step. `dismissedNudges` is capped at `MAX_DISMISSED_NUDGES` keys.

### 2. `IngredientRecord` — `…/ingredients/{id}`

```typescript
interface NutritionValues { calories: number | null; protein: number | null; carbs: number | null; fat: number | null }

interface IngredientRecord {
  id: string;                                   // a generated id (custom) or the catalog id (an adjusted catalog ingredient)
  catalogId: string | null;                     // immutable; null for a custom ingredient
  name: string | null;                          // required for custom; null means "use the catalog name"
  familyId: string | null;                      // a catalog family id (cat-fam-…) or a Family id; null = its own family
  iconKey: string | null;                       // @deprecated: icons were removed; old documents may carry one, new writes null
  nutritionPer100g: NutritionValues | null;     // null = the catalog's values (or none, for custom); always per 100 g, whatever basis the form was typed in
  servingAmount: number | null;                 // what "1 serving" of this ingredient is (null = catalog / 100 g)
  servingUnit: Unit | null;
  gramsPerMl: number | null;                    // density, bridges mass <-> volume (user-supplied; editing UI is Next Steps)
  gramsEach: number | null;                     // weight of one, bridges mass <-> count (same)
  defaultProductId: string | null;
  isArchived: boolean;                          // custom ingredients only; a document written before archiving lacks the key (reads as false, backfilled on edit)
  createdAt: number;                            // immutable
  updatedAt: number;
}
```

For a catalog ingredient the document id *is* the catalog id, so there is exactly one record per catalog ingredient and "upsert" is a plain `setDoc` with merge-free full shape on first write. For a custom ingredient the id is `generateUuid()`.

**Resolved ingredient (client-only, never stored).** `resolveIngredient(id)` = catalog entry (if `cat-`) overlaid by the record: `name`, `familyId`, `nutritionPer100g`, serving and conversions each fall back to the catalog when the record's field is `null`. An id with neither a catalog entry nor a record resolves to a placeholder `"Removed ingredient"` so a dangling reference can never crash a screen.

### 3. `Family` — `…/families/{id}`

```typescript
interface Family { id: string; name: string; createdAt: number; updatedAt: number }
```

Only the person's own families live here. Catalog families (`cat-fam-chicken`) are constants. A family is a grouping label with no data of its own and no parent.

### 4. `Store` — `…/stores/{id}`

```typescript
interface Store {
  id: string;
  name: string;
  catalogStoreId: string | null;               // set when picked from the well-known list; immutable
  imagePath: string | null;                    // Storage path of the person's own image
  isArchived: boolean;                         // hidden once products point at it; adding the same catalog store back un-archives it; legacy documents lack the key
  createdAt: number;
  updatedAt: number;
}
```

A well-known store becomes a `Store` document the first time a product uses it, so every product points at a store the person owns. Its logo is derived from the catalog entry's domain; a person's own store shows `imagePath` (its download URL is a TanStack query keyed by path and `updatedAt`, `storageImageQueryOptions`; `useStoragePhoto` only uploads and deletes), else a letter tile.

### 5. `Product` — `…/products/{id}`

```typescript
interface Product {
  id: string;
  ingredientId: string;                        // immutable
  storeId: string;
  label: string | null;                        // "Great Value"
  priceCents: number;
  sizeAmount: number;                          // > 0
  sizeUnit: Unit;
  url: string | null;                          // https only
  createdAt: number;
  updatedAt: number;
}
```

The default product is the ingredient's `defaultProductId`, not a flag on the product, so changing the default is one field write on one document. The first product added to an ingredient becomes its default in the same batch.

### 6. `Recipe` — `…/recipes/{id}`

```typescript
interface RecipeIngredient {
  id: string;                                  // row id, so reorder/edit keys are stable
  ingredientId: string;
  amount: number | null;                       // > 0; null = no amount ("to taste"), written with unit 'EACH'
  unit: Unit;
  note: string | null;                         // "diced"
  group: string | null;                        // the heading it sits under ("For the sauce"), 1 … 40 characters; a missing key (lines written before groups) reads as null
}

interface RecipeCare {
  fridgeDays: number | null;                   // whole days, 1 … 365
  freezerDays: number | null;
  reheat: string | null;                       // trimmed, 1 … 300 characters
  tip: string | null;                          // trimmed, 1 … 300 characters
}

interface RecipeLink {
  id: string;
  kind: 'HOW_TO' | 'ARTICLE' | 'ORIGINAL' | 'TECHNIQUE' | 'OTHER';   // at most one HOW_TO per recipe
  label: string | null;                        // the person's own, 1 … 40 characters; null shows the kind's default ("How-to video", "Article", "Original recipe", "Technique", "Link")
  url: string;                                 // an http(s) address, trimmed, at most 2,000 characters
}

interface Recipe {
  id: string;
  title: string;
  description: string | null;
  servings: number;                            // > 0, halves allowed
  prepMinutes: number | null;
  cookMinutes: number | null;
  coverPath: string | null;
  ingredients: RecipeIngredient[];             // 1 … MAX_RECIPE_INGREDIENTS
  steps: string[];                             // 0 … MAX_RECIPE_STEPS
  nutritionOverride: NutritionValues | null;   // per serving; a null metric falls back to the derived value
  source: RecipeSource;                        // how the draft was made, never where it came from
  sourceId: string | null;                     // a Source id; a missing key (recipes written before sources) or an id no source has reads as none
  collectionIds: string[];                     // the Collections it is in, 0 … 20; a missing key (recipes written before collections) reads as [], an id no collection has is ignored
  care: RecipeCare | null;                     // how to keep the leftovers; a missing key (recipes written before care) reads as null, and a map with nothing in it is written as null
  links: RecipeLink[];                         // the how-to video and other links, 0 … 8; a missing key (recipes written before links) reads as []
  isArchived: boolean;
  createdAt: number;
  updatedAt: number;
}
```

`care` is the person's own storage notes. Every reader goes through `getRecipeCare(recipe)` (`utils/recipeCare.ts`), which turns a missing key, `null`, a map with nothing in it and any part that isn't usable (days outside 1 to 365, text that is blank) into null for that part or for the whole record, so a recipe written before care existed, one written with `care: null` and one whose form was saved empty are all read the same way. The recipe's own save writes `care` on every save (new or edit), so an edit backfills the key on an older recipe in the same field-scoped `updateDoc`, and nothing else ever writes it.

A line with no amount never scales, adds nothing to cost or nutrition and is never a gap (`getLineCost` returns `{ cents: null, gap: null }` and `getRecipeNutrition` skips it, so it is never "missing"), and it is left out of `getPricedCoverage`'s count, `selectUsedIngredientIds` ("Needs a price") and `getIngredientUses`. The rules don't walk recipe lines, so no rule changed for it. Every recipe stored before this has amounts; readers take both and nothing is migrated.

**Ingredient groups.** A line's `group` is the heading it sits under, nothing more: there is no group collection, a group exists while a line carries its name, and one ingredient may have a line in several groups (soy sauce ¼ cup for the chicken and 2 tbsp for the sauce). Every list of a recipe's lines goes through `groupIngredientLines(lines)` (`utils/ingredientGroups.ts`, pure, checked by `check:pinch-math`): the lines with no group first and with no heading (below a group they would read as part of it), then each group in the order its first line appears, two spellings of one name ignoring case and spaces being one group shown as its first line spells it. The drawer's Ingredients, the form's rows, cooking's Ingredients view and the step matching use it, so the order is the same everywhere, and a recipe with no groups is one block with no heading, exactly as before. `cleanGroupName` stores a name trimmed, spaces collapsed and cut to `MAX_INGREDIENT_GROUP_LENGTH` (40); blank is null. The form keeps its rows in that order on every change (`placeLineInGroup` puts a moved or new line at the end of its group, a new group last; `renameLineGroup` renames every line of a group and, given another group's name, makes them one under that group's spelling; `ungroupLines` takes a group away and keeps its lines, and `restoreLineGroup` is that removal's Undo), so the saved order is the shown order. Nothing merges two lines of one ingredient: the form saves both, the reader's normalizer keeps both, and `getDataFromExtracted` adds a second line into the first only under the same heading; the shopping list sums them per ingredient and unit on purpose (`getRecipeLines`), and cost and nutrition add lines up, each keyed by its line id. Where a line shows away from its heading (a step's "For this step", a tweak's change lines) `getRepeatedLineGroups` names its group, only when its ingredient sits in more than one group ("Soy sauce · For the sauce", "Added Soy sauce (For the sauce), 2 tbsp"). Moving lines between groups is not a change to the dish, so `getRecipeChanges` compares lines without their group and checks the order within each group: a regroup on its own records no tweak, like a change to how a recipe keeps. The 40-character cap and the trimming live in the client only (`cleanGroupName`, the field's `maxLength`); the rules don't walk the lines (Security Rules, criterion 14). Search ignores group names. Legacy lines read as no group, and the recipe's own save writes every line with `group` (null when none), so an edit backfills the key.

### 6a. `Source` — `…/sources/{id}`

```typescript
interface Source {
  id: string;                                  // generateUuid
  label: string;                               // trimmed, 1 … 80 characters, unique ignoring case
  createdAt: number;
  updatedAt: number;
}
```

Nothing else: a source has no image, URL, notes or archive state. `Recipe.source` and `Recipe.sourceId` are two different things on purpose: `source` is the existing `'MANUAL' | 'AI_TEXT' | 'AI_FILE' | 'AI_LINK'` (how a draft was made, set once and kept on edits), and `sourceId` is the person's own answer to "where did you get it from?". Every reader goes through `getRecipeSource(recipe, sourcesById)` (`utils/sources.ts`), which treats a missing key, `null` and an id with no matching source alike as "no source", so a recipe written before this field, one whose source was deleted on another device, and one created while the delete ran are all read the same way. Uniqueness of the label is checked against the loaded list only (in `renameSource`, the recipe form's reuse of a typed label, and the form sheet's live note), so two devices adding the same label at the same moment can leave two sources with one label; nothing breaks, and the person can rename or delete one.

### 6a.2. `Collection` — `…/collections/{id}`

```typescript
interface Collection {
  id: string;                                  // generateUuid
  label: string;                               // trimmed, 1 … 40 characters, unique ignoring case, spaces and hyphens ("High-protein" and "high protein" are one)
  createdAt: number;
  updatedAt: number;
}
```

A collection is a named set of recipes the person made ("Bulk", "High-protein", "Weeknight"); `Recipe.collectionIds` names the ones a recipe is in, so a rename follows every recipe and a recipe can be in many. It has no image, colour, notes or archive state. Readers tolerate every legacy and stale shape: a recipe written before collections has no key (read as in none, and the next save of its form writes `[]`), and an id no collection has (deleted on another device, or not loaded yet) is ignored by every reader (`getRecipeCollections`, `getCollectionCounts`, `filterByCollection`) and dropped by the next save of the form. The UI word comes from one pair of constants (`COLLECTION_NOUN`, `COLLECTION_NOUN_PLURAL`), because an ingredient line's "group" already means something on a recipe screen. See §5a.2.

### 6b. `ShoppingItem` — `…/shoppingItems/{id}`

```typescript
interface ShoppingItem {
  id: string;                                  // `${ingredientId}__${unit}`; immutable
  ingredientId: string;                        // a catalog id or a custom ingredient's id; immutable; 1 … 80 characters
  amount: number | null;                       // > 0, at most 1,000,000, two decimals; null = just the name, always unit 'EACH'
  unit: Unit;                                  // immutable: a different unit is a different item
  isChecked: boolean;                          // got it: shown under "Got it" until cleared
  sources: string[];                           // what put it on the list: planned meal ids, 'recipe:<id>:<servings>' (the servings this item holds of that recipe; an older 'recipe:<id>' reads as the recipe's own servings), 'meal:<id>'; oldest first, at most 60; [] for a typed line. Missing on items written before sources: readers use ?? [], and an add sheet treats such an open line as on the list from somewhere unknown (starts unticked)
  createdAt: number;
  updatedAt: number;
}
```

The id carries the identity, so no query is needed to find "the line for chicken thigh in pounds": a transaction reads that one document. A unit's name never holds `__` (`FL_OZ` has one underscore), so the last `__` always splits the id. The list stores no name, family or price: those are read through the ingredient at render time, so renaming an ingredient or moving it to another family reaches the list, and an ingredient that is gone reads as "Removed ingredient" in the "Other" group. Items are plain documents with no archive state; the person deletes them. `amount` is `null` for a line with only a name, and `sources` (at most 60 entries) says what put the line on the list; the client writes both, and the rules read `sources` with `get('sources', [])`, so a line written before it reads exactly as before. Sources are merged from the add transaction's own read (the added ones move to the end and the oldest go past 60), not with `arrayUnion`, which can't keep them under the rules' cap of 60. A ticked line that an add starts again keeps its old sources with the new ones, so a day that was already bought is still known as added.

### 6c. `Idea` — `…/ideas/{id}`

```typescript
interface Idea {
  id: string;                                  // generateUuid; immutable
  kind: IdeaKind;                              // 'LINK' | 'PHOTO' | 'TEXT'
  title: string;                               // trimmed, 1 … 120 characters
  note: string | null;                         // 1 … 1,000 characters, or null
  url: string | null;                          // http(s), at most 2,000 characters; a LINK idea's page
  siteName: string | null;                     // the page's own name (og:site_name), else the link's host; at most 120 characters
  imageUrl: string | null;                     // https only, at most 2,000 characters; the page's picture, a third party's address
  photoPath: string | null;                    // Storage path of a PHOTO idea's picture: apps/pinch/kitchens/{uid}/ideas/{id}/photo.webp
  recipeId: string | null;                     // the recipe it became; a missing key, an id no recipe has, or an archived recipe reads as not made
  createdAt: number;                           // immutable
  updatedAt: number;
}
```

`kind` decides which of `url`, `siteName`, `imageUrl` and `photoPath` carry a value (a LINK has the first three, a PHOTO the last, a TEXT none), but the rules don't check that pairing: the client writes it and every reader tolerates any mix. The collection is new, so the rules require all eleven keys (no older shape exists) and `kind` can't change after the idea is created. Readers still default a key with `?? null`, and `updateIdea` and the recipe link write back any key a document lacks (`getMissingIdeaFields`), which only matters for a document written outside the app. The list's "made" test is `selectIdeaGroups`: `recipeId` is set and a listed (non-archived) recipe has that id, so a deleted recipe, an archived one and an id that never existed all read as "still to try".

### 6d. `Cook` — `…/cooks/{id}`

```typescript
interface Cook {
  id: string;                                  // `${plannedMealId}__${recipeId}` from a planned meal, else generateUuid; immutable
  recipeId: string;                            // 1 … 80 characters; immutable; an id no recipe has reads as no recipe and is ignored
  plannedMealId: string | null;                // 1 … 80 characters when set; immutable; null for a cook marked from a recipe, from cooking mode, or added by hand
  servings: number;                            // > 0, at most 1,000, halves allowed
  cookedAt: number;                            // an instant (ms): when it was made; at most a day after the server's clock
  createdAt: number;                           // immutable
  updatedAt: number;
}
```

`cookedAt` is a real moment, not a calendar day: it is written from a date and time (`fromLocalDateAndTimeInputValues`), read back with `toLocalDateInputValue` and `toLocalTimeInputValue` and displayed in the viewer's own time zone with `formatDateShort` or `formatDateTime`. A cook marked from a planned meal on today gets `Date.now()`; one on a day that has passed gets local noon of that day (`getPlannedCookedAt`), so the day label is right west of UTC as well as east; a day still ahead can't be marked. The editable fields are `cookedAt`, `servings` and `updatedAt`; the rules hold `id`, `recipeId`, `plannedMealId` and `createdAt` fixed. A cook whose `plannedMealId` points at a planned meal that was later removed stays as it is: the history says "From your plan" and, while that meal is still loaded, the day it was planned for. Readers go through `?? null`, and an edit writes `plannedMealId: null` back when an older document lacks the key (`getMissingCookFields`), because the rules want every key on the merged document.


### 6e. `RecipeNote` — `…/notes/{id}`

```typescript
interface RecipeNote {
  id: string;                                  // generateUuid; immutable
  recipeId: string;                            // 1 … 80 characters; immutable; an id no recipe has reads as no recipe and is ignored
  kind: NoteKind;                              // 'NOTE' | 'TWEAK'; immutable
  text: string | null;                         // 1 … 2,000 characters; required (never null) for a NOTE, the optional reason for a TWEAK
  changes: string[];                           // at most 8 lines of 1 … 200 characters, fixed when a TWEAK is saved; always [] for a NOTE; immutable
  cookId: string | null;                       // 1 … 80 characters when set; the cook a NOTE follows up (a TWEAK writes null); immutable; a cook that no longer exists reads as none
  createdAt: number;                           // an instant (ms), shown in local time; immutable
  updatedAt: number;
}
```

A note's date is a real moment (`Date.now()` from the device, or the recipe's own `updatedAt` for a tweak, so the two writes of one save carry the same time), displayed with `formatDateShort` in the viewer's time zone. Only `text` and `updatedAt` are edited after the fact. The collection is new, so the rules require all eight keys; readers still default `cookId`, `changes` and `text` (`?? null`, `?? []`) and `updateNote` writes back any key a document lacks (`getMissingNoteFields`), which only matters for a document made outside the app. `Recipe` is unchanged and so is every existing document.

### 6f. `Container` — `…/containers/{id}`

```typescript
interface ContainerEvent {
  kind: ContainerEventKind;                    // MADE | FRIDGE | FREEZE | THAW | EAT | TOSS | EDIT
  at: number;                                  // an instant (ms), shown in local time
  servings: number | null;                     // whole portions 1 … 100 the event affected; null for an EDIT
  place: ContainerPlace | null;                // where it went: set for FRIDGE, FREEZE and THAW, else null
  note: string | null;                         // at most 80 characters; set for an EDIT ("Eat by Oct 14 → Oct 18"), else null
}

interface Container {
  id: string;                                  // generateUuid; immutable
  title: string;                               // 1 … 120 characters; the recipe's or meal's name when it was saved, or what the person typed
  recipeId: string | null;                     // 1 … 80 characters; immutable; a recipe that no longer exists reads as none and the container keeps its title
  mealId: string | null;                       // 1 … 80 characters; immutable; never set together with recipeId
  cookId: string | null;                       // 1 … 80 characters; immutable; the cook it came from (cooking mode's Finish panel); a cook that no longer exists reads as none
  servings: number;                            // whole portions 1 … 100; a finished container keeps the portions it ended with
  status: ContainerStatus;                     // 'IN_STOCK' | 'DONE'; a new container is always IN_STOCK
  place: ContainerPlace;                       // 'FRIDGE' | 'FREEZER'
  eatBy: number;                               // a date-only value: UTC midnight of the last day to eat it, between 2000-01-01 and 2100-01-01
  history: ContainerEvent[];                   // 1 … 10 events, oldest first; the first is MADE (from a cook) or where it first went
  createdAt: number;                           // immutable
  updatedAt: number;
}
```

A saved meal's container counts plates, shown as "servings" like any other, and a planned meal eats one plate of it. `title` is a snapshot, cut to 120 characters when the container is saved (a recipe's title can be 200, a container's is capped at 120 by the rules): the app shows the recipe's or meal's current name while it exists (`getContainerTitle`), and the stored title once it doesn't. The collection is new, so the rules require all twelve keys (no older shape exists); a key added later is read with `get(key, default)` and left out of the required set. `eatBy` is written with `fromDateInputValue` from the date input (or computed as the viewer's local today plus a number of days, `getEatByDefault`, which is again a UTC-midnight day), and read back with `toDateInputValue`; it is never displayed with a local-time formatter. The first event always carries the portions (`getStartServings`), so the Finished view and a cook's "Put away 4 servings" read them without a field of their own. A deleted recipe leaves its containers (they keep their title and still read as stock); a deleted cook leaves its container with a `cookId` that reads as none.

### 7. `Meal` (saved) — `…/meals/{id}`

```typescript
interface MealItem {
  id: string;                                  // row id
  kind: ItemKind;
  refId: string;                               // a recipe id or an ingredient id
  servings: number;                            // > 0, halves allowed
}

interface Meal {
  id: string;
  name: string;
  items: MealItem[];                           // 1 … MAX_MEAL_ITEMS (no isArchived: a saved meal is always deletable)
  isFavorite: boolean;                         // the star; a missing key (saved meals written before favorites) reads as false, and only the star writes it
  createdAt: number;
  updatedAt: number;
}
```

### 8. `PlannedMeal` — `…/plannedMeals/{id}`

```typescript
interface PlannedSnapshot {
  frozenAt: number;                            // when the day was first fixed; kept when the day is priced again
  repricedAt: number | null;                   // null when the app froze the day; an instant once the person had it priced again. Snapshots written before this key lack it: readers use `getRepricedAt`, the rules accept it missing
  costCents: number | null;                    // null when nothing could be priced
  leftOut: { kind: GapKind; ingredientId: string }[];  // the ingredients the cost left out, so a day or month can still name (and de-duplicate) them
  nutrition: NutritionValues;                  // totals; a null metric = unknown
  lines: { itemId: string; costCents: number | null }[];
}

interface PlannedMeal {
  id: string;
  date: number;                                // UTC midnight of the planned day (date-only)
  mealType: MealType;
  name: string | null;                         // the saved meal's name when it came from one
  sourceMealId: string | null;                 // set when copied from a saved meal; never read for display
  items: MealItem[];                           // this day's own copy
  refIds: string[];                            // every recipe and ingredient id in items, for the "in use" query
  snapshot: PlannedSnapshot | null;            // set after the day has passed; replaced only by the explicit re-price (decision 22)
  createdAt: number;
  updatedAt: number;
}
```

`refIds` is derived from `items` on every write (`Array.from(new Set(items.map(i => i.refId)))`). Several planned meals can share a day and a type (two snacks); the calendar orders them by `createdAt`.

### Catalog (constants, not documents)

```typescript
interface CatalogIngredient {
  id: string;                                  // 'cat-chicken-breast'
  name: string;
  familyId: string | null;                     // 'cat-fam-chicken'
  aliases: string[];                           // search + AI matching ("capsicum" for bell pepper)
  per100g: { calories: number; protein: number; carbs: number; fat: number };
  serving: { amount: number; unit: Unit };     // a sensible "1 serving"
  gramsPerMl: number | null;
  gramsEach: number | null;
}
interface CatalogFamily { id: string; name: string }
interface CatalogStore { id: string; name: string; domain: string }   // 'walmart.com'
```

Files: `catalog/catalogIngredients.ts`, `catalog/catalogFamilies.ts`, `catalog/catalogStores.ts`, `catalog/index.ts` (lookup maps built once at module load). Values are generated by an author-time script (`scripts/catalog/buildCatalog.ts`) from USDA FoodData Central and checked in; the app never calls USDA at runtime. A search index (lowercased name + aliases) is built once per module. The catalog is a separate lazy chunk loaded with the Pinch route, so it never reaches other mini-apps.

### Derived values (never stored, except a past meal's snapshot)

| Value | Computed from | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Resolved ingredient | catalog entry + `IngredientRecord` | `selectIngredientsById` |
| Quantity in base units | `amount × UNIT_TO_BASE[unit]`, in the unit's dimension | `utils/quantity.ts` |
| Bridged quantity | base units converted to another dimension via `gramsPerMl` (mass ↔ volume) or `gramsEach` (mass ↔ count); `null` when no bridge | `utils/quantity.ts` |
| Ingredient unit cost | default (else first) product: `priceCents ÷ sizeInBase`, per base unit of the product's dimension | `utils/unitCost.ts` |
| Unit price and the price echo (display only) | `formatUnitPrice(product, sameAs)`: a package's price per lb, kg, oz, l, fl oz (spoons and cups), each, or per 100 g / 100 ml, in the unit of the ingredient's default price when it measures the same way, null for a package of exactly one of that unit; `formatRecipeUnitEcho(product, unit, conversions)`: a typed price read back per one of the unit the recipes and plan use most (`getIngredientUses`, the heaviest line; per 100 for g and ml) through `getUnitCost` and `bridge`, the cost math's own path, null when the units can't be bridged | `utils/productFormat.ts` |
| Line cost | recipe line amount bridged into the product's dimension × unit cost; otherwise a `NOT_COMPARABLE` gap; no product → `NO_PRODUCT` | `utils/recipeMath.ts` |
| Recipe cost | sum of the priced lines; `{ totalCents, perServingCents, leftOut: Gap[], lines }` (`lines`: each recipe line's own cost or gap, for the drawer); `null` totals when nothing could be priced | `utils/recipeMath.ts` |
| Recipe nutrition per serving | per metric: override if non-null, else sum of line grams × `per100g ÷ 100` ÷ servings; a line with no per-100 g value or no gram bridge is left out of that metric (never counted as 0) and adds a `NO_NUTRITION` gap for it | `utils/nutritionMath.ts` |
| Ingredient item per serving | serving size (record, else catalog, else 100 g) through the same bridge: cost and nutrition of one serving | `utils/mealMath.ts`, `utils/nutritionMath.ts` |
| Grams in one serving | the serving bridged to mass (density or weight each); `null` when it can't be weighed or isn't positive | `getServingGrams` in `utils/nutritionBasis.ts` |
| Nutrition typed for an amount | `toPer100g(typed, grams)` = typed × 100 ÷ grams, two decimals, unchanged at exactly 100 g; grams from `getEntryGrams(amount, g \| oz \| serving, servingGrams)`; the result is what `nutritionPer100g` stores | `utils/nutritionBasis.ts` |
| Ingredient nutrition per serving (display only) | `scaleNutrition(per100g, servingGrams ÷ 100)`; a metric that is unknown stays unknown | `utils/nutritionBasis.ts` |
| Meal nutrition | sum of the items' nutrition (`getMealNutrition`); a metric is known when some item knows it, and the ingredients left out are reported | `utils/nutritionMath.ts` |
| Item cost | `servings ×` (recipe per-serving cost or ingredient per-serving cost) | `utils/mealMath.ts` |
| Planned meal totals | sum of items; for a frozen meal, the snapshot instead | `utils/mealMath.ts` |
| Would today's prices change a frozen meal | the meal's items priced from the current data, compared with its snapshot as shown (cents rounded, each line, what it leaves out) | `wouldRepriceChange` in `utils/mealMath.ts` |
| Day totals | planned meals for a day key, inside the period pass | `selectPeriodStats(...).byDay` |
| Period stats | `{ spendCents, mealsPriced, fullyPricedMeals, avgPerFullyPricedMealCents, dailyNutrition, proteinCentsPerGram, proteinPricedMeals, nutritionMissing, leftOut }` over the shown month or week (`getPeriodStats(range)`); `byDay` and `byType` each carry their `nutrition` | `selectPeriodStats(startKey, endKey)` |
| Gaps | the union of left-out ingredients for a scope, minus dismissed ones | `utils/gaps.ts` |
| Day key | `toDateInputValue(date)` for a stored date; `toLocalDateInputValue(cell.getTime())` for a Dreamer UI cell | `utils/dayKeys.ts` |
| A recipe's notes | the loaded notes grouped by `recipeId`, newest first (`createdAt`, then id) | `selectNotesByRecipe` (`groupNotesByRecipe`) |
| Notes per recipe, last tweak | `{ count, lastTweakAt }` per recipe in one pass, `lastTweakAt` the latest TWEAK's `createdAt` or `null` | `selectNoteSummaries` (`getNoteSummaries`) |
| "Written before your Oct 20 tweak" | a NOTE whose `createdAt` is before the recipe's `lastTweakAt` | `getWrittenBefore` in `utils/notes.ts` |
| Whether an edit is recorded | `'on'` when the cooks and notes listeners have landed without error, the recipe has a cook and fewer than 300 notes; `'full'` at 300; else `'off'` | `selectTweakTracking`, `getTweakTracking` |
| A tweak's lines | `getRecipeChanges(before, after, ingredientsById, sourcesById?)`: plain-word lines in the form's order, at most 8; a recipe's care is not compared (§5f) | `utils/recipeChanges.ts` |
| Days as words | `formatCareDays(days)`: months when the days are a multiple of 30 ("3 months"), else weeks when a multiple of 7 ("2 weeks"), else days ("1 day", "5 days") | `utils/recipeCare.ts` |
| The form row's summary | `getCareSummary(typed)`: "Fridge 3 days, freezer 3 months, reheating, tip" (commas, so a wrap never leaves a dot at the end of a line; days that cannot be used show as typed, next to the red problem line); empty when nothing is typed | `utils/recipeCare.ts` |
| Cooking mode's put-away pills | `CookPutAway` reads `care.fridgeDays` / `freezerDays` through `formatCareDays`: "Fridge · keeps 3 days", "Freezer · keeps 3 months"; just the place when a time isn't set (`getLeftoversLine` is no longer used by cooking mode) | `components/cooking/CookPutAway.tsx`, `utils/recipeCare.ts` |

*Daily nutrition* is the period's total of a metric divided by the number of days that know it (for a single day, that day's own total), so a day with nothing known doesn't pull it toward zero. *Average per fully priced meal* divides the cost of the planned meals that have a price and nothing left out (no gap, and every item has a cost) by their count (`fullyPricedMeals`, `avgPerFullyPricedMealCents`): a meal with even one ingredient unpriced is a floor, and averaging floors read as a real price. *Price per gram of protein* divides what those same meals cost by their protein grams, over the ones that know their protein (`proteinPricedMeals`). Neither can say "so far" or "+", because nothing in their base is left out; each is not shown under three such meals (`MIN_FULLY_PRICED_MEALS`), and both name their base in the label. `mealsPriced` (meals with at least one priced item) now only decides whether the Total has a figure.

---

## State Machines & Logic

### 1. Quantity and cost

```
toBase(amount, unit) = { dimension: UNIT_DIMENSION[unit], value: amount × UNIT_TO_BASE[unit] }

bridge(q, targetDimension, ingredient):
  same dimension                → q
  MASS   → VOLUME (needs gramsPerMl)  value ÷ gramsPerMl
  VOLUME → MASS   (needs gramsPerMl)  value × gramsPerMl
  MASS   → COUNT  (needs gramsEach)   value ÷ gramsEach
  COUNT  → MASS   (needs gramsEach)   value × gramsEach
  VOLUME ↔ COUNT                      via MASS (needs both)
  otherwise                     → null   (a NOT_COMPARABLE gap)

lineCost(line, ingredient, product):
  no product                    → gap NO_PRODUCT
  q = bridge(toBase(line.amount, line.unit), productDimension, ingredient)
  q is null                     → gap NOT_COMPARABLE
  cost = q.value × (product.priceCents ÷ toBase(product.sizeAmount, product.sizeUnit).value)
```

Cents are rounded once, at the display edge, never per line, so a recipe's total doesn't drift from the sum of its rounded lines. A product's size of `EACH` ("12 count") compares directly with a recipe line in `EACH`, and with mass or volume only through `gramsEach`.

Worked example: chicken breast, Walmart, $9.48 (948¢) for 3 lb → 3 × 453.592 = 1360.776 g → 0.6967¢/g. A recipe line of 2 lb = 907.18 g → 632¢ ($6.32). A line of `1 cup` chicken breast has no `gramsPerMl` → `NOT_COMPARABLE`, left out and named.

### 2. Nutrition

Per metric, independently. Ingredient values are per 100 g, so a line's grams (bridged as above) give `grams ÷ 100 × per100g[metric]`. A recipe's per-serving value is the sum over lines ÷ `servings`, unless `nutritionOverride[metric]` is non-null. An ingredient with `null` for a metric, or a line that can't be bridged to grams, adds a `NO_NUTRITION` gap for that metric only. Nutrition is computed for all four metrics whether or not they're switched on; the switches only choose what is rendered and which gaps are surfaced (`gaps.ts` takes the `showNutrition` switches). In the recipe form `NutritionReview` shows the calculated per-serving values as `MacroStats` tiles (with a warning tip when some ingredients have no nutrition). Edit turns the four numbers into inputs: in place with Done from `sm` up, and on a phone in a `DetailSheet` drawer ("Nutrition per serving", four compact `Field`s, Done). A changed number is saved as that metric's `nutritionOverride` while a number equal to the calculated one stays derived (`null`), and "Reset to what we calculated" clears the overrides (in the drawer it refills the fields and leaves the drawer open). `MacroStats` and `src/utils/macroUtils.ts` are where calories (whole numbers) and macros (grams) are formatted.

**Typing an ingredient's nutrition on the label's basis.** `IngredientFormSheet` asks "Per [amount] [g | oz | serving]" above the four numbers (starting at 100 g), so what is typed is what the label prints, and `getPer100g` turns it into the stored per-100 g values (`toPer100g` over `getEntryGrams`, see Derived values; a blank metric stays `null`). The serving option needs the form's own serving size to convert to grams through `getServingGrams` (the ingredient's density or weight each): until it does the pill is disabled and a hint says so, with an "Add serving size" link when the size is off. A blank or non-positive amount, or a converted value outside 0 … `MAX_NUTRITION_VALUE`, shows one line and keeps Save disabled; any basis other than exactly 100 g shows "Saved as per 100 g: …" with the converted figures. An existing ingredient reopens in per 100 g, since that is what is stored. `NutritionEntry.isTyped` stays false until a number field is edited: while it is, picking g, oz or serving converts the numbers on screen to the new unit from the per-100 g value (`switchBasis`, per 100 g × grams ÷ 100, two decimals), so what is stored doesn't move; once a number has been typed, picking another unit only relabels the typed numbers and "Saved as per 100 g" shows what that makes. Display never changes a number elsewhere: the ingredient drawer and an ingredient row both read the Kitchen list's View options basis (`useListViewOptions(uid, 'ingredients').basis`, default per 100 g), and the drawer's thin Per serving / Per 100 g `PillGroup` writes that same setting rather than keeping its own. The pills are left out and the drawer shows per 100 g when one serving is exactly 100 g or can't be weighed, and a row falls back to per 100 g when the serving can't be weighed; `scaleNutrition` does the per-serving numbers. Recipes, meals, gaps and stats always compute from per 100 g. `npm run check:pinch-math` covers the conversions.

### 3. Copy-on-plan, edit semantics and the past

| Action | Result |
|---|---|
| Plan a saved meal on a day | one `plannedMeals` document: items copied with new row ids, `sourceMealId` set, `name` set |
| Plan a single recipe or ingredient | the same document with one item, `sourceMealId: null` |
| Edit a saved meal | the `meals` document only; planned days unchanged |
| Edit a planned meal | the planned document only (items, servings, date, type) |
| "Save to Saved meals" on a planned meal | a new `meals` document from its items; the planned meal's `sourceMealId` is left as it was |
| Edit a recipe | `recipes` document only; every non-frozen planned meal that points at it reads the new values |
| Edit a price, serving size, nutrition or conversion | first `freezePastMeals`, then the write |
| Day passes | its planned meals are frozen the next time the app loads that month (below) |
| "Price this day with today's prices" | every frozen meal of that day gets a fresh snapshot from the current prices and recipes, in one transaction (below) |

**A planned meal's name and the plan builder's picks.** `getPlannedMealTitle` names a planned meal everywhere it is titled (the day drawer's rows, the planned meal's header, the month peek, the calendar, the Shopping "for" line; a plate's cooking title keeps the plan's own name and otherwise names the session's dishes through `getPlateTitle`, see cooking mode); a plate with no name of its own is named by its items through `getPlateTitle` ("Adobo chicken & Cilantro lime rice", three in a list, past three two and "and 2 more", the first and "& N more" once a name runs past 30 characters, "N more" joined with no-break spaces so a heading never breaks inside it), and its `namedCount` tells the day drawer's and the month peek's muted line which items are still to say ("With …" lists only those). `updatePlannedMeal` writes only date, type, items and `refIds`, so a Modify that changes the items keeps `name`: a tweaked Taco night is still Taco night. "Save to Saved meals" (a row in the planned meal's ⋯ More) writes a new `meals` document and leaves the planned meal's `name` and `sourceMealId` as they were, so its row hides by comparing plates (kind, ref and servings), not by a link. The plan builder's recent picks are read, not stored: `rankRecentPicks` (`utils/planPicks.ts`) orders recipes and saved meals by the latest of their `createdAt`, the `createdAt` of a loaded planned meal that holds them (a recipe item, or `sourceMealId` for a saved meal) and a recipe's last cook (`selectCookSummaries`). Planned meals come only from the listener's window (shown month ± 2), so a recipe planned only long ago ranks by its cooks and creation. Two records that share an id are ranked once, so the list's keys (`RECIPE:<id>`) never repeat. The builder offers `getOfferablePicks` of them (a recipe already on the plate, and a saved meal whose items all are, are left out), at most 12, as `RecentPickRows`: six rows, then "Show N more" and "Show fewer" only when folding would hide more than three (`getRecentRowWindow(total, isExpanded, openingTotal)`), kept after the first pick and shown while the search is empty. `MealBuilder` owns the fold (`isRecentExpanded`, so typing and clearing the search doesn't fold it again) and the row count when the screen opened (`openingTotal`): a fold exists only if it opened with more than nine and stays until six or fewer remain, so a pick never makes hidden rows appear at once, and a list that opened unfolded never grows one. `ItemSearch` keeps the typed text itself and tells `MealBuilder` only when the field goes from empty to typed or back (`onTypingChange`), which is what hides the recents; a keystroke then renders the search and its results, not the plate, the Cost tile and the recents (Taylor at 4× CPU with four dishes on the plate: the median keystroke long task went from 149 ms to none, the one that starts typing stays about 200 ms). Each row reads the Recipes list's View options (`useListViewOptions(uid, 'recipes')`: prices and the stock line) and the same selectors the Recipes rows do (`selectRecipeCosts`, `selectStockByRecipe`, `selectStockByMealWithRecipes`, `getMealCost`). Leaving asks only when something changed: `getBuilderSnapshot` (the plate's rows by kind, ref and servings, the trimmed name and, for a planned meal being changed, its day and meal) is taken when the builder opens and compared with the live one, so a new empty plate and an edit left as it opened close at once. `MealBuilder` hands one `goBack` to its `Subview` as `onBack` and to Cancel, so the header, Cancel, Escape and the gesture share one confirm through `useActionModal` ("Keep editing" is the default, "Discard" destructive); `isAskingRef` ignores a second press while it is open, a write already in flight leaves without asking, and the close is `closeSubviewIf` for the subview it opened as. The search takes focus on open (a new plan with nothing on the plate) only when `(hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)` matches, as the readers do, so a touch screen's keyboard never covers the recents and the footer; under `(max-height: 449px)` the footer's note is dropped and the disabled primary carries it. The builder's When row is `getWhenSummary` over a `SummaryEditor` with `isFramed`, and the footer's reason is `getBuilderNote` (what is missing for the primary: a day, a name, a plate). `getDefaultMealType` and `getNearDayOptions` in the same file give the starting meal and the day strip. Pure, checked in `check:pinch-math`.

`freezePastMeals` runs in two places: `useFreezePastDays` (from `Pinch.tsx`) once the kitchen has loaded and again for any unfrozen past meals a newly loaded month brings (each meal is submitted once per visit, so the list shrinking as writes land never starts another freeze of the same ones; a failed attempt isn't retried until the next visit or the next edit's freeze), and as the first step of every action that changes a value the math reads (`saveProduct`, `setDefaultProduct`, `removeProduct`, `saveRecipe`, `removeRecipe`, `saveIngredient`, `removeIngredient`, through `freezeLoadedPastMeals`). It never runs while a listener has failed, since the numbers it would freeze come from incomplete data. "Past" is the viewer's local day (`getLocalTodayDate`), the same clock the calendar shows as today, so an evening in a time zone behind UTC doesn't freeze the day still being planned. It finds loaded planned meals with `snapshot === null` and `date` before that, computes each snapshot from the current data, and writes `snapshot` field-scoped (no reads), in one `writeBatch` per 100 meals so each batch is one snapshot event for the listeners. A batch is all-or-nothing, so a refused one (a meal another device froze first, or deleted) falls back to one `updateDoc` per meal, settled independently: the refused meal is denied alone and doesn't stop the rest. The rules let the app's own freeze write `snapshot` only from `null` (a snapshot written over an existing one without an instant `repricedAt` is refused), so a stale device can't overwrite history; only the re-price below can. A freeze that fails never blocks the edit that triggered it (`freezeBeforeEdit` swallows it): a person's own price or recipe change isn't held up by bookkeeping, and the cost is only that those meals stay open until the next freeze.

A frozen meal still lists its items from the live recipes and ingredients (so a deleted-then-archived recipe still shows its title), but its totals come from the snapshot. A meal planned on a day that has already passed is frozen at once (`planMeal` writes the snapshot with the meal, unless a listener has failed, in which case it is left open and the load-time freeze takes it). A frozen meal can't be edited: `updatePlannedMeal` is a transaction that refuses it (a snapshot would no longer match its items), the planned meal view hides Modify, and the way to change what it contains is to remove it and add it again; the way to change only its prices is to price the day again. Frozen gaps still count in what a total says it leaves out but are not offered as nudges, since a new price doesn't change a fixed day by itself (`PeriodStats.openLeftOut`); once a price would change it, the drawer offers to price the day again instead. The copy for a frozen day says the same thing: its warning says a price added now counts from today on, its cost tile carries the line "Fixed when the day passed" (or "Priced with today's prices on Oct 14" after a re-price) and reads "Not priced" when nothing could be priced, a row with no price is a dash, and a fixed day's partial total reads "$0.15+" like its rows and never says "so far" (`formatCostTile`), since it can't be priced by itself (an open day says "No price yet" and "$18.40 so far", which invite a fix).

**Pricing a day again (`repriceDay`).** `RepriceDayRow` is the only caller, in the day drawer, and renders only when the day has passed, the kitchen has loaded without a failure (`hasCompleteCostData`) and `wouldRepriceChange` is true for at least one meal of that day, computed in a `useMemo` over that day's meals and the cost context, so opening a drawer costs one `getMealCost` per meal on that day and nothing else is walked (the 1,100-meal fixture included). The confirm shows the result before anything is written: at the tap, `getRepricePreview(meals, costContext)` (`utils/mealMath.ts`, covered by `npm run check:pinch-math`) runs `buildRepricedSnapshot` over the day's frozen meals from the loaded store and adds up the totals, and `getRepriceLines` (`utils/mealFormat.ts`) words them: "Before" in the cost tile's own words ("Not priced", otherwise the total), "After" as "about $0.66" (about, because the transaction reads the meals again), and "N ingredients still have no price" only above zero. It runs on the tap, not on render. The thunk checks the same preconditions again, takes the day's frozen meals from `selectPlannedByDay`, and runs one `runTransaction`: it re-reads every one of them with `transaction.get`, stops with one sentence and nothing written if any is gone, no longer frozen or no longer on that day, writes nothing when today's prices change none of them, and otherwise writes every meal of the day (a day is never half re-priced, so its label is the same for all its meals): `transaction.update(ref, { snapshot, updatedAt })` where `snapshot = buildRepricedSnapshot(...)`, which is `buildSnapshot` over the meal's own items and the current cost context (the same function the freeze uses, so the numbers equal what a freeze run today would write, nutrition included) with `frozenAt` kept and `repricedAt` set.
