The prices and recipes come from the loaded store (as for the freeze), the meals from the transaction's own reads. A day is limited to `MAX_REPRICE_MEALS` (400) meals so it stays under Firestore's 500 writes in one transaction; the limit is the action's, not the rules'. A transaction needs a connection, so a write that can't reach the server fails with "Check your connection and try again." (any other Firebase error is logged and shown as one plain sentence). Nothing else is written: not the meals' ids, dates, items, `refIds`, `name` or `sourceMealId`, not their cooks, the shopping list or any note. `useFreezePastDays` and `freezeBeforeEdit` select `snapshot === null`, so a re-priced day (non-null) is never touched by them; the person's own later price edits don't move it either, and they can ask again, which writes nothing and hides the row once today's prices change nothing. The copy never claims more than is known: a snapshot that has `repricedAt` reads "Priced with today's prices on {date of the latest}" (an instant, in local time), one that has none reads "Fixed when the day passed" (a day where only some meals were re-priced says "Some meals priced with today's prices on …"), the tile with no price reads "Not priced" (before and after; its detail line says which), a line with no price is a dash, and nothing says "as they were that day".

*Honest limit:* a meal is frozen from the data on the first load after its day, not the data on the day itself. A price changed during a gap in use, in a month the window never loaded, can leak into history for meals that were never frozen. The freeze-before-edit step keeps this to meals older than the loaded window.

### 3a. Archive on delete

```
removeRecipe(id):
  usedInMeals    = meals.some(m => m.items.some(i => i.kind === 'RECIPE' && i.refId === id))
  usedInPlanned  = (await getCountFromServer(query(plannedMeals, where('refIds', 'array-contains', id)))).data().count > 0
  usedInPlanned || usedInMeals  → updateDoc({ isArchived: true, updatedAt })   // hidden from lists and pickers
  otherwise                     → deleteDoc  (+ delete the cover from Storage)
```

The same shape serves custom ingredients (also checked against `recipes.ingredients[].ingredientId`, saved meals and `products`) and stores (against products). Products are deleted outright (the ingredient's `defaultProductId` is re-pointed to another product, or `null`, in the same transaction), and the MVP has no way to delete a family. The confirm names what will happen ("…is used in 3 planned meals and 1 saved meal, so it will be hidden, not erased"), from `getUsage` (the loaded collections plus one server-side count of the planned meals that list the id, so offline it says it can't check instead of undercounting); the remove action asks again itself, so the confirm text can never disagree with what is done to a record that changed in between. The read is a query, so it can't sit inside the delete's transaction: a planned meal added in the instant between the read and the delete is the one race left, and it would show the removed record as "Removed recipe" until the person re-adds it.

Archived items never appear in pickers, search or lists (`selectRecipes`, `selectIngredients`, `selectStores` are the visible lists; the `selectAll…` ones and every `…ById` map still hold them, so `Retired chili` keeps its title and cost on the day that used it). A catalog store the person archived shows as addable again in the store pickers. Selectors that resolve a reference (`resolveIngredient`, recipe titles for planned items) still find them.

### 4. Gaps and nudges

`getGaps(scope)` walks the lines in a scope (one recipe, one meal, a day, the month) and returns `{ kind, ingredientId, detail }[]`:

- `NO_PRODUCT` — a priced ingredient with no product, or a recipe/ingredient item with no price at all
- `NOT_COMPARABLE` — a product exists but the amount and the package size can't be bridged
- `NO_NUTRITION` — only for a metric in `showNutrition`

A gap is **hidden** when `dismissedNudges["{kind}:{ingredientId}"]` exists: the record is still read, so a line closed before stays quiet, but nothing writes new keys any more (`dismissNudge` has no caller; `saveProduct` still clears the keys for an ingredient it prices). "Not now" on a `GapNudge` quiets that line until reload, kept in a module-level set keyed by uid and the sorted gap keys, so a different set of gaps speaks again. The line names the gap that weighs most: `utils/gapOrder.ts` builds one index of where each ingredient is used (`getIngredientUses`: the listed recipes' lines and the unfrozen planned meals' lines through `getItemsLines`, each converted to rough grams, volume at 1 g/ml without a density and a piece at 50 g without a weight; memoized with `weakMapMemoize` on its four store inputs, so the drawer, the Calendar and the sheet share it) and `rankGaps` orders the gaps by it. A nudge renders only when the filtered gap list is non-empty, and only where the gap is owned: the recipe drawer and the calendar's summary sheet (the saved-meal drawer, the day drawer and the planned meal view keep their cost tile, the day's called Total, and its warning and render no nudge). A total that excludes lines always says so, whether or not its nudge is quieted, in the figure ("so far" on a tile, "+" on a per-serving cost) and through `LeftOutTip` (two sentences: no price yet vs can't be compared, and where to add them). `NudgeCountNote` and its "asks about fewer" line are gone.

The fill-in sheet takes the gap list and renders one row per ingredient with only the fields that gap needs: a product's store, price and size for `NO_PRODUCT`; the size for `NOT_COMPARABLE`; the metric for `NO_NUTRITION`. Saving writes through the same actions as the full forms. A row left blank stays unchanged; a row that is part-filled is not saved and says what it still needs, so Save never writes half a product. A saved row asks to become the ingredient's default product, and a `NOT_COMPARABLE` row rewrites the size of the default product it is about. The rows are ranked once when the sheet opens (`rankGaps` over the shared index, so saving a row never reorders the rest), each says `getUseText` ("Used in Adobo chicken and 1 more · 2.5 lb planned": the plan's need summed in the unit of its biggest line when the units convert, else the one recipe's amount), a row's store starts on the store last picked in this sheet, and its size unit on `getPackageUnit` (mass units as the recipe has them, any volume as fl oz unless ml or l, each for a count; the ingredient's serving unit when nothing uses it). The last row's Save or Skip closes the sheet onto the screen behind; returning to the drawer it came from would need the screen to remember its opener (not built). Kitchen's "Needs a price" filter hands what it shows to the sheet as `NO_PRODUCT` gaps through its "Add prices" button.

### 5. Recipe ingestion (AI)

```
idle → reading(source) → draft(recipe, unread[]) → (person edits) → saved
             │                                           
             └→ failed(message)    (always ends on idle for the next try; nothing was written)
```

- Entry point: a new recipe lists its ways to start through `RecipeStart`, rows only from the shared `MethodPicker` (required `onPick`, one `Method` per `RECIPE_START_METHODS` entry): **From text** (up to `MAX_PASTE_CHARS`), **From a file** (a photo, screenshot or PDF up to `MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES`; JPG, PNG, WebP, HEIC, PDF through `getUploadMimeType`; images through `compressIngestionImage`; what the picker and the drop accept is `isReadableFile`), **From a link**, **Write it yourself** (dashed, quieter) and, last and also dashed, `KEEP_AS_IDEA_METHOD` ("Keep it as an idea", which calls `onKeepAsIdea`; it is a row of the same `MethodPicker`, not a `RECIPE_START_METHODS` entry, because it makes no recipe). On a phone "New" in Recipes and Calendar's "Add your first recipe" raise `RecipeStartDrawer` (a `DetailSheet`), which `Pinch.tsx` renders once at its root from `isStartOpen` in `PinchOverlayContext` (`setIsStartOpen`), so any screen can raise it; picking a way closes the drawer and opens `RecipeSubview` already on that way (`{ kind: 'recipe', id: null, method, fromDrawer: true }`; `fromCalendar` marks one started from the calendar's empty state, carried through the drawer by a ref in `Pinch.tsx` and read by the form's save for its "Added … to your recipes" toast). From `sm` up they open the subview with no `method`, whose first stage is the same list. Editing an existing recipe goes straight to the form.
- `RecipeSubview` stages are `start | text | link | file | idea | form`. Picking text, link or file shows `RecipeReader` for that way (`components/recipes/RecipeReader.tsx` holds the three views), titled with the way's name; "Write it yourself" (on the list, and as the dashed `MethodPicker` row each reader ends with) goes to the form (asking first when a link or text is typed), and a finished reading goes to the form with its result. `RecipeSubview` passes `Subview` an `onBack` (`goBack`), which the header's back button, Escape and the browser's or phone's back gesture all call. On a reading screen every way out of typed work goes through `confirmDiscardTyped(action)`, which asks `confirm` "Discard this link?" / "Discard this text?" (Keep editing / Discard, destructive; the message names the action: "If you go back now…", "…start a blank recipe now…", "…paste its text instead…") while `isTypedRef` says the reader holds a pasted link or text (the reader reports it through `onTypedChange`; an address that came with an idea doesn't count), and returns whether to go on: `stepBackFromReader` (the header, Back, Escape, the gesture), `startBlank` (the dashed "Write it yourself" row) and `switchToText` ("Paste its text instead", which also clears `kept`). Typed words are never carried into the blank form or the text screen: a link has nothing to carry, a pasted recipe is far too long for a description, and "Save as an idea" already keeps it. `askToDiscard` holds an `isAskingRef`, so a second route used while a question is open does nothing and one question is ever open (the dialog takes the first Escape and the first back gesture, the subview the next). After a Keep editing focus returns to the control the question came from. Back then raises the phone's start drawer again (`closeSubview()`, then `setIsStartOpen(true)`) when the way came from it (`fromDrawer`), closes when it came from an idea, and returns to the start stage otherwise; on the start page and the form it calls `leave`. `leave` closes the subview, first asking `useActionModal().confirm` "Discard this recipe?" (Keep editing / Discard, destructive) while `isDirtyRef` is set. `RecipeForm` sets that ref when its data differs from the initial data, when a cover is chosen or removed, and when it started from a reading; `RecipeSubview` also sets it as a reading lands. Cancel goes through the same `leave` (`onLeave`); saving and deleting close with `closeSubviewIf` and an identity check: the subview showing when the person tapped must still be the one showing, so a save that lands late never closes a screen it didn't start (a slow save, then back and Discard, then Cooking mode: the form is gone and the cooking screen stays).
- Each reader opens with an `IntroCard` (`@/components/IntroCard`), on screen before anything is sent (text, link and file alike): each leads with one sentence of what the AI does ("An AI reads the page / your text / the file and fills in the form.") and adds "You look it over before anything is saved." A reading opened from an idea says "From your idea: {title}" in the card's lead (`ideaTitle`) and that the link is opened by the function, never the person's device. Each fact is said once: the card says what the AI does, the field's own hint how to get the input ("Copy the address from your browser." for a link), the footer note why the primary is off; the words between the card and the primary at 390×844 (card, hint, dashed row) are 32 for link, 30 for text and 44 for file. The input is one framed card (`FormSection` caption, the field with no border of its own, a hairline, one line that is the hint, the reading state or the failure; a failure never clears what was typed, and typing clears it), then the `ActionChips` row ("Save as an idea", or "Try this file again" after a failed file, with `RefreshCw`: `RotateCcw` is Reset on the other screens), then the quiet `MethodPicker` rows, "Paste its text instead" (only on the link way, only while the failure says the link or what was read is the trouble, and never on a reading opened from an idea) above `manual`, then `ModalFooterActions` as a direct child of the reader's root (`Subview`'s `form-footer` rules pin it): Back (`onBack`, the same `goBack` as the header), and the primary, "Fill in the recipe" (disabled and noted "Paste a link first" / "Paste the recipe first" while empty) or "Choose a file" ("Choose another file" after a failed reading). The file way keeps the picked `File` in state, shows it in `FilePickCard` (`@/components/FilePickCard`: the empty dashed slot is a button for the picker and a drop target whose `title` is its action ("Tap to choose a file", one `Upload` icon) over `accepts · limit`, the picked file shows `formatFileSize` from `@/utils/fileUtils`) and retries it from `handleFile`. `useIsMounted` drops a reading that finishes after the reader left, so it can't call `onRead` and move the person to the form. The field takes focus on mount only when `(hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)` matches. A picked and a dropped file go through the same `handleFile`: `isReadableFile` (`@/utils/fileUtils`: `application/pdf` or `image/*` by type, or one of png, jpg, jpeg, webp, heic, heif, gif, pdf by extension when the browser left the type empty), then `MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES`; each refusal has its own sentence ("That file isn't a photo or a PDF."), leaves the slot empty (`rejectFile` also remounts the hidden input so the same file can be picked again) and makes no request. A link without a scheme gets `https://`, and anything that isn't an http(s) address with a dotted host is refused before any call.
- A failed reading is classified by cause, not by where it was caught: `classifyReadFailure(error, navigator.onLine)` in `utils/readFailures.ts` (pure, checked in `check:pinch-ingest`) returns `unreachable` (offline whatever the error says; a callable that failed, timed out or was refused by the platform; an AI call that never got an answer), `busy` (a 429 or a 5xx from the AI, or the function out of quota), `unavailable` (the AI refused, 403 or 404), `link` (the function said the address is bad or the page isn't one, or its own "Couldn't load that link."), `unread` (anything else, such as an answer that isn't JSON) or `empty` (the reading found no ingredients). `getReadFailureLine(cause, source)` turns it into one sentence that blames only the cause, so a lost connection never says anything about the link, the text or the file, and no sentence names a door (the rows under it are the doors); `canPasteTextInstead(cause)` is true only for `link`, `unread` and `empty`, which is when the link reader draws "Paste its text instead". `functions/unavailable` is two things: the function's own message for a page it couldn't fetch (the link) and the platform's (the reader), told apart by that message; a network failure from the AI SDK is code `error` with a message ending "Failed to fetch" (only a non-OK response is `fetch-error`, with a status), so the message is read too. Every failure is still logged with `console.error` and never shown verbatim.

- `lib/extractRecipe.ts` exports `extractRecipeFromText(text)`, `extractRecipeFromFile(file)` and `extractRecipeFromPage(page)`. All call the shared `generativeModel` from `src/lib/firebase/ai.ts` with a response schema: `{ title, servings, prepMinutes, cookMinutes, description, ingredients: [{ name, amount, unit, note, group }], steps: [string], fridgeDays, freezerDays, reheat, tip, unread: [string] }`, where `unit` is an enum of the app's unit labels and the four storage fields are nullable (see §5f). The five ingredient keys are all required and each but `name` is nullable, so the model always answers the note and the group (left optional, it was dropping notes, "diced" included). The prompt tells the model to leave a line it can't measure with no amount and its words in the note ("to taste", "a handful", "as needed"), to split a line naming two ingredients ("salt and pepper to taste") into two with the same group, and to give each line the heading the recipe puts it under ("For the sauce:", "Marinade") word for word as its group, null under no heading, never inventing one; the same ingredient under two headings is two lines. `normalizeExtractedRecipe` takes the colon and any markdown off a heading (`asGroup`), drops the recipe's own "Ingredients" title, caps it at 40 characters, and a line split from "salt and pepper" keeps its source line's group. Files go as `inlineData` base64. Extraction calls are not cached and not Query-backed (non-idempotent), like the other extraction calls in the repo.

- **From a link** fetches the page first through `fetchPageContent` (shared and app-neutral: `src/lib/pageContent/*`; Pinch's `queries/recipePageQueries.ts` asks it for `text`, `meta` and `structuredData` filtered to `schemaTypes: ['Recipe']`, kept ten minutes and never persisted, so a retry of a failed reading doesn't refetch). The callable (`functions/src/linkMetadata/fetchPageContent.ts`) is signed-in only, takes http/https links only, uses the same private-network guard as `fetchLinkMetadata` (`safeFetch.ts`), returns only the kinds asked for and stores nothing. `extractRecipeFromPage` hands the page title and description, the Recipe data (when the page has any, marked as possibly incomplete) and the page text to the model, with a line telling it to ignore ads, comments and related links; the model writes the draft, so a page with partial, missing or odd data reads where a hand-written parser would drop it. The client maps a bad link, an unreadable page and a failed fetch to one warm sentence each.
- The model output is **only a draft for the form.** `matchIngredient(name)` (`utils/ingredientMatch.ts`) resolves each ingredient name to an ingredient id against the person's custom ingredients first, then catalog names and aliases (normalized: lowercase, singular, punctuation stripped). No match → the row is marked "Add as new ingredient" and, if kept, creates a custom ingredient with that name in the same save as the recipe (one `writeBatch`). The row's drawer offers up to three close names first (`findNearMatches` in `utils/catalogSearch.ts`: each word that names a thing, with filler and words like "shredded" dropped, finds the ingredients with a word of their name, then an alias, starting with it; the words take turns from the last), excluding archived ones and those already in the recipe.
- The prompt tells the model the pasted text is data, not instructions, and asks it to list anything it could not read in `unread`; the draft never writes anything itself, so injected text can at most fill form fields the person reviews.
- **The reading becomes the form's starting values.** Because the start list and the reading screens come before the form, `RecipeSubview` holds a finished reading (`{ recipe, source, label }`, the label being "your text", the file's name or the page's host) and hands it to `RecipeForm` as `initialRead`; `getDataFromExtracted` builds the form's initial data from it (a second line of an ingredient already a row under the same heading adds to it when the units agree, and stays its own line under another heading or in another unit; unmatched names become "Add as new ingredient" rows; the rows come out in group order). The form then says what it was filled from and lists `unread`, and nothing is saved until the person saves. `normalizeExtractedRecipe` also cuts every value to the limits the form and `firestore.rules` enforce (title, description, note, step lengths, servings, minutes, counts), so a long answer can't leave Add disabled or fail the write. It also reads an unmeasured line the way a cook means it when the model slips: "salt to taste" or "parmesan, for serving" moves the phrase to the note, "handful of fresh parsley" becomes parsley noted "a handful", and "salt and pepper" (no amount, two parts of one to four words, not the same word twice) is two lines, but only on a signal: a to-taste or for-garnish tail was found, or both halves are salt or pepper kinds (`SEASONING`), so "macaroni and cheese", "sweet and sour sauce" and "half and half" stay whole; a measured line is left as read and the split lines still stop at 60. `getDataFromExtracted` marks a line with no amount `isAmountless`, so it saves as it is.
- **The form's basics are summary rows.** `RecipeBasics.tsx` holds `RecipeTitleField`, `RecipeTimesField` and `RecipeDescriptionField`, each a `SummaryEditor` over the same form values as before (`title`, `basics.prep` / `basics.cook` as text, `description`), so dirty tracking, `onTitleChange` and the header's live name work through the editors unchanged; only Servings is still an inline `Stepper`. The title editor opens by itself once (`defaultOpen`) when `recipeId` is null and there is no `initialRead`. Validation is the same predicates as before (`title` required and at most 200 characters, `description` at most 2,000, `isValidMinutes` for prep and cook) and each shows on its row as well as in its editor; the pinned footer's note names the first thing to fix ("Give it a title to save.", "Shorten the title to save.", "Check the prep and cook time to save."). The two minutes fields are `type='number'` with a visible "min" suffix and an `aria-label` of "Prep time in minutes" / "Cook time in minutes"; no label carries "(minutes)".
- **Removals in the form are undoable.** `RecipeForm` owns one `useUndoRemoval` and passes `showUndo` to `RecipeIngredients` and `RecipeSteps` (`onRemoved`), so an ingredient and a step removed in a row can't show two bars at once; `MealBuilder` owns its own for the plate (and puts the saved meal's name back when Undo returns the last item). The `UndoSnackbar` is the form's first child, not its last, because `Subview overlay` stretches its last child to fill the screen. Dreamer UI's toast was not used: its action label is fixed when the toast is added, so it can't show the seconds left without removing and re-adding the toast every second.
- A reading that finds no ingredients at all is a failure ("We couldn't find a recipe in that"). Errors map to one warm sentence each (busy, offline, unavailable, unreadable) and the detail is logged, never shown.
- `source` is saved as `AI_TEXT` / `AI_FILE` / `AI_LINK` when the draft came from the reader, `MANUAL` otherwise (even if the person edited it).

### 5a. Recipe sources

- **Choosing one.** The recipe form's "Where it's from" row (after Steps; `RecipeSourceField`, a `SummaryEditor` whose editor holds the `PickOrCreate`; the row says the chosen source's name or the typed new one) is a `PickOrCreate` over the sources that at least one listed recipe uses (`selectSourceCounts.bySource`), plus the form's own starting choice, so a source left with no recipe stops being offered; a typed label still reuses any loaded source with that name, used or not (existing labels as pills, a dashed "New source" first, nothing preselected for a new recipe), with a text field in its place while there are no sources yet. The form keeps the answer as `{ choice, newText }`: a source id, `NEW_CHOICE` with the label typed, or empty. Saving resolves it in `RecipeForm`: a typed label that matches a loaded source (ignoring case and spaces) reuses its id, otherwise it becomes `draft.newSource` (a new id and the label) that `saveRecipe` writes in the same `writeBatch` as the recipe, like `newIngredients`, so a failed save leaves no orphan source and an offline save is queued whole; a picked id that no longer exists becomes `null`; the result is `draft.sourceId`. While a source is chosen the picker shows a visible "Remove source" chip (`PickOrCreate`'s optional `clearLabel`, an `ActionChips` chip with an ✕), besides tapping the chosen pill again.
- **Reading from a link.** A recipe read from a link (`source === 'AI_LINK'`) starts on its site: `getReadSourceChoice` takes the reading's label (the page's host), drops a leading `www.`, and picks the existing source whose label matches the host the same way (so "seriouseats.com" matches "www.seriouseats.com"), otherwise starts "New source" with the host as the text (cut to 80 characters). Only the form's starting value is set, so what the person picks or types afterwards is never replaced; the text field takes no focus on its own (`isFocusedOnOpen={false}`) so the keyboard doesn't open over the form. A recipe read from text or a file starts with none.
- **Writing.** `saveRecipe` writes `sourceId` on every save (new or edit), so an edit backfills the key on a recipe that lacked it and the field-scoped `updateDoc` never touches it elsewhere. `renameSource` is a field-scoped `updateDoc` of `label` and `updatedAt` after checking the loaded list for another source with that label. `removeSource` is one `writeBatch` per 400 writes holding the source's delete (in the first batch) and `sourceId: null` on every loaded recipe that names it, archived ones included; a recipe's `updatedAt` is left alone because it versions the cover image. A recipe created on another device at the same moment, or one that wasn't loaded yet, keeps a pointer to a source that no longer exists, which reads as none (above); if a later batch fails the source is already gone and the rest read the same way, and the error is shown.
- **Failure.** The sources listener is an optional enhancement: its error is logged (`console.error`), clears the loaded sources, never reaches the fatal load-error screen (`selectPinchLoadError` leaves it out), and its first answer or error ends the loading gate. While it has failed the form leaves the section out and keeps a recipe's own `sourceId` as it was, so editing never drops a label it couldn't read; the Source filter question and the header's Sources icon simply don't appear.
- **Reading and counting.** `selectSources` (alphabetical), `selectSourcesById` and `selectSourceCounts` (`{ bySource: Map<id, count>, withoutSource }` over the listed, non-archived recipes, one pass) feed the Recipes screen. The Source filter is a `FilterBar` with one `FilterSection` holding a pick-one `PillGroup`: "Any", each source used by at least one listed recipe as "Label · count", and "No source · n" only when some recipe has none; a picked source or "No source" stays in the list while picked even if its count falls to zero. The Source question exists while any source exists (`sources.length > 0`); its pills appear while at least one listed recipe has a source, otherwise one line says none does, and it has no link to the Sources screen (that is the header's icon, below). The picked value falls back to "Any" when its source is gone or the pills disappear, and saved meals are never filtered by source. The way into the Sources screen is one icon button in `RecipesScreen`'s `SectionHeader` action, beside the New button in the same `flex gap-1` group the Calendar header uses for its eye and Plan: a quiet `tertiary` round `size-10` button with the lucide `BookOpen` icon, `aria-label` and `title` "Sources", drawn while `hasSourceSection` holds (the Recipes view and `sources.length > 0`, the Source question's own condition), so a first run, Saved meals and Ideas have none; it calls `openSubview({ kind: 'sources' })`. The recipe drawer has no door to the Sources screen (the owner wants the way to the sources on the Recipes screen, not inside a recipe), but it names its recipe's source: `RecipeDrawer` reads `selectSourcesById` and `getRecipeSource` and draws a `LabelChip` first in its who-it-is group, above the description and facts line, nothing for a recipe with no source or a deleted one. It is a door only while the source holds other recipes: `selectSourceCounts.bySource` (the one definition the Source filter's pills and the Sources screen count with, the open recipe counting itself) says more than one, and the chip is then a button ("From {label}" and a chevron, `aria-label` "From {label}: show its recipes"); a source that holds only this recipe draws the same soft pill as a plain label (a `span`: no chevron, no button, no hit area), since its list would be the one recipe already open. `keepEnd` cuts a long name in the middle ("From The big spiral-bound … church fair") by splitting the last word or two off into a span that doesn't shrink while the head truncates. A tap calls the required `onShowSource(sourceId)`, which `RecipesScreen` answers by clearing `openId` (the drawer unmounts, and its history entry steps back), calling `showSource` (the same four setters `useOnSourceShown` runs: Recipes pill, search and In stock cleared, source picked, so it works from the Ideas pill's "Open the recipe" too) and `scrollToTopAfterSheetCloses()`: a sheet's history step is followed by the browser restoring the scroll the page had when it opened, which would leave the chip above the viewport on a long list, so the jump to the top waits for that `popstate` (and gives up after a second). The drawer is mounted only by `RecipesScreen`, so no other screen needs a door of its own; a host that mounts it elsewhere has to say what `onShowSource` does there. `SourcesSubview` lists `selectSources` (a search from `LIST_SEARCH_THRESHOLD`) with `selectSourceCounts`. Every row is one `Button` with a chevron that sets `editingId` and opens `SourceFormSheet` (a source on no recipe too; there is no pencil), and focus returns to the row when the sheet closes (`closeSheet` focuses the remembered opener on the next frame, unless the row is gone because the source was deleted). `SourceFormSheet` takes `onShowRecipes`: for a source with listed recipes its first row is a roomy `ActionRow` ("Show the N recipes from X", a 📖 chip, a chevron) that the subview answers with `announceSourceShown(id)` and then `closeSubview()`, which unmounts the sheet with it (its history layer closes with it, so the next back press leaves the Recipes tab as usual); a source on no recipe shows the line "Not on any recipe" there instead, and the one delete is the trash in the sheet's footer, whose confirm says "It isn't on any recipe" for such a source. The subview is rendered by `Pinch.tsx` outside `PinchSessionContext` and the Recipes tab stays mounted underneath it, so it cannot write `recipes:view`, `recipes:query`, `recipes:source` and `recipes:stock` itself (its own copy of the hook would not reach the mounted screen's state): `announceSourceShown` (`hooks/useSourceShown.ts`, a module-level set of listeners like `useRecipeAdded`) tells the mounted `RecipesScreen`, whose `useOnSourceShown` callback (`showSource`) sets the Recipes pill, clears the search and the In stock filter and picks that source, all before the subview unmounts, so the tab reappears already filtered with the chip "From X". The Sources subview no longer creates sources (they are made in the recipe form's batch).
- **Searching recipes.** `buildRecipeSearchIndex(recipes, ingredientsById, sourcesById)` (`utils/catalogSearch.ts`) normalizes each recipe's title and the rest of what a person would type (ingredient names and aliases, the source's label, the description) once; `RecipesScreen` builds it only while a search is typed (memoized on recipes, ingredients and sources) and `searchRecipes(index, query)` keeps a recipe when every word is in the title or starts a word of the rest, titles first, each group A to Z. The source and In stock filters apply to that result, so an empty filtered result can say how many recipes match outside it. With no recipe match the screen counts saved meals (`searchByName`) and ideas (`getIdeaSearchText`) and offers to show them; `IdeasView` takes `initialQuery` so the search follows. The row chunks are keyed on the filters, not the typed text, so a keystroke updates rows instead of remounting all of them (Taylor at 4× CPU: about 100–145 ms per keystroke once the result narrows). Checked in `scripts/checks/pinchMath.ts` (R2 block).
- **After a new recipe is saved.** `RecipeForm` toasts "Added X to your recipes" for every new recipe (from New, an idea or the Calendar) with a Plan it action that calls `openSubviewFromToast({ kind: 'plan', … fromRecipeId })`; that overlay helper (`Pinch.tsx`) does nothing while a subview is up or a dialog is open, like `showCalendarDay`. It then calls `announceRecipeAdded(id)` (`hooks/useRecipeAdded.ts`, a module-level set of listeners); a mounted `RecipesScreen` answers in the listener callback by switching to the Recipes pill, clearing search and filters and setting `revealId`, which makes the row's chunk eager and an effect scrolls `[data-recipe-id]` into view. The old "Recipe added · moved to Made into recipes" toast is gone.
- **Deleting a recipe says what goes.** The confirm (built in `RecipeForm` at the tap, from `store.getState()`) names what a hard delete takes: "Its cooking history (4 times) and its 3 notes are deleted too" from `selectCookSummaries` and `selectNotesByRecipe` (or "Its cooking history and notes go with it." while either listener hasn't landed or has failed), and for a recipe an idea was made into, on delete and on archive alike, "“X”, the idea you made it from, goes back to your ideas." What is deleted or archived is unchanged.

### 5a.2. Recipe collections and favorite saved meals

- **Making one.** Only in the recipe form's Collections row (`RecipeCollectionsField`, a `SummaryEditor`: one line, "High-protein, Weeknight" or "Add to a collection", a drawer on a phone and a popover from `sm`, no field focused on open so no keyboard covers the pills). The editor is `PillOptions` over every collection (staged new ones first, then A to Z; a search past 12 and "Show all N" from `PillGroup`'s own rules) with a dashed "New collection" pill first that reveals a name field and an Add button (Enter adds, and never closes the editor or saves the recipe). The form holds `{ ids, added }` (`CollectionsChoice`); a typed name that matches a collection that exists or one already staged (`getCollectionKey`) picks it instead of making a second, a new one gets its id from `generateUuid` at once, and unpicking a staged one drops it. Saving runs `getCollectionsToSave` (ids that still name a collection, new ones still picked) into `draft.collectionIds` and `draft.newCollections`; `saveRecipe` writes each new collection in the same `writeBatch` as the recipe, like `newSource`, so a failed or offline save leaves no orphan and queues whole.
- **Caps.** At most 50 collections a person (`MAX_COLLECTIONS`): the form stops offering "New collection" and says so, the client only, since the rules cannot count documents (a second device can pass it; nothing breaks). At most 20 per recipe (`MAX_RECIPE_COLLECTIONS`): the form stops at 20 and says so, and the rules refuse a longer `collectionIds`. A label is 40 characters in the form and the rules. `check:pinch-math` reads `firestore.rules` and fails if the rules' 20 and 40 drift from the constants.
- **Writing and legacy.** `collectionIds` is a field the recipe form owns: an edit writes the form's list with `title`, `servings` and the rest, so a recipe from before collections gains `[]` on its first save, and a new recipe always writes the key. A form that couldn't offer the row (the collections listener had not landed, or had failed, when it opened: `ownsCollections` is read once at mount) sends `collectionIds: null`, which leaves the stored list alone (an older recipe still gains `[]`), so a form that didn't show collections never overwrites them. Changing a recipe's collections is organizing, not changing the dish, so it records no tweak (`getRecipeChanges` doesn't read it). The save does not re-read the stored list, so two devices editing the same recipe's collections at once last-write-wins on the whole list, as for every field the form owns.
- **Deleting and renaming.** `renameCollection` is a field-scoped `updateDoc` of `label` and `updatedAt` after checking the loaded list for another collection with the same key. `removeCollection` takes the collection off every recipe in the loaded store that names it, archived ones included (`getRemovalChunks`, chunks of `COLLECTION_BATCH_SIZE` = 200), one `runTransaction` per chunk that re-reads each recipe and `update`s only those whose stored list still names it (`withoutCollection`, which returns null for a missing key, a non-list or a list that doesn't have it, so a recipe another device already took it off is not written and not counted), leaving `updatedAt` alone because it versions the cover; the chunks run in parallel and their counts add up to the number really changed; then it deletes the collection document last, so a chunk that fails leaves the collection in place to be deleted again. The confirm says how many listed recipes it is on ("They stay, and only come out of this collection"), the toast how many it came off. A recipe added to it on another device after the chunks were picked keeps an id nothing names, which every reader ignores. A transaction needs a connection, so an offline delete fails with "Check your connection and try again." and leaves everything as it was.
- **Reading.** `selectCollections` (A to Z), `selectCollectionsById`, `selectCollectionCounts` (`{ byCollection, withoutCollection }` over the listed, non-archived recipes in one pass; a recipe naming only a deleted collection counts in `withoutCollection`) and `selectIsCollectionsReady` (loaded without error). The listener (`startCollectionsListener`, from `usePinchSync`) is an optional extra like sources: its error is logged, clears the list, shows only in the UI it feeds (the form's row, the filter's section and the drawer's chips disappear), is not part of `selectIsPinchLoaded` and is left out of `selectPinchLoadError`.
- **The filter.** A Collections question in the Recipes `FilterBar`, drawn while any collection exists and the Recipes pill is on (`hasCollectionSection`): a pick-one `PillGroup` from `getCollectionFilterOptions` ("Any", each collection that holds a listed recipe as "Label · count", the picked one while it is empty, and "No collection · n" only while some recipe is in none), `filterByCollection` applies it between the source and stock filters, the session key is `recipes:collection`, it falls back to Any when the picked collection is gone or no recipe is in any, and the active chip reads "In High-protein" or "In no collection" (removing it or Clear clears it). One pick, not several: two picked pills could mean "either" or "both", and the pills' counts would then be wrong either way, so the intersection of two collections is not offered. The question's title line carries a 40px pencil (`Edit collections`) that closes the filter panel first (`FilterBar` hands its children a `close`) and opens `CollectionsSheet`: one `FormSheet` whose list (a tinted intro card under eight, a search from eight, a framed list capped so it scrolls on its own with a floor of about three rows at 320×568) swaps in place to one collection's name and trash with ‹ Back (`useBackStep`) and a destructive confirm on top. Collections are not made there. A collection's label is in the recipes search (`buildRecipeSearchIndex`'s optional fourth argument adds the labels of the collections a recipe names to the part of the haystack a word has to start a word of, after the title), so typing "weeknight" finds that collection's recipes; a collection named like an ingredient adds results after the title matches, which was judged helpful rather than noisy, and the collection filter is one tap away for the exact set.
- **The drawer.** `RecipeDrawer` draws a recipe's collections as `LabelChip`s on the source's line (`flex-wrap`, so they wrap under it), A to Z, each a door (a chevron, `aria-label` "In X: show its recipes") only while its collection holds other recipes (`selectCollectionCounts`), else a plain label; `getCollectionChipWindow` shows all of them unless more than three would hide, then four and a "+N more" chip that opens the rest in place and "Show fewer" after. A tap calls `onShowCollection`, which `RecipesScreen` answers like `onShowSource` (drawer closed, Recipes pill, search, source and stock cleared, the collection picked, scroll to the top after the sheet's history step). This puts them in the drawer's first group, who it is, with no fourth group and no fact said twice; a recipe with no source and no collection draws nothing, as before.
- **Suggestions.** `getSuggestedCollections(source, collections, memberIds)` is pure and checked. High-protein: protein per serving, rounded to the whole grams the form shows, at or above `HIGH_PROTEIN_GRAMS` (25). The protein is `getTrustedProtein`: the person's own typed number, else the added-up one only when no ingredient with an amount lacked a protein value (`getRecipeNutrition`'s `missing`) and every line is complete; a sum with an unknown ingredient is not trusted. Quick: prep plus cook at or under `QUICK_MAX_MINUTES` (30), both known and the total above zero (a missing time is not zero). Freezer-friendly: the recipe's care has `freezerDays`. A suggestion names the collection that matches by `getCollectionKey` (under the person's own spelling) or says it would make one, is left out once the recipe is in it, and is left out when it would make a 51st. They appear as `SuggestionChips` in the editor, and as "Suggested: High-protein, Quick" in place of "Add to a collection" on the closed row while the recipe is in none; a tap adds the recipe (making the collection in the same save), nothing is ever added on its own. **Vegetarian is left out on purpose:** nothing stored says it without guessing (the catalog's "Sauces & condiments" holds both fish sauce and soy sauce, "Broth & stock" both chicken and vegetable broth, and a custom ingredient has no family), and a wrong "Vegetarian" is worse than none.
- **Favorites.** `Meal.isFavorite` (a boolean; a saved meal from before has no key and reads `false` through `isStarred`). `setMealFavorite` is a field-scoped `updateDoc({ isFavorite, updatedAt })` of the value asked for, never a toggle read from a cache; `saveMeal`'s edit path writes only `name`, `items` and `updatedAt` and never `isFavorite`, and a new meal writes `false`, so renaming a meal can't un-star it nor starring it lose a rename. The star is a `StarButton` (`src/components`, a 40px icon-only tertiary button whose icon fills when on, `aria-pressed`, a constant label "Favorite Adobo and rice") on `MealRow`, beside the chevron on the first-line band (a sibling of the row's button, positioned over its right edge, because a button can't sit inside a button), and in `MealDrawer`'s title line through `DetailSheet`'s `actions`. `useMealFavorite(uid)` is the one tap: it ignores a tap while the previous write for that meal hasn't shown yet (`shouldIgnoreStarTap`, so a double tap can't read stale state, and one deliberate second tap later goes through even while the first still waits for a connection) and says "Couldn't star X" in a toast when the write fails. `selectMeals` is `sortFavoritesFirst` of newest first (a stable partition: each group keeps its order), so every reader (the list, the plan builder's search, the container form) gets favorites first, and `rankRecentPicks` puts starred saved meals first in the plan builder's recents. The Recipes `FilterBar` gets a Favorites question (one "★ Favorites · n" pill, session key `recipes:favorites`, ignored once nothing is starred) on the Saved meals pill only while a meal is starred and there are at least eight saved meals (`LIST_SEARCH_THRESHOLD`, the list's own search rule: with fewer, the starred ones already lead and a filter bar would have nothing to narrow); its chip reads "Favorites". Recipes are not starred in this round: a "Favorites" collection does that.

### 5b. Shopping list

- **Lines are pure functions** in `utils/shopping.ts`, checked by `npm run check:pinch-math`. `getRecipeLines(recipe, ingredientsById, servings)` scales each recipe line by `servings / recipe.servings`. `getItemsLines(items, recipesById, ingredientsById)` serves a saved meal, a planned meal and a whole day: a recipe item scales its recipe by the item's `servings` over the recipe's own, an ingredient item is its `servings` times the ingredient's serving size. A line whose recipe or ingredient is gone is left out. `mergeLines` adds lines with the same ingredient and unit, rounds once at the end to two decimals (thirds add up to one before rounding), caps an amount at 1,000,000 and drops a line that rounds to nothing. Units are never converted. A recipe line with no amount is `{ amount: null, unit: 'EACH' }` at every serving count; `mergeLines` keeps two of them as one name-only line, lets an amount take in a name-only line of the same item, and drops a name-only line when the batch has the ingredient with an amount in any unit (and keeps it when that amount rounds to nothing).
- **Adding** is `addToShoppingList({ uid, lines })` where each line carries its `source` (or null for a typed add): the lines are merged per item with every source behind them (`mergeSourcedLines`, which also drops a name-only line that an amount of the same ingredient absorbs and hands its sources to that amount, so a day with "Parsley" and "Parsley 30 g" reads as on the list) and checked (not empty, at most 200 distinct, an id of 80 characters or fewer, a known unit, an amount above zero or none in `EACH`), then one `runTransaction` reads every target document first and writes each: a missing item is created (`isChecked: false`), an unchecked one gains the amount (rounded to two decimals, capped), and a checked one restarts with the new amount and `isChecked: false` (a name-only line adds nothing to an open amount and restarts a checked item as just the name). Each written item's `sources` is `mergeSources(existing, added)` (a set, newest last, capped at 60), backfilling the key on an item written before it. That decision is the pure `getMergedFields(existing, line, now, isChecked)` in `utils/shopping.ts`, so `npm run check:pinch-math` covers it (a new line, an open item gaining an amount, a checked item restarting, the 1,000,000 cap, a unit move carrying its flag, an amount that rounds to zero, a recipe with no servings); the transaction code only reads and writes around it. `mergeLines` also drops a non-finite amount, so a recipe whose servings are 0 gives no lines instead of an endless one. Two devices adding at the same time retry on conflict, so neither amount is lost (checked in the browser with two pages adding one recipe at once). The manual "+ Add" uses the same action with one line at the ingredient's serving size and no source. A transaction does not queue offline, so adding needs a connection and says so by failing; ticking, changing and removing are ordinary writes and queue. `ShoppingPick` (in `components/shared/AddToShoppingSheet.tsx`, the body both the drawers' `AddToShoppingSheet` and the Shopping tab's "From your plan" render) builds its rows with `groupShopLines`: one row per ingredient and kind of unit (`getShopRowId` = ingredient + `UNIT_DIMENSION`), each row's tick writing all its lines; a line with just the name joins the amount row of the same ingredient (the first by id when several have one), so "Salt" is one row, not two. A row starts unticked when this sheet's own sources already put it on the list (below) or `isUsuallyAtHome` (section `spices`, or `USUALLY_AT_HOME_IDS`: water). A range request reads `selectPlannedByDay` through `getPlanInRange(plannedByDay, range, todayKey)` (days before the viewer's local today are left out, `isClamped` says so); the range is clamped to the loaded planned-meals window (`getMonthWindow(windowKey)`), the calendar can't pick outside it, and a sheet opened while the window doesn't hold today says the plan isn't loaded. The sheet's list and the Week summary's list keep a floor of 9rem on screens under 800px high (`[@media(max-height:800px)]:min-h-36`), so at 375×667 and 320×568 they show about three rows instead of none; on those heights the drawer body may scroll a little, the accepted fallback.
- **Defaults.** A sheet builds its lines per source (`getSourcedLines`: a planned meal's lines carry its id, unrounded, so shared amounts round once when they meet; a recipe's carry `recipe:<id>:<servings>` with the stepper's servings, a saved meal's `meal:<id>`). Sources are compared by key (`getSourceKey`: a recipe's id without the servings), so one recipe added at 4 and at 8 servings is one source everywhere it is read. An add onto an open item adds the servings up (`addSources`: 4 then 8 is `recipe:<id>:12`, an older `recipe:<id>` stays beside the new entry), and an add onto a got item, whose amount starts again, keeps only the new add's servings. `getRowOnList(lines, rowItems)` says, per row, which of the sheet's sources already put it on the list (open or got), whether every one has (`isCovered`, the row starts unticked), and what ticking adds: the lines of sources not yet there, or all of them once every source is there. An open item with no `sources` key covers its row (the guard every line had before). `getOnListHint` writes the row's line ("Already on your list for Thu dinner", "Already got it for …", "…; this adds the rest", or "On your list · 2 lb" when something else put it there), and the line under the sheet's "Adding 3 of 6" count is "Untick what you already have." while any row starts ticked, else "Tick what you need.". The day drawer's row uses `getDayOnList` (same helpers): "On your list ✓" when every planned meal with lines is a source of any item, else the count of covered rows.
- **The other writes are field-scoped.** A tick is one `writeBatch` of `update({ isChecked, updatedAt })` over every stored item behind the row (usually one; two for a row that combines units), so it still queues offline and touches only its own row (no surface-wide busy state). Changing an amount is `updateDoc({ amount, updatedAt })`. A tick and an amount edit never touch `sources`. Changing the unit moves the item in one transaction: the old document is deleted and the amount lands on the new unit's document by the same rules as an add, except that a new document takes the moved line's `isChecked`, so a ticked line stays ticked, and the row's sources (the union of the items it folds) move with it. Moving into an existing document follows the add rules: an open one gains the amount, a ticked one restarts open with only the moved amount, and the row sheet's note says which before saving. Remove is a `writeBatch` of deletes for the row's items, dispatched without waiting (so it queues offline) and followed by an Undo toast; Undo waits for the delete, then runs `restoreShoppingItems`: one `runTransaction` per chunk of 250 that recreates each item exactly as it was (its `createdAt` and `isChecked` kept, `updatedAt` now) unless it exists again, which keeps what it has. Like an add, Undo needs a connection. Saving a row that stands for two units passes `mergedIds` to `updateShoppingItem`, whose transaction deletes the row's other items and writes the typed amount to the target unit. "Clear" takes the ids of the checked items from the store when it is dispatched, then deletes them in chunks of 250, one `runTransaction` per chunk: each re-reads its documents and deletes only the ones that are still checked, so an item another device un-ticked or removed in the meantime survives (a stale cache can't delete it), and the thunk resolves to the items really deleted, as they were, so the toast can count them by row ("Cleared 15 items", with "The rest changed in the meantime" when fewer than were ticked at the tap) and its Undo can put them back with `restoreShoppingItems`. There is no confirm. Clearing or removing deletes the document, so its sources go with it, and Undo puts them back. Chunks are independent transactions, so several chunks are not atomic together: one that fails leaves the others cleared and the list shows what is left. A Firebase error is logged and replaced by one plain sentence, "Check your connection and try again." for `unavailable` or when the browser is offline; the app's own sentences ("This is already off your list.") are shown as written.
- **The caps are limits of the app, not of the database.** 200 distinct lines per add and about 300 items on the list are enforced in the action and the screens only: the rules can't count a collection, so another client could pass either. Nothing breaks if it does (the list is longer, and a section still draws 100 rows then "Show N more").
- **Reading.** `selectShoppingRows` groups once per change (`groupShoppingItems`): what is left by shop section in walking order (`SHOP_SECTIONS`; `getShopSection` takes `SHOP_SECTION_BY_INGREDIENT`, then `SHOP_SECTION_BY_FAMILY`, else `other`, so a custom family and a gone ingredient are Other), one row per ingredient and kind of unit (`ShoppingRowData.itemIds`, largest first; ids end in `/got` in the Got it half so the halves never share one), rows A to Z by name; a row's amount is `getShopAmount` (summed in the unit of the largest item, a count rounded up, null for under a tablespoon or 15 g of a spice or oil); what is checked off is grouped the same way into the Got it half (`groupShoppingItems` returns `{ toBuy, got }`); a row in the optional `held` map is placed by its held side instead of its stored flag (see "Holding a ticked row"). `selectShoppingOpenCount` feeds the Shopping tab's badge. `selectShoppingCost` prices the unchecked lines through `getLineCost` with each ingredient's default product, the same way a recipe's lines are priced, so it prices the amount on the list and not whole packages; what it can't price comes back as `Gap`s (`NO_PRODUCT`, `NOT_COMPARABLE`), which the help icon beside the cost line lists by ingredient; it also returns `rowCount` and `pricedRowCount` (rows as the list draws them, priced when every line is). `getShoppingCostCoverage` turns that into `none`, `tooFew` (under half), `some` or `all`; the screen draws the line ("About $3.16 for 3 of 4 things" or "for all 4 things") only for `some` and `all`, on To buy and All and under the search field (so the field doesn't move between To buy and Got it), and draws nothing for the other two, so Shopping never says "Not enough prices yet" or "No price yet". The icon (`HelpTip`, a modal on a phone or touch screen with its button saying "OK", and a tooltip with a mouse) is a plain help icon when nothing is left out and a warning when something is; beside a figure it says the amounts aren't whole packages, and it names what has no price ("Prices come from what you've told Pinch about where you buy things."); a `NOT_COMPARABLE` gap keeps its Kitchen pointer (a fill-in sheet never opens over this screen's own sheets, so the text points the way). A name-only item joins the row of the same ingredient that has an amount on its side of the list (`getItemRowIds`), so ticking, removing or editing that row covers it; the badge counts drawn rows (`countOpenShopRows`). The add sheet reads what a row stands for through `getItemsByRow(rows, items)` (every item, for a day's "On your list ✓" in `getDayOnList`) or `getOpenItemsByRow` (the open ones): a name-only row maps to the row the list draws for that ingredient, and a row that holds a name-only line also picks up that ingredient's name-only items. The "Got it" count and "N are ticked off" count rows (`countRows`), the same unit as the rows under them; the "rest changed in the meantime" check on Clear counts stored items. `selectShoppingCost` leaves a name-only line out of both the total and the row counts: it has nothing to price.
- **What a line is for.** `selectShoppingSourceLabels` (the name, kind, and a planned meal's day and meal of every source on the list that still resolves, by key) feeds `getShoppingSourcesText(sources, labels, todayKey)`, which names every source and never only counts them: a planned meal by its slot (`formatSourceSlot`: "Thu dinner", a day in the coming week by weekday and otherwise by date, "Oct 23 lunch"; two plates in one slot add their dish, "Thu dinner (Taco night)"), a recipe or a saved meal by its own name, in the list's order (`compareSourceLabels`: planned meals by day, the ones that have passed first, then meal, then recipes and saved meals by name). Three names are said in full ("Thu dinner, Fri lunch and Adobo chicken"), more as two and "and 5 more" (`joinSourceNames`, which names a single leftover rather than writing "and 1 more"), null when nothing resolves. The screen works each row's line out once per set of sources and change of names (a `Map` in a closure made per change of the labels, read by both the row and the chunk's height estimate), so a tick re-renders no names. The row's detail line adds "· for …" and wraps (two lines at most for two names and a count at 390 and 320px, three with three long names at 320; the separator is glued to the amount with non-breaking spaces so it never ends a line); "For …" alone for a line with no amount. The row sheet lists every source instead (`getRowSourceShares`): the slot or name, under it the dish for a planned meal (`selectShoppingPlannedParts`: for an unnamed plate of two or more items, the one item that wants the ingredient, else the plate's name), and on the right what that source asks of the row now (`getKeyShares`, the same share a stop takes off, in the row's units; nothing for a name-only line), rounded by largest remainder so the shares add up to the amount under them (0.13 and 0.12 cup above 0.25; whole counts that add up to the count to pick up) when every share is in the row's unit, each in its own unit otherwise, five shown and "Show N more" only when four or more would hide. A source whose planned meal is outside the loaded months reads like a deleted one until the calendar loads it.
- **What the list is for, and stopping one meal.** `selectShoppingMeals(state, todayKey)` (`getShoppingMeals`) lists every source that still resolves with the rows it put on the list, counted the way the list draws them (`getItemRowIds` per half, so a row in each half counts twice, like the All count), how many are still to buy, and whether its day has passed, in the list's order. The Shopping tab shows it as one `EntryTile`, "Meals on your list", in a `nav` under the search (or under the switch below 8 things), only while that list has something in it and nothing is typed in the search: a first run, a list of typed adds only and a list from before sources were kept have no door. Its status names the soonest meals (`getShoppingMealsText`: two by name, a slot two plates share said once, "and 74 more"). It opens `ShoppingMealsSheet`, a `DetailSheet` (a drawer on a phone, a modal from `sm`, which ends in a Done that takes the first focus so Enter never removes a meal): a line saying what Remove does, a `SearchInput` from 8 meals (slot, dish, "Recipe", "Saved meal"), and one scrolling list capped by its column (`max-h-[max(12rem,calc(92dvh-12rem))]`, `sm:max-h-[calc(100dvh-21rem)]`, a floor of `min-h-36` under 800px high) of 40 rows and "Show N more". A row is the slot (planned) or the name, a `FactsLine` ("Passed" for a day gone by, the dish or "Recipe" / "Saved meal", "3 of 8 to buy", "8 to buy" or "Got everything") and a secondary "Remove" (`aria-label` "Remove Thu dinner from your list", a 40px hit area from a pseudo-element, centred on the title line) only while something of it is still to buy. A removed row stays where it was, struck through, saying "Taken off your list", until the sheet closes, so the rows under a second tap never move; the button is disabled while its write runs. Remove dispatches `stopShoppingFor({ uid, source })` with the source's key: it checks the source still resolves, reads the other sources' shares from the store (`getOtherShares`), then, in chunks of 125 items run one after another, one `runTransaction` re-reads each item carrying the source and the name-only (`EACH`) item of each measured ingredient among them, and applies the pure `planStopShopping`: a got item only loses the tag; one to buy loses the source's share (`getKeyShares`: each entry of the source on an item is worked out the way its add was, a recipe at the servings that entry kept or its own for an older entry, a planned or saved meal's items as they are, and the entries add up; within an entry `getSourceShares` lands a line on the item of its ingredient and unit, else on one of the same ingredient and kind of unit, converted, for an amount the person moved to another unit) and is deleted when nothing is left (an amount within 0.02 of zero, since each add and each share round to hundredths, or a name-only line nothing else wants); what is left with no other source behind it stays, untagged, and is reported, never deleted, since it may be an amount the person typed onto the line before the add (a typed line and a meal's share are one document); when the share runs past what is left and something else wants the item, it keeps that one's share but never grows (`min`), keeps its amount when one of the others no longer resolves, or becomes just the name (in place for `EACH`, else moved into that ingredient's name-only item, created or merged into). An item the person typed on the list has no source, so it never carries the tag and is never touched. Each write is a full `set` of the item as planned (same `id`, `ingredientId`, `unit` and `createdAt`, so the update rule holds) or a delete; each entry records what happened to its line (`removed`, `kept` for one that still has another source, `reduced` for a share taken off with nothing else behind the rest, `left` for one the source asks nothing of now, `got`), and the thunk resolves to the entries and `countStopRows` over the list as drawn at the tap: rows, not stored items, so a cup and two tablespoons of soy sauce are one thing. The toast says only what happened, clause by clause ("Took Thu dinner off your list", "5 things came off your list.", "…; 1 thing went down by this meal's share" ("this recipe's" for a recipe), "…; 1 thing stays, since something else on your list still needs it" only when the line still has another source, "…; 1 thing stays as it was"). There is no confirm: the toast's Undo (6 s, once per toast, above the open sheet since toasts sit above drawers) runs `undoStopShopping`, one transaction per chunk of 250 that puts each item back as it was (`updatedAt` now) only while it is still as the stop left it (`isAsStopLeft`: still gone, or the same `updatedAt`), so a line ticked or changed on another device in the meantime keeps what it has, and says "Put back 3 of 5" when it skipped some. **What can drift:** the share is worked out from the meal as it is now, so a meal or recipe edited after it was added takes off its new amounts, clamped at zero; a recipe's servings are kept, so the stepper never drifts, but a planned or saved meal added a second time on purpose (a row that was already on the list, ticked again) holds two shares under one tag and loses one; an amount the person typed on top of a meal's share, or a line the meal no longer has, stays, untagged, and the toast says it went down or stayed as it was; a planned meal deleted from the plan can't be removed here (it no longer resolves, so it isn't listed). **Offline:** a transaction needs the server, so with the browser offline Remove doesn't start and the sheet says "Removing a meal needs a connection, so Thu dinner is still on your list. Try again once you're back online."; a failure while it runs is one red line in the sheet with the action's sentence ("Check your connection and try again." for `unavailable`). No rules or index change: every write keeps the round-6 shape (`amount` null only in `EACH`, at most 60 `sources`, which the rules check only as a list, so the servings suffix needs nothing, immutable `ingredientId`, `unit` and `createdAt`), proven against the emulator as the owner (a lowered line with fewer sources, a delete, a new name-only line, a merge into a line with no `sources` key, each Undo write) and refused as another signed-in user and signed out.
- **Searching.** The list is one thin `PillGroup` of "To buy · n", "Got it · n" and "All · n" (counts of the rows drawn, which follow the search; All is the sum). `ShoppingView` is `ShoppingHalf | 'all'` and `groupShoppingItems` still returns the two halves (`Record<ShoppingHalf, …>`); All renders the to-buy half as usual and the got half as one flat run of rows (`ShoppingRows`, chunked and lazy like a group, eager only when the to-buy part is short) under a `SectionDivider` "Got it" that carries the Clear button. The shown view is derived each render by `getShownShoppingView(pick, query, matches)` (All is always kept; a half is the person's pick unless text typed since the pick matches nothing there and something in the other half), never mirrored with an effect, and with nothing left to buy the To buy half says so and offers "See what you got". From `LIST_SEARCH_THRESHOLD` (8) items the screen shows a `SearchInput` under the switch that filters both halves by ingredient name (`searchByName`, accent-insensitive, every word must match) from the typed text, not a debounced value. Nothing matching says so in one muted line ("Nothing on your list matches “milk”."). The pill counts show the matches; "Clear what you got" (a quiet line in the Got it half and on All's divider, only while something is got and never while searching) still clears every ticked item (with an Undo). If the list drops under 8 items the field disappears and the filter with it.
- **Searching the catalog.** `searchIngredients` (`utils/catalogSearch.ts`) matches every typed word against the name, aliases and the catalog family's name, and ranks: exact name, exact alias, name starts with the text, every word starts a word of the name, member of a matching family, alias starts with it, anything else; A to Z within a rank. `ItemSearch` keeps its 8-result cap and draws its results, and a search with no match (one line and the "New ingredient" row), in one framed block; Kitchen's search uses the same matching, so a family's name finds its members there too ("pasta" lists the pastas).
- **Holding a ticked row.** A tick used to move its row into "Got it" within one frame, so the row below jumped up by a row height and a quick second tap landed on the wrong item. Now the stored flag, `selectShoppingOpenCount` (the tab badge) and `selectShoppingCost` change at once, and only where a row is drawn lags: `useHeldRows` in `ShoppingScreen` records, before the write, the side the row was drawn on (`groupShoppingItems(…, held)` places by that side while the row keeps its stored `isChecked`, so the checkbox and the strike-through are the truth) and lets go after `SHOPPING_MOVE_DELAY_MS` (1.2 s). An un-tick in "Got it" leaves it the same way, a second toggle of one row keeps its first side and restarts only its own timer, and the other rows' timers are untouched. The held map is a ref, read by the selector closure when the store changes: holding renders nothing by itself (the snapshot that follows the write does, once, as before), and only letting go renders, so a tick costs no extra render of the screen. Timers are cleared when the screen unmounts, so leaving the tab mid-hold leaves nothing stuck (the row is simply in "Got it" when you return); a hold that never sees its write land (a failed tick) just lets go. "Clear" counts what is stored, so a row still drawn in its group is cleared with the rest, and the "To buy · N" / "Got it · N" labels count what is drawn, so for a second they are one behind the badge. A tick holds every item behind its row. Checked on the 80-item list of the scale kitchen at 4× CPU: click to next frame for a tick is a median of 86 ms before and 93 ms after (5 interleaved runs each); a tick renders about 30 components and the move after the hold about 48, against about 51 in one go before.
- **Drawing.** A section's rows are chunks of 12, each a `LazyMount` with `defer-offscreen` on the chunk (a row estimated at 57 px; the first chunk of the first three sections mounts at once), so one long section (the scale kitchen's 64 custom-family rows in Other) never mounts whole and can flow across both `lg` columns; under 8 rows the list is one run with no dividers; a section draws 100 rows then "Show N more" (and nothing is hidden for 103 or fewer), and the "Got it" rows mount only while that half is open. The drawers' add sheets (`AddToShoppingSheet`, titled "Shopping list") live in the screen that owns the drawer (`RecipesScreen`, `CalendarScreen`) and open after the drawer closes, as `FillInSheet` does.
- **Failure.** The shopping listener is an optional extra: its error is logged (`console.error`), clears the loaded items and sets `loadError`, never reaches the load-error screen (`selectPinchLoadError` leaves it out) and is not part of the loading gate (`selectIsPinchLoaded`). The screen then says it couldn't load the list and offers no Add, and the tab's badge shows nothing. While the first snapshot hasn't landed the screen says "Loading your list…". Both add sheets wait for `state.pinch.shopping.isLoaded` (and a null `loadError`) before they let anything through: `AddToShoppingSheet` disables Add, "Tick all" and its checkboxes with "Loading your list…" or "Your list couldn't load, so adding is paused." in the pinned footer, because its defaults (what this sheet's own sources already put on the list starts unticked) come from the listener's data, and `ShoppingAddSheet` locks its results with the same lines and also checks the 300-item cap when a result is picked, not only when it opens. The gate deliberately leaves the shopping slice out of `selectIsPinchLoaded`, so a shopping failure never reaches the fatal screen.

### 5c. Recipe ideas

- **Adding.** "New idea" opens one `FormSheet` (`IdeaAddSheet`: a drawer on a phone, a modal from `sm`), whose first view is the `MethodPicker` of the three ways (`IDEA_START_METHODS`; *Jot it down* is last and quieter). A way's own view swaps in place with a "Back" link to the list, so there is never a second overlay, and Escape or the back gesture closes the one sheet. Each way is a Dreamer UI `Form` of custom fields with the footer pinned (`Cancel`, "Save idea" disabled until valid); the title of the sheet is "New idea" on the list and "Idea" on a way. The sheet is also mounted by `Pinch.tsx` (`openIdeaSheet` on the overlay context) for the New recipe list's last row, "Keep it as an idea", with `confirmsWithToast` and no `onOpenExisting` (a duplicate link then only says so).
- **Saving without waiting.** The ways run `getNewIdeaProblem(draft, ideasState)` (the checks `createIdea` makes, exported from `ideaActions.ts`) before anything else, so a problem stays in the sheet. A LINK or TEXT idea then gets its id from the caller (`createIdea({ uid, draft, id })`), the thunk is dispatched without being awaited and the sheet closes at once; `trackIdeaSave(id, promise)` (`hooks/useIdeaSaving.ts`) marks the idea as waiting if the write hasn't been acknowledged after 1.5 s, and `IdeaRow` (`useIsIdeaWaiting`) shows "Saves when you're back online" until it settles; a refusal raises a "That idea didn't save" toast. A PHOTO idea still awaits the upload and the write; its Cancel becomes Close while it runs (the save carries on; a failure after the sheet closed is a toast). Firestore's persistent cache keeps a queued write across a reload.
- **Save as an idea from a reading screen.** `RecipeReader` takes `onSaveAsIdea` (a chip on the text and link screens, disabled while empty or reading; `RecipeSubview` passes it only when not opened from an idea) and `initialText` / `initialLink`, so the reader keeps what was typed when it mounts again. `RecipeSubview` holds the kept `{ text } | { link }` and a stage `'idea'` (header "Idea"; back, Escape and the way's Cancel return to the reading stage): a link renders `IdeaLinkWay` with `initialUrl` (the pasted text run through `toWebLink`), text renders `IdeaTextWay` with `initialTitle` (the first non-blank line, `clipIdeaTitle`) and `initialNote` (the text cut to `MAX_IDEA_NOTE_LENGTH`, with a line saying so). Saving closes the subview and toasts "Saved … to your ideas" (with the offline line when `navigator.onLine` is false). The file way isn't covered: it reads a file as soon as it is picked.
- **From a link.** The field is `type='url'` (the submit button carries `formNoValidate`, since a link with no scheme is fine), `toWebLink` (`@/utils/urlUtils`) trims the ends, picks the one `http(s)://` token out of pasted text that has words around it (two such tokens give no link, and the field says "That looks like more than one link. Paste one at a time."), joins a scheme-less link that wrapped over lines, adds `https://` when no scheme is typed and refuses anything that isn't an http(s) address with a dotted host and no user name or password, and `toIdeaUrl` also caps it at 2,000 characters. A paste into the field that holds more than one word is run through it first (the browser would otherwise glue or drop the line breaks of a pasted line). After `DEBOUNCE_MS.linkDetection` the page is looked up with `fetchPageContent` through a TanStack Query factory (`queries/ideaPageQueries.ts`): `include: ['meta']` only, a callable timeout of `IDEA_LOOKUP_TIMEOUT_MS`, the shared 10-minute stale time for a success, no retry and no `persist`; a failure is a query error, never data, so it is never kept as an empty answer and a repeat of the same link asks again. `meta` carries the page's `<title>` (as `title`), `og:site_name` and `og:image` or `twitter:image` (resolved to an absolute address); the `images` kind is not asked for, because its other entries are the page's own `<img>` tags (logos, ads) and a wrong picture is worse than the tile. The line before anything is sent says what happens ("We fetch the page to find its title and picture. Nothing is saved from it except those."). While the lookup runs the sheet says so; when it returns, the preview shows the picture, a "Looks like" title field the person can change, and the site name; when it fails or times out, a title field with the host as its placeholder and "Couldn't read that page. You can type your own title." (a page that answered with no title gets the same field and "That page didn't give a title. You can type your own."). Saving waits for the lookup to settle (a result, a failure or the `IDEA_LOOKUP_TIMEOUT_MS` limit, plus the debounce): until then Save is disabled and the pinned footer says "Looking up the page. You can save in a moment.", so a link is never saved before its page's title could arrive. Once settled the title is what was typed, else the page's title for this very link, else (in `createIdea`, for a title left blank after a failed or empty lookup) the host, and `siteName` is the page's or the host. A typed title belongs to the link it was typed for (`{ link, title }`), so pasting another link starts again. A link already on the list (`findIdeaByUrl`, comparing `getComparableUrl`: no scheme, `www.`, fragment, trailing slash or tracking parameters, the other parameters in order) shows "That one's already in your ideas" with an "Open it" button, which closes the sheet and opens that idea's drawer, and disables saving; the lookup isn't made for it.
- **From a photo.** `PhotoPicker` (the shared file picker; its new `uploadLabel` and `changeLabel` props and optional `onRemove` are general) and `useImageUpload` (images only, up to 5 MB) feed `createIdea`, which resizes the file with `resizeImageToWebp` to `IDEA_PHOTO_MAX_DIMENSION` (a result that isn't `image/webp` counts as unreadable, and the resize happens before anything is uploaded, so a refused file touches no Storage object), uploads it to `getIdeaPhotoPath(uid, id)` before the document is written, and deletes it again if the write fails. A photo idea needs a title (the field starts empty with "What is it?") and a picture; a failed upload keeps the form and says why through `getStorageErrorMessage` (a neutral "We couldn't save that picture." when the code has no message of its own). A save failure shows as one red line in the footer's `note` slot (the pinned footer, beside the buttons, never below the form where the footer would cover it) and clears when the form changes or a picture is picked.
- **Jot it down.** A title and, shown from the start, a note.
- **The note** is a visible optional second field: an "Add a note" chip (`RevealChip`) with a "Remove note" chip on the link and photo ways, open from the start on the text way and in the edit sheet.
- **The drawer.** `IdeaDrawer` (a `DetailSheet`, titled "Idea") shows the picture (only if there is one; a failed one leaves nothing), the title, the note (pre-wrapped), a made idea's tinted "Made into <recipe>" row, and for a link a quiet "Open <site name>" row (`Button href`, `target='_blank'`, `rel='noopener noreferrer'`, only for an http(s) address). Its footer is Modify (secondary) beside one primary: "Turn it into a recipe", or "Open the recipe" once made. Modify closes the drawer and opens `IdeaFormSheet`; "Open the recipe" closes it and opens the recipe's own drawer (`RecipesScreen`'s `openId`).
- **Editing.** `IdeaFormSheet` (title, link for a LINK, a photo with "Replace photo" and no remove for a PHOTO, note) saves through `updateIdea`, a field-scoped `updateDoc` of `title`, `note`, `updatedAt`, plus `url` (and `siteName`, `imageUrl: null` when the link names another page; the same page spelled differently keeps its site and picture) and `photoPath` when a replacement was picked, plus the keys an older document lacks. A replaced photo overwrites the same Storage object, and the picture's download URL is keyed on `updatedAt`, so it is fetched again. Delete is a destructive `useActionModal().confirm` ("Delete this idea?" naming it; for a made idea it says the recipe stays), then `removeIdea` (`deleteDoc`, then the photo object, best effort).
- **Turning an idea into a recipe.** The primary opens the recipe subview with `method: getIdeaRecipeMethod(idea)` (`'link'` for a LINK whose address `toWebLink` accepts, `'manual'` for a photo, a few words or an unusable address) and `fromIdeaId`: `{ kind: 'recipe', id: null, method, fromIdeaId }`. `RecipeSubview` reads the idea once, when it mounts. On the link way it hands `RecipeReader` the idea's address as `initialUrl` (the field starts filled; nothing is sent until the button is tapped, so the way's own line is the disclosure) and shows "From your idea: <title>" above it; `RecipeReader` with an `initialUrl` never draws the "Paste its text instead" row after a failed link, since the text way isn't one tap away from an idea.
