Back, Escape and the phone's back gesture from that way call `closeSubview` (to the Ideas list, never to the list of ways), the dashed "Write it yourself" row goes to the form below (asking first only if the address was edited), and a reading goes through `getReadingForIdea` before it reaches the form: the idea's note replaces the page's description when it has one, and the idea's title stays unless it is only the link's host (what a failed lookup saved), which gives way to the recipe's title; everything else (ingredients, steps, servings, times, the website as the source) is the reading's, and `fromIdeaId` still reaches `saveRecipe`, so the idea is marked made whichever way the form was reached. `getDataFromIdea` starts the form with the title, the note as the description and, for a LINK, `getSiteSourceChoice(getWebLinkHost(url), sources)`: the existing source named for the link's host (the `getHostSource` matching, as PR A's link reading does), else "New source" with the host, created with the recipe. It deliberately uses the host and not the page's own site name, so a person with a source called "seriouseats.com" doesn't get a second "Serious Eats". Nothing else is read from the idea: a photo is not copied and stays with the idea (the form says so, and points to "From a file", for a PHOTO idea; the file reading can't take a picture from storage). The form shows no extra line except, for a photo idea, the one saying its photo stays with the idea, and its title editor doesn't open by itself. Leaving an untouched prefilled form asks nothing, and discarding changes nothing. `saveRecipe` takes `fromIdeaId`. The recipe's batch (the recipe, its new source and its new ingredients) is committed on its own, exactly as for any recipe, so an idea that has gone from the server can never take a recipe down with it (a batch queued offline is refused whole if one of its documents is missing). Only after that commit, and only if the idea is still in the loaded store, one field-scoped `updateDoc` writes `{ …missing keys, recipeId, updatedAt }` on the idea; a failure there is caught, logged with `console.error` and not thrown, and `saveRecipe` returns `{ recipeId, linkedIdea }`. The form's toast is the same "Added X to your recipes" with Plan it as for any new recipe, whatever `linkedIdea` says. The recipe's `source` is `MANUAL`.
- **Reading.** `selectIdeas` (newest first), `selectIdeasById` and `selectIdeaGroups` (`{ toTry, made }`: a set of the listed recipes' ids, then two filters over the ideas) feed `IdeasView`, which shows "Still to try" and "Made into recipes" with `SectionDivider` once both have rows, a search (`SearchInput`) from `LIST_SEARCH_THRESHOLD` ideas that looks through title, site name and note, and rows in chunks of twenty (`LazyMount` with `defer-offscreen`, the first chunk eager) that are as tall as their lines: a `IdeaThumbnail` (the link's own picture through `FallbackImage` with the emoji tile as its fallback, a photo's download URL, or the tile), a title of up to three lines, one muted line (`getIdeaDetail`: a link's site, else the first line of the note, and never a line that only repeats the title, as a link saved with its host as the title would) and a chevron pinned to the first line. Two columns from `lg`.
- **The cap.** `SOFT_MAX_IDEAS` (200) is enforced by `createIdea` against the loaded list and by the screens (the "New idea" button is replaced by "Your list holds up to 200 ideas. Delete one to add another."), not by the rules, which can't count a collection.
- **Failure.** The ideas listener is an optional extra: its error is logged (`console.error`), clears the loaded ideas and sets `loadError`, never reaches the load-error screen (`selectPinchLoadError` leaves it out) and is not part of the loading gate. "New idea" is disabled until the first snapshot has landed (the list says "Loading your ideas…") and stays disabled after a failure (the list says it couldn't load them and shows no search), and `createIdea` refuses in both states with a plain sentence, so the guard holds even if the button is reached some other way; Recipes, Meals and Kitchen are unaffected.
- **Not cascaded.** Deleting a recipe never touches the ideas that name it (they read as not made), deleting an idea never touches its recipe, and a recipe made from an idea keeps no pointer back.

### 5c.2. Cooking mode

- **No data of its own.** The screen reads `selectRecipesById` and `selectIngredientsById`, which the app already keeps (and, in `CookNotes`, the notes selectors of §5e), and listens to nothing new. Its writes are the cook Finish records (and its servings, a field-scoped `updateCook`), which belongs to the cooks collection (§5d), and the note typed there about that cook (§5e), neither of them cooking mode's own: with no cooks listener the panel isn't offered, and with no notes listener it has no note link.
- **Entry.** "Start cooking" is a row in the recipe drawer's ⋯ More ("Steps one at a time, with a timer"), only when the recipe has a step, and in the planned meal view, as its primary tonight (a row in its ⋯ More on a day ahead or while a due box is the primary), only on a day that has not passed, only when a recipe item has steps and only for a recipe not yet cooked for that meal (`getPlannedMealOffer`); the recipe drawer makes it the footer's primary instead when the recipe is on today's plan and not yet cooked there (§5d). Both close their drawer and call `openSubview({ kind: 'cook', … })` through `usePinchOverlay`. The planned meal works out its plate with `getPlateCookRecipes(items, recipesById, offer.cookRecipeIds)` (`utils/cooks.ts`, covered by `check:pinch-math`): each recipe with steps that isn't marked cooked for this meal yet, once, in plate order, its items' servings summed (capped at 1,000). One opens like a recipe (`recipeId`, `servings`, `plannedMealId`; cooking mode reads `servings` as what the plan has for tonight and starts at `getCookStartServings(recipe.servings, planned)`: the recipe's own, or the plan's when it is more); two or more pass `plate` too and open one session. There is no chooser. A recipe the day names but that has no steps, an ingredient item and an archived or deleted recipe are not offered. `Pinch.tsx` renders `CookingSubview` keyed on the recipe, the planned meal and the plate's recipe ids, and keeps the tabs mounted and visible underneath (an overlay, like the recipe form), so closing returns to the same list position.
- **State, all local to `CookingSession`.** `view: 'steps' | 'ingredients'` (shared by the session; the "Ingredients" button beside the step counter sets it, and on the ingredient list the subview's one `onBack` (header back, Escape, back gesture) sets it back before anything leaves), `currentId` (the recipe on screen) and `plate: Record<recipeId, PlateCookState>` (`getPlateStart`: each recipe's `stepIndex`, `servings` (`getCookStartServings` in `utils/cooks.ts`: the recipe's own, 1 when that isn't above zero, or the plan's count when it is more, capped at 1,000; the steppers map each tap through `stepCookServings`: whole servings, `MIN_COOK_SERVINGS` (0.5) only below one; both are checked by `check:pinch-math`) and `gotOut: Record<lineId, boolean>`) and the timers (with which one is in focus, kept by `useCookTimers`) sit side by side in the session (one recipe is the one-entry case), not in the view that shows them: the ingredients view and the steps view unmount when the person switches, so state held there would be lost (the ticks were, before this moved up). A tick re-renders the session, which is cheap because the rows are `memo` components with plain-value props and a stable `onToggle`. The index is clamped at render (`Math.min(stepIndex, lastIndex)`), so a recipe edited on another device while open can't leave it past the end; a recipe that loses its steps says "This recipe has no steps yet." and disables Next. `isFinished`, `isMarking`, a `markError` line and `cookedIds: Record<recipeId, cookId>` (the cooks Finish wrote; the panel trusts them at once, so a transaction whose snapshot lags never flickers, and only an Undo whose local delete has landed reads one as gone; per-recipe records (written, already marked, history full) are one `useMemo` over the store's cooks), `undoState` ('PENDING' | 'FAILED' | null) and a `finishedAt` ref (Done ignores a tap within `DONE_GUARD_MS`, 600 ms, of Finish) sit beside them, and `useCookNote` keeps the one note box open on that panel for one recipe at a time (`openRecipeId`, `text`, `isSaving`, `error`, the recipes with a saved note); nothing is kept when the screen closes.
- **A recipe that changes while the screen is open.** `CookingSubview` keeps the last recipe it saw: the store's `selectRecipesById` entry while there is one, and a snapshot that a `store.subscribe` callback updates (state is set from that callback, not during render or in an effect body, and a ref can't be read in render) for when the document goes. An edit shows at once; an archive (the selector includes archived recipes), a delete or a changed `updatedAt` adds one muted `role="status"` line, "This recipe was changed or removed elsewhere."; the session, its timers and its wake lock are never remounted. The "isn't in your kitchen anymore" line is only for a screen opened on a recipe that was never there.
- **Leaving.** While the timer view is up, the header's back, Escape and the back gesture put the timer in the background and show the step that was on screen (`backToStep`; the timer keeps running in the pill row), one layer per press. Otherwise the header's back, Escape (`Subview`'s capture-phase handler, which ignores a dialog on top) and the back gesture (`useSubviewHistory`, which restores its history entry before calling `onBack`) all call the same `leave()`, as does the final panel's Done (and "Apply to the recipe" on an open note, which then opens the recipe form): it first asks "Discard this note?" (Keep editing / Discard, destructive) while a note is typed and unsaved (`hasDraft`: it counts only while the cook is in the store and the note isn't saving, so Back during a save leaves and lets the write finish, and after an Undo the hidden box is not asked about; the note's own Cancel asks the same question), then, with no live timer, it calls `closeIfStillOpen()` (`closeSubviewIf` for this recipe's cooking screen, like every close after an await); with one or more running or paused (`liveCount`) it awaits `useActionModal().confirm({ title: 'Stop timers and leave?', confirmText: 'Leave', cancelText: 'Stay', destructive: true })` whose message is a small `LeaveMessage` reading the live count from a tiny store the session feeds (`useSyncExternalStore`, so a timer that finishes behind the open dialog stops being "still going" in its text: "Your timer has finished. Leaving closes cooking mode."), and every confirm in the screen goes through `ask`, which puts keyboard focus on the footer's primary when the dialog closes if a focus move was skipped while it was open. A finished timer and a dismissed one don't count. One Escape or one back gesture over the confirm closes only the confirm (the existing dialog-on-top rule), and Stay is the focused button. Checked from each of the four with a running timer, a paused one, only a finished one and none. The page layer is part of the same history stack as every drawer (see "The back gesture, one layer at a time").
- **Steps view.** `CookStepView` shows "Step n of m" with a thin progress bar (`flex-1`, 24 to 128px, so the row never wraps at 320px whatever the step count) and, at the row's end, the `action` the session passes (the "Ingredients" button, none from `lg`), then the step inside one polite, atomic live region that carries a visually hidden "Step n of m." before the text (the visible counter row is `aria-hidden`, so the button's label isn't read with every step; a screen reader says "Step 3 of 14" and the step after Next, an arrow key or a swipe, and focus stays on Next so Enter can press it again). The step is 28px (`text-[1.75rem]`, 30px from `lg`), 24px over 160 characters and 20px over 400, a size smaller on a screen under 640px tall (so a 568 or 560px phone fits the step and its card above the pinned stack), `font-medium` with `whitespace-pre-wrap`; `getStepType` returns that class and a ready card's digits' class from one ramp, the digits a size below the step (24/20/18px, never larger than the words); under it come the step's ready timer cards (24px gap, 16px under 640px tall) and then the "For this step" block (24px under a card, 32px straight under the step). A plate's recipes are `CookDishSwitch` above everything (a `role="group"` of `aria-pressed` `Button`s; the dish on screen has no status line because its counter is just below, the others read "Step n of m" from `plate`; three or more are 42% wide in a sideways scroller with snap points that scrolls the dish on screen into view, never a `Pill` or `PillGroup`; a name is `line-clamp-2`, so it wraps before it is cut). The header is `Subview`'s with `isTitleWrapped` (an opt-in `line-clamp-2` in place of `truncate`); a plate's title is the planned meal's own name when it has one, else `getPlateTitle` (`utils/mealFormat.ts`, the one helper that words a plate everywhere, `check:pinch-math`) over the session's recipes, never an ingredient item: "A & B", "A, B and C", "A, B and 2 more", and "A & 3 more" once a name runs past 30 characters, the count glued to its words with non-breaking spaces. Back and Next are 56px tall in a footer pinned by `Subview overlay`'s `.form-footer` rule (44px on a short landscape screen), with the pill row of timers running in the background above them (one row with them under 500px tall); the one scroller is the overlay layer, so a long step scrolls between the sticky header and footer, and a new step scrolls it back to the top. Nothing scrolls the step on its own: the dish cards, the note line and the counter row with Ingredients are the screen's controls and stay where the content starts (an earlier layout effect scrolled a timer card above the footer, and on a plate at 320×568 or held sideways that took the dish switch and Ingredients under the sticky header, so it was removed); a timer card that doesn't fit above the pinned buttons on a short phone is reached by scrolling the one scroller, and `document.elementFromPoint` at the centre of each dish card, the note line, the counter row and Ingredients returns that control at 320×568, 390×560, 375×667, 390×844 and 844×390 on a plate's timed step (with a timer in the pill row) and on a 414-character step; Start, Back and Next were checked the same way, with Start reached by scrolling to the end where it sits under the footer. Swipe is the shared `SwipeArea` (`src/components`, no app words) on the step area (touch and pen only, at least 60px sideways and 1.5 times the vertical travel, with `touch-action: pan-y pinch-zoom`, so vertical scrolling is the browser's and pinch-zoom still works on the text; only the first finger counts, and a swipe is ignored while `visualViewport.scale` is above 1.05, so zooming a step never turns the page); ArrowLeft and ArrowRight work in the steps view unless a field has focus, a modifier is down or a dialog is open. Cooking a plate, the wide button follows `getPlateNext` (`utils/cooks.ts`, `check:pinch-math`): the next step, then "Next: {recipe}" for the next recipe still short of its last step (wrapping), and Finish only when every recipe is on its last; a recipe whose steps were all removed counts as done.
- **Amounts on the step.** `matchStepIngredients(steps, lines)` (`utils/stepIngredients.ts`, pure, checked by `check:pinch-math`) runs once per recipe in a `useMemo` keyed on the steps, the lines and `selectIngredientsById` (about 2.5 ms for 40 lines and 25 steps unthrottled). Each step lists the lines whose ingredient it is the first to name (an amount is measured once): names and catalog aliases are normalized like `normalizeName` with describing words dropped, whole phrases are matched longest first on word positions not yet claimed (so "garlic powder" never also reads as "garlic"), then the first or last word of an ingredient's own name when only one ingredient in the recipe has it and it isn't a vague word (colours, "sauce", "powder", "and", "half"…), never a word from inside an alias. Every line of a matched ingredient is listed, in mention order, except that an ingredient with lines in several groups is shared out: each step that names it takes the next group's lines (in the order the groups show) and the last step that names it takes what is left, so "marinate in the soy sauce" lists the soy sauce for the chicken and "add the rest of the soy sauce" the soy sauce for the sauce, each labelled with its group. `hasWrittenAmount` is a number followed by a measure word ("2 cups", "350g"), and only on a scaled step does it show "Amounts written in this step stay as they are." It is a `bg-muted/50` block with a hairline and a "For this step" caption, rows in 14px (the step above is 28px), so the amounts read as a reference under the instruction rather than a second subject. From `lg` the step's list is hidden and the side column tints the same lines instead (one fact, one place).
- **Ingredients view.** `CookIngredientsView` scales each recipe line with `scaleAmount(amount, servings, recipe.servings)` (`utils/quantity.ts`), the same expression `getRecipeLines` uses for the shopping list, so a line reads identically in both (checked at 6, 2.5 and 0.5 servings against the shopping sheet), and formats it with `formatKitchenAmount` (`utils/kitchenMeasure.ts`, display only, checked by `check:pinch-math`): ¼ ⅓ ½ ⅔ ¾ (⅛ for teaspoons), a cup under ¼ (or one that is not within 5 % of a kitchen fraction, up to ½ cup) in tablespoons, a tablespoon under 1 in teaspoons, a pound under ¼ in ounces, under 0.1 kg or l in g or ml, whole items rounded to a whole (from 3) or a half with "about", "a pinch" for a sliver of a teaspoon, and "about" whenever it rounded by more than 5 %. The amount itself and `scaleAmount` are unchanged, so the shopping list's numbers are not affected, and the rows are built once in the session and shared with the step lists. Units are only ever stepped down for display, and a recipe whose servings aren't above zero is left unscaled instead of dividing by zero (the shopping list leaves such a recipe out). Rows are `memo` components with plain-value props, so a tick re-renders its own row; the ticks come in as a prop from the session. A line that names an ingredient that is gone reads "Removed ingredient". A line with no amount shows its note where the amount goes, in muted text, and never scales. A recipe with groups lists its lines under the same `SectionDivider` headings as the drawer, through `groupIngredientLines`; the "N of M got out" count is the whole recipe.
- **Timers.** `useCookTimers` (`hooks/useCookTimers.ts`) holds timers as a discriminated union (`running` with `endsAt` and `startedAt`, `paused` with `remainingMs`, `done` with `endedAt`, when it rang), each with the `durationMs` it was started with (for the progress bar), in state mirrored by a ref that every action reads and writes, so a callback never works from a stale list. Only `running` and `paused` timers are live and count toward `MAX_COOK_TIMERS` (4); a finished one waits for Dismiss, and starting a timer drops the oldest finished ones beyond four so the list can't grow without end. A countdown runs to a wall-clock deadline and `settle` (mark each ended timer done once with its `endedAt`, ranked by `getEndedAts` from its deadline and kept past any Time's up already waiting so a stepped-back clock can't reorder them, bring the view up and raise the alerts) runs from three places: the nearest `setTimeout` (the deadline plus 20 ms), which re-arms for what is left if it wakes before the deadline (the clock stepped back, so it can't leave a timer at 0:00 unalerted); `useOnTabReturn`, so a timer that ended while the tab was hidden shows done the moment the page returns; and the 250 ms tick of the pill row and of the timer view, which catches a deadline a forward clock step jumped over. **A Time's up that nobody dismisses rings again**: `ringsRef` holds `{ id, lastAt, repeats }` per waiting timer, and a timeout armed from `getNextAlertRepeatAt` (a minute after the soonest `lastAt`, `ALERT_REPEAT_MS`; re-armed after each repeat and when the set of waiting timers changes, cleared on Dismiss, `clear` and unmount) buzzes and beeps through `getAlertRepeatsDue` up to `MAX_ALERT_REPEATS` (5) more times per timer, skipping the sound (not the count) while `hasDialogOnTop()`, with no toast for a repeat; the timer view says "Rang just now" / "Rang 2 min ago" from `endedAt` (`getRangAgo`), re-rendered by a timeout aimed at the next minute. `dismiss` of a finished timer adds its slot (`getTimerSlotKey`: recipe, step, length) to `finishedSlots` (state, memory only, returned as a `Set`), cleared when a timer starts in that slot, so the step view's card reads "Done · 30 min" with a quiet "Start again" (`CookStepTimer`'s `isDone`) instead of offering the time as if it never ran. The alerts are the timer view's "Time's up", `navigator.vibrate` when it exists, three short beeps through one `AudioContext` created and resumed in the tap that started the first timer and resumed again before the beeps (a browser can suspend it in the background), each in its own `try` that logs and carries on, and a toast ("Timer done", "Simmer everything · 25 min", 10 seconds) only while `document.visibilityState` is hidden or `hasDialogOnTop()`; each timer carries `stepName` (`getStepName`, `utils/cooks.ts`: the step's first three words without a trailing little word), which names it in place of "Step 3". **Focus is one value in the hook:** `focus = { isShown, pickedId }`, and `focused` (the `CookTimer` the timer view shows, null while every timer runs in the background) is derived each render through `getFocusedTimerId(timers, pickedId)` (`utils/cooks.ts`, `check:pinch-math`): the Time's up that has waited longest first (the smallest `endedAt`, so a later finish queues behind the one in front whatever started first, and a pick that finishes later doesn't take the front), then the one picked (started or tapped), which keeps focus while paused, else the running one ending soonest. `start` picks the new timer and shows the view (and returns its id, or null at the cap), `focus(id)` picks one, `unfocus()` hides the view (the timers keep running), `settle` shows the view over whatever is on screen (`getFocusAfterFinish`: it keeps the pick while the view was up, so Dismiss returns to the timer the person was watching, and forgets one already in the background), and `dismiss(id)` (Reset when paused, Dismiss when done; `getFocusAfterDismiss`) shows the next finished timer, else the one still picked (watched, or just started while a finished one waited), else hides the view, and ending a timer from its row changes nothing; `pause` and `resume` change nothing about focus. Both transitions are pure and in `check:pinch-math`. The session hides the view while the Finish panel holds a typed note or open put-away details (a finish then waits, lit in the pill row, until the draft closes), and an effect keyed on the focused timer and whether it is done moves keyboard focus to the footer's primary (Pause, Resume or Dismiss; back to Next when the view closes), skips the move while a dialog is on top (the dialog keeps focus; `ask` restores it when it closes), scrolls the overlay to the top when the view appears and starts the 0.6 s tap guard (`DONE_GUARD_MS`, the constant the Finish panel's Done uses) that makes Pause, Resume or Dismiss, and a Time's up's Next, ignore a tap right after the view or a Time's up appears (a ref, not state: the button looks the same). The voice PR reads `focused` for "how much time is left" (`getRemainingMs(focused, Date.now())`) and calls `pause`, `resume`, `dismiss` and `start` with its id, and `focus(id)` to show one. The step on screen shows a `CookStepTimer` card only for a time it mentions that has no timer yet: `getReadyStepTimers(timers, shown)` (`utils/cooks.ts`, `check:pinch-math`) takes the shown step's `findDurations` (the first `MAX_STEP_TIMERS`, 4, as many as can run at once) and keeps those with no timer of the same recipe, step number and label (`formatDuration`); `shown` is null on the ingredient list and the Finish panel. A card is only ever ready: "⏲️ Timer", the length and a filled Start; Start hands the screen to the timer view, so a started time is never also a card (one fact, one home). Cards are keyed by step number and label, and the step view by recipe. Nothing starts a timer but a tap. With four live timers a ready card's Start is disabled and "4 timers are going. Another can start once one finishes or is reset." shows under the cards (four finished timers leave Start enabled), in an always-mounted `role="status"` wrapper that is `empty:hidden`. **The pill row** (`CookTimerStrip`) is what the step view, the ingredient list and the Finish panel show of the timers, in `getTimerRows(timers, null)` order (finished first, then in the order they started, so nothing moves under a finger): one 40px row of pills, each one button with only the time left (`formatClock`, tabular) and the step's first words (its length before them when two come from one step, so a cut name keeps what tells them apart), muted digits and "Paused ·" while paused (⏸ is the Pause action, never a state), a bell, "Time's up" and the accent tint once done; a tap brings that timer into focus. A pill is as wide as its words (at most 18rem, never shrunk to a fragment) and the row scrolls sideways when they don't all fit. The row's inner component mounts only while there are timers, so its `now` is read afresh on every return from the timer view (a tick-driven value seeded at first mount showed each timer's full length for a tick). The pills are plain text while the Finish panel holds a draft. There is no pill row while the timer view is up. Leaving asks first while a timer is live (see Leaving) and then ends every timer (the hook's state and its pending timeout go with the component). Each timer carries `recipeId` and, on a plate, `recipeTitle`; `getTimerCaption` names it ("Adobo chicken · Marinate the chicken", the step alone for one recipe) in aria labels and the hidden-screen toast, and `countLiveTimers` / `canStartTimer` hold the cap of four for the whole session.
- **The timer view** (`CookTimerView`, keyed by the focused timer so "Show this step" starts closed for each). While a timer is in focus it replaces the step (and the dish cards, the note line, the "changed elsewhere" line, the ingredient list or the Finish panel) inside the same overlay: a waiting screen that shows only what helps while waiting. From the top: a plain muted line "Step 2 of 4 · Show this step" with a chevron (40px, `aria-expanded`) that opens the timer's step in place (20px text and its "For this step" block, `CookStepAmounts`, shared with the step view; the amounts are listed from `lg` too, since there is no side column); the hero card (`@container`, `rounded-3xl`): on a plate the timer's recipe in muted text, the name ("Marinate the chicken · 30 min"), the countdown in `font-medium tracking-tight tabular-nums` at `clamp(4.5rem, 28cqi, 9rem)` (72px at 320, 88px at 390, 144px from `lg`; an hour or more at `clamp(4rem, 22cqi, 7rem)`, so "1:30:00" is 64px and 246px wide in a 248px card at 320; a bold weight would not fit), a progress bar (elapsed over `durationMs`), under it one reserved 24px row (so pausing never grows the card: "Paused · ↺ Reset" while paused, the Reset a 40px target by pseudo-element, "Rang 2 min ago" when done, empty while it runs) and a `role="status"` "The 25 min timer is done."; "Show this step" opens the step under the card, never above it; running is the drizzle tint with an orange edge, paused the muted tint with a hairline and muted digits, done the accent tint with an ink edge, a bell and "Time's up" at `clamp(2.5rem, 17cqi, 5rem)` and a full bar. Under it, "Other timers": the session's other timers (`getTimerRows(timers, focusedId)`) as rows in one hairline card (the name, wrapping, leading with the length when two share a step; the recipe on a plate; the time left; the row is one button that brings that timer into focus, and its own 40px Pause or Resume sits 12px to the right), or, while a Time's up is in front, plain text rows with no control (a tap on a row used to do nothing, because a finished timer outranks the pick); then, not at the cap and not over a Time's up, the timer's step's other times with no timer yet as one muted disclosure line ("2 more timers on this step · Show") that opens rows of length and Start ("Start again" for a slot in `finishedSlots`), so two ready rows never push "Up next" under the footer at 320×568. Then "Up next" (`getTimerNext(positions, timer)`, `check:pinch-math`: the step after the timer's step while the person is before it; nothing once they are on it or past it; on a recipe's last step the next recipe still short of its last; nothing on the last step of all or on the Finish panel) with the first sentence of that step (`getFirstSentence`: up to a stop, question or exclamation mark before a capital or digit, or a line break; six lines at most, three under 640px tall). On a plate, while the timer isn't finished and `getMeanwhile` finds another recipe still short of its last step that "Up next" doesn't already name, one tertiary row, "Meanwhile: {recipe} · Step 1 of 2" with a chevron, calls `jumpTo` on that recipe's own step. Last, while the timer runs, the keep-the-screen-open line (below). On a phone held upright the hero and its rows sit a third of the way down the free space and "Up next" just above its button; under 500px tall it is a two-column grid, the hero (and the line) on the left and the step line, the rows and "Up next" on the right; from `lg` it is one centered `max-w-xl` column. The footer is the timer's: a secondary chosen by `getTimerSecondary(next, status)` — "Next" (the step footer's word, so one act has one name; "Next recipe" with `aria-label` "Next recipe: Cilantro lime rice" on a plate's last step) while there is something up next, which puts the timer in the background and shows that step (`jumpTo(recipeId, stepIndex)`; from "Time's up" it also dismisses the finished timer, ignored for 0.6 s after the Time's up appears); "Back to step 3" (`aria-label` naming the recipe on a plate; "Back to ingredients" or "Back" over those screens) for a running or paused timer with nothing up next, which is a peek from a pill while the person is further on; and nothing for a Time's up over a later step, so no two buttons end at the same screen; and one wide primary: Pause while it runs (the only control a running timer has), Resume while paused, Dismiss when done (`aria-label` "Pause the 30 min timer" and so on). ArrowRight or a swipe to the left does what Next does; ArrowLeft or a swipe to the right goes back to the step, like the header's back. Checked with `document.elementFromPoint` at the centre of Pause, Resume, Reset, Dismiss, Next, "Show this step" and every row's control, running, paused, with the step shown, two on one step, an hour long, four live (at the cap), "Time's up" with three others and on the last step, at 320×568, 390×560, 375×667, 390×844, 844×390 and 1280×900, and on a plate. From `lg` the footer's content is capped to the same `max-w-xl` column as the timer (only while the view is up and taller than 500px), so Pause is never a bar wider than the column.
- **Finding the times.** `findDurations` (`src/utils/durationUtils.ts`, no recipe words) reads a text for lengths of time: a number (integer, decimal, `1/2`, `1 1/2`, `½ ¼ ¾` alone or after a whole number) then hours, minutes or seconds in their written forms (`hour`, `hr`, `min`, `sec` and plurals), "an hour" and "half an hour"; a range (`10-15`, `10–15`, `2 to 3`, `1 or 2`) takes its longer end; "1 hr 30 min", "1 hour and 15 minutes" and "2 hours, 30 minutes" join into one length when only a comma or "and" sits between them and the units step down; repeats are dropped, and zero, anything over 24 hours, anything past the first 5,000 characters and any number written with a decimal comma or thousands commas ("1,5 hours" could be 1.5 or 15, "2,500 seconds" 2,500 or 500; a wrong timer is worse than none) is left out. The parser is linear (`joinPieces` builds one array in a single pass) and `findDurations` takes an optional `maxChars` (5,000 by default) so a check can lift the cap. Words like "tablespoons", "minced" and "second batch" without a number in front are not times. It returns `{ seconds, text }`; `formatDuration` ("25 min", "1 h 30 min") labels the chip and `formatClock` ("25:00") the countdown, rounding up so it reads 0:00 only at the deadline. Its cases, including the comma rule, the 5,000-character boundary (a text just under and just over, which fails if the cap is removed) and benchmarks with the cap raised (250,000 repeats of "5 min " and other hostile bodies, each under a second), are in `scripts/checks/durations.ts`, run by `npm run check:pinch-math` with the shopping math (which also covers `scaleAmount`). English only.
- **Keeping the screen on.** `useWakeLock(isActive)` (`src/hooks/useWakeLock.ts`, general) returns `{ isSupported, isHeld, hasFailed }`: it requests `navigator.wakeLock.request('screen')` while active, asks again each time `useOnTabReturn` fires (a browser lets go of the lock when the page is hidden, which also clears `isHeld` through the sentinel's `release` event), releases on unmount, and logs a refusal or a failed release instead of throwing. It mirrors the sentinel carefully: a sentinel whose `released` is true counts as absent, so the next return asks again even if its `release` event hasn't arrived; a `release` event changes `isHeld` only when it comes from the sentinel the hook currently holds, so a late one from an old sentinel can't clear a newer lock; and a request that resolves after the person left is released with its own `catch`, so a refused release is logged and never sets `hasFailed`. Cooking mode shows "This device may turn the screen off; timers keep running only while this screen is open." (one sentence that also says what to do about timers, instead of a second line beside theirs) at the foot of the timer view while its timer runs or is paused (never over a Time's up, so pausing never moves the digits), only when `!isSupported || hasFailed`; when the lock works it shows "Keep this screen open: timers stop if you leave it." there instead, from the first timer until every timer is gone; the step view, the pill row and the footer never carry either. Wake Lock needs a secure context, so a plain-HTTP LAN page shows the line. It was checked with a stub (one request on open, none before, released when hidden, requested again on return, released on close, a refusal shows the line, a late `release` event from an old sentinel, a released sentinel without its event, and a refused release after leaving, each of which fails on the earlier hook); headless Chromium refuses the real call, which showed the line.
- **Limits.** Timers end when the screen closes and don't ring in the background or on a locked phone. Vibration is Android only and needs a recent tap. Sound is prepared in the tap that starts a timer, because a browser won't start audio on its own later. A step's text is read only to list the ingredients it names, never to rewrite it, so amounts written in it don't scale (a scaled step that has one says so).
- **Wide screens.** From `lg` (`useMediaQuery().isAtLeast('lg')`) the overlay widens to `max-w-5xl` and draws the step (with the note line and the ready timer cards) and `CookIngredientsView` side by side, no Ingredients button, with the dish cards across the top (`sm:max-w-72` each); the footer is unchanged, the Finish panel stays one centered `max-w-md` column and the timer view one centered `max-w-xl` column, with no ingredient list.

- **Voice commands.** Three layers, so the lifecycle and the grammar can serve another app. (1) `useSpeechCommands({ lang, speechLang, onFinalTranscript, idleStopMs, onHalt })` (`src/hooks`, no app words) returns `{ isSupported, status: 'off' | 'starting' | 'listening' | 'speaking', isOn, error, start, stop, say, quiet }`. `isSupported` is `window.isSecureContext && (SpeechRecognition ?? webkitSpeechRecognition)`; where it is false the caller draws nothing. The machine is `createSpeechListener(setStatus, setError)`, a plain object made once in a `useState` initializer (no React refs, so the compiler's render checks have nothing to flag) and driven without React by `scripts/checks/speechListener.ts` (part of `npm run check:pinch-voice`) against a fake `window`, recognizer and voice. Its one recognition object is made on the first `start` (a tap), never in an effect, so Strict Mode's double mount creates none; unmount (and Strict Mode's fake one) detaches the four handlers and calls `abort()`. `start` also speaks one silent, empty utterance, since some browsers (Safari) only speak later once a tap has. It runs `continuous`, `interimResults: false` and three alternatives, and hands each final result's alternatives, best first, to `onFinalTranscript` once (a per-session set of handled result indexes); the callback returns whether it used the phrase, and `idleStopMs` after the start or the last used phrase it stops with `error: 'idle'` (Pinch passes none: its time limits belong to the listening states below). `onHalt(error)` runs each time it stops by itself, for any of the errors below or idle, and never for a `stop` the caller asked for or a teardown, so the caller's own state follows the listener's instead of mirroring it in an effect. On `end` it restarts while the person has it on: a session that heard something, or lasted 1.5 s, restarts after 250 ms and resets the failure count; a quicker end with nothing heard (or after a `network` or other error) counts as a failure and waits 500 ms, 1, 2, then 4 s; five in a row stop it with `error: 'unavailable'`. `no-speech` and `aborted` are normal. `not-allowed` stops it at once with `'denied'`, `service-not-allowed` (Safari with Siri and Dictation off) with `'service'`, `audio-capture` with `'no-microphone'`, `language-not-supported` with `'language'`; none of them retries, so a refused microphone is never asked for in a loop. `start()` after an `abort()` whose `end` hasn't arrived (Chrome throws `InvalidStateError`) waits for that `end`. `say(text)` aborts the session, reads through `speakText` and starts listening again only when the reading's own `end` arrives (a token drops a cancelled reading's `end`), so the app never hears itself; `quiet()` cuts a reading short; a reading whose `start` hasn't come within 1.5 s is given up at once and nothing more is read for the session, so a browser with no working voice never leaves the mic deaf for the length guard. A hidden page (`visibilitychange`) aborts and cancels the reading; `useOnTabReturn` starts it again only if the person still had it on, so a microphone the browser stopped is never re-granted silently. State is set only in recognition, speech, timer and visibility callbacks and in the tap handlers, never in an effect body. (2) `speakText(text, { lang, onStart, onEnd, startTimeoutMs, onNoStart })`, `pickVoice` and `primeSpeech` (`src/utils/speechUtils.ts`): cancels what was being read, picks a voice for the language (exact, then the same language, preferring one on the device; where only a network voice exists the text goes to its vendor, which the explainer says), keeps the utterance referenced until it ends (Chrome otherwise drops its `end`), and calls `onEnd` once on end, error, cancel, no start in time or a guard of 4 s plus 90 ms a character (60 s at most), since some browsers never send `end`. (3) The grammar is `readCookCommand(alternatives, context)` (and `parseCookCommand`, its command alone; `utils/cookVoice.ts`, pure, `npm run check:pinch-voice`): each guess is lower-cased, accents and punctuation removed, a leading "okay", "hey", "pinch", "please", "can you" and a trailing "please", "thanks" or "now" dropped, and only a guess that is a whole command counts (more than 80 characters never is). The recognizer's top guess is tried first; a lower-ranked one counts only as a phrase of two or more words that isn't a move (an amount, a dish, a timer with "timer" in it, "stop listening"), so "black" heard as "back" or "text" as "next" never acts, and the line names the guess that did. Plain commands are exact phrase lists holding only what "What to say" shows and plain synonyms of it (next, "go to the next step", back, previous, repeat, "read the step", ingredients, steps, how much time is left, "timer check", stop listening, next or previous recipe, and finish, done, "I'm done", "mark as cooked", which are refused); everyday words said to people too ("go on", "keep going", "continue", "again", "what was that", "how long", "the other dish") are not commands. Timers are one pattern (start, set, pause, hold, resume, unpause, continue, stop, dismiss, silence, cancel, reset, clear, turn off… then an optional "the" and length and "timer" or "alarm"): "start" needs "timer", a bare "pause", "resume", "unpause", "stop", "dismiss" or "silence" is allowed, a bare stop or "the alarm" only silences a ringing timer (`scope: 'RINGING'`), and a length is read from words or digits ("twenty five", "an hour and a half", "half an hour") through `findDurations` and must be nothing but a length, so "start the oven" and "pause for effect" are not commands. "How much X" (or "how many") matches X against the lines of the recipe in front, every name and alias, by whole words after a rough singular ("tomatoes", "bay leaves"), looking past a leading quantity and "of" when the whole query finds nothing ("cups of rice", "tablespoons of vinegar", "cloves of garlic"; "cream of tartar" stays whole): an exact name beats a longer one ("garlic" is not "garlic powder"), the step's own lines win when the step names one (otherwise every line of that name), at most three come back, and a query of more than four ingredient words or with words like "do", "you", "time" or "cost" is not a command; X with no match is still the question, answered with the words searched ("I can't find “cups of cilantro” in this recipe's ingredients."). "Switch to / go to / show me X" (nothing else: "cook the chicken" and "open rice" are not commands) matches the plate's titles by words: an exact title wins, otherwise every title holding the words is a candidate, one other than the dish in front is chosen, and several ask which; no match is not a command. (4) `useCookVoice({ uid, step, onTurnOn })` (`hooks/useCookVoice.ts`) wires them: the explainer's Turn on (`pinch:{uid}:voice:agreed`) and Read steps aloud (`pinch:{uid}:voice:read-aloud`) through `useLocalStoragePreference` (try/catch); `onTurnOn` runs inside the tap that starts listening (the screen passes the timers' `prepareSound`, so a Time's up started by voice can still beep in Safari); the message line (2.5 s for what it heard, 8 s for an answer, 5 s for a refusal; a phrase that is no command clears it back to the hint and is never echoed, except in an open command window, which quotes it for 3.5 s as "Not a command: “can you pass the salt”" and does nothing); the stop line (8 s for each stop, then nothing, since the unlit mic carries the state; a tap on the mic after any stop but a quiet-time or lock one reopens the explainer with "Last time:" and the whole cause); the listening states, in the next bullet, which replace the old fifteen-minute idle stop; the 1.2 s guard against the same command twice; ignoring everything but "stop listening" while `hasDialogOnTop()`; and one effect keyed on the shown step that reads it aloud while listening with Read steps aloud on, once per showing (listening coming back after a hidden tab or a reading doesn't read it again), and otherwise calls `quiet()`, so a step change cancels any reading. Recognition listens in `navigator.language` when it is English and in `en-US` otherwise (the grammar is English); readings use the page's language. `CookingSession` hands it one function, an effect event (`useEffectEvent`) that builds the actions and runs the command (`runCookCommand`), through `connect` in an effect, so no action closure leaves the screen; with the actions connected the compiler's purity check flags every `Date.now()` read in the screen, none of which runs during render (the Finish path's `handleNext`, `writeCooks` and `handleDone` run from a tap, and the timer view's tap guards are set in an effect), so those four lines carry a `react-hooks/purity` disable with that reason (tried: building the actions in render as a plain object, handing them to a child component or connecting them from an effect flags the same lines; it takes the start-timer action together with any action that changes the view). The voice path reads its own clock in utils (`withTimeLeft`, `isBeforeDeadline`). The actions do what a tap on the screen in view does: `next` (`goBy(1)`, or the next dish with "Showing Cilantro lime rice"; it never calls `handleNext`, so it can't open the Finish panel that records the cook; on the ingredient list, whose only button is "Back to the steps", it refuses with "Say “steps” to go back to the steps."; in the timer view it follows the footer's secondary (`getTimerSecondary`): `NEXT` is `goNextFromTimer`, the footer's Next or Next recipe, with the timer left running, `BACK` is `backToStep()` and shows "Back to step 3", and with none (a Time's up over a later step, where the footer has only Dismiss) it refuses with "Time's up: say “dismiss” first."), `back` (`goBy(-1)`; on the ingredient list it is "Back to the steps", `showView('steps')`, to the step the cook left; `backToStep()` from the timer view), `repeat` (reads "Adobo chicken. Step 2 of 3. …", in the timer view the step being waited on), `showIngredients`/`showSteps` (`showView`, after `backToStep()` from the timer view), and `finish`, which refuses: "Last step: tap Finish yourself." on the plate's last step ("That's the last step. Tap Finish when you're done." aloud) and "Finish is on the last step." before it. Voice calls the session's functions, never the footer's guarded handlers, and has its own guard sized to the recognizer's delay instead: for 2 s after a Time's up takes the front (`VOICE_DONE_GUARD_MS`, set with the 0.6 s tap guard in the focus effect), "next" and "stop"/"dismiss" on it are held back ("Just rang: say that again to go on."). Timers follow the timer view's API: "start the timer" takes the step in front's first time with no timer yet (`getReadyStepTimers`), or the time of the length said, and calls the session's `startTimer(recipe, stepNumber, seconds, label)`, which focuses the new timer, so the timer view takes the screen ("Started the 30 min timer"; aloud, the first start of a cook adds "Say next step to keep going", or "Say back to see the step" when `getTimerNext` finds nothing ahead of it); a running one of that length is brought to the front with `timers.focus`, a paused one resumed, a finished one dismissed and started again, and at the cap (`timers.isFull`, or a null id) it shows "4 timers are going already." and says "4 timers are going. Another can start once one finishes or is reset.". Pause, resume and stop pick with the pure `pickVoiceTimer` over the timers with their time left: the one in `focused` (the session's `timers.focused`, null while a typed note or a put-away is open on the Finish panel) when it fits, else the one that ends soonest (among Time's ups, the earliest `endedAt`), of the length said; while a Time's up shows, pause and resume refuse ("That timer is ringing. Say “dismiss”."), so voice never pauses a timer nobody can see. "Stop the timer" follows the footer's controls, since a running timer's only control is Pause: the Time's up in front is dismissed (`endTimer`; the focused one, else the earliest `endedAt`, or one of the length said), one per command like the footer's Dismiss, so a queued Time's up is never dismissed unseen, a running one is paused ("Paused. Say “stop” again to reset it."), a paused one is reset (`endTimer`); a bare "stop", "silence" or "dismiss" only dismisses a ringing one. "How much time is left" tells `pickTimersToTell` (pure): the focused timer (else the one with the earliest `endedAt`, else the soonest running, else a paused one) and then the soonest other running timer, through `describeTimersLeft` (two running timers show as "24:59 and 29:55 left", the pills naming them, and are named aloud). Amounts come from the scaled rows of the recipe in front (`getRows`; in the timer view, the timer's dish, and the grammar's context is that dish and step too), through `describeAmounts` (said through `toSpokenAmount`: "1 and a half tablespoons"; an ingredient in two groups gives each amount with its group), and a dish switch is `showRecipe`, or `jumpTo(recipeId, its step)` from the timer view. Each result is `{ ok: true, show?, speak? } | { ok: false, reason, spoken?, isSpoken? }`: an answer is always read aloud, and a refusal is read aloud with Read steps aloud on or when `isSpoken` (it answers a question or a ringing timer: not found, no timer, which dish, a Time's up, Finish); a refusal's shown text fits one line at 320px and its `spoken` form is the full sentence. Tapping Finish calls `voice.stop()`, and the mic isn't drawn on the final panel. The step that read-aloud follows is null while the timer view shows, so nothing is read over a timer, and the step is read again on the way back. The mic is the shared `Subview`'s `headerAction` (one screen-wide switch at the end of the title row, never a call to action; tinted with an edge and a pulse while it listens, and its icon filled while on), so it is on the steps, the ingredient list and the timer view; the line is the first child of the footer's stack, one line tall above the pill row and the buttons on a phone (above Pause and Next in the timer view), which adds 32px, and the first item of the footer's one row on a short landscape screen (`max-w-44`, sized to its text, two lines at most, so the footer doesn't grow there; while listening with nothing to say it is only the chip, so one timer pill fits beside it). It starts with the state chip, a 24px thin pill (a 40px target from a pseudo-element, `aria-label` "Voice options") that names what the mic is doing and opens the voice sheet: "Listening" with a pulsing dot and a bar that empties over the window's ten seconds (a CSS animation keyed on `windowKey`, so each command refills it; static under reduced motion), "Waiting" with a steady dot, the lock icon and the time left (`LockCountdown`, a leaf that redraws each second; the digits are `aria-hidden`), "Starting…" or "Reading"; the text after it is the hint, what it heard, an answer, a refusal, "Reading aloud… tap Next to skip" or, while the browser may be asking, "Allow the microphone if asked."; after a stop the line is only why, for 8 s. Every state is that one line, and the message truncates first. The two modals lead with what matters and fold the rest (`CookVoice.tsx`): the explainer shows two sentences (what to say; the browser asks for the microphone and its speech service hears it; Pinch keeps nothing), then two rows that ask how it should listen (Tap to talk, Say “Hey Pinch”), above a "How it works" line; and the voice sheet (a `Modal` with `useSheetHistory`, mounted by `CookVoiceLine` outside the line's own conditions so it survives the mic turning off under it, its open state in the hook) has two panes chosen with a thin `PillGroup`: "How it listens" (the three ways as rows, `aria-pressed`, one chosen, the lock row counting down; "Last time: …" after a stop that needs explaining; the Read steps aloud switch) and "What to say" (the five commands of a step, led by "hey pinch" in wake mode, above "More commands"). The sheet is 58% of a 390×844 screen. Each fold is a tertiary button with `aria-expanded` and a turning chevron, opens in place with one fact or phrase a line, adds no history layer, and is folded again whenever its modal opens (the explainer folds on every close path, "What to say" on its open); opened, the modal's overlay is the one scroller and the buttons stay pinned (`sticky`, with a hairline only while folded content is open); focus starts on Not now and on Done, after the modal's own focus lands on the fold. With no reveal scroll, what a short screen already leaves under the footer (a plate's step-2 Start behind the pill row, the timer view's Meanwhile line, at 320×568 and 390×560) needs 32px more scroll, and nothing visible at scroll 0 with voice off is covered by it. Nothing here touches Firestore, Storage, indexes or rules.
- **Voice: where it works.** Chrome and Edge on desktop and Android expose `webkitSpeechRecognition` and send audio to their vendor's service (Google for Chrome); Safari 14.1+ on macOS and iOS exposes it and uses Apple's (Siri or Dictation must be on, or it stops with `service-not-allowed` and the "speech service is off" line; continuous listening there ends sooner); Firefox has no recognition constructor, so no mic shows; Brave exposes the constructor but its service fails with `network`, which reads as five failed starts and "Voice control stopped: no answer."; any plain-HTTP page (the LAN address, share links) isn't a secure context, so no mic shows. `speechSynthesis` is in all of them; where it is missing, a reading ends at once and listening carries on. None of this was checked on a real device here (Open questions, item 24).
- **Voice: how the mic listens (`utils/cookVoiceMode.ts`, pure, `npm run check:pinch-voice`).** The person's choice is a **mode** (`TAP` or `WAKE`, remembered per person as `pinch:{uid}:voice:wake`), the mic's condition a **phase**: `VoiceState = { mode, phase: 'OFF' | 'WAITING' | 'WINDOW' | 'LOCKED', epoch, lockEndsAt, stopped }`, moved only by `reduceVoice(state, event)` (a step that changes nothing returns the same object) and carried out by `getVoiceEffect(previous, next)` (`START` when the mic opens, `STOP` when it closes). `useCookVoice` keeps one machine in a ref mirrored by state and a `send(event)` that every tap, recognizer callback and timer goes through, so a start or stop never depends on an effect. OFF has no recognition. WAITING exists only in wake mode: recognition runs and each final phrase is looked at only for the wake phrase. WINDOW takes commands for `COOK_WINDOW_MS` (10 s) counted from the mic really being open, restarted by each command or by the phrase, paused while a reading is spoken (the effect is keyed on `[phase, epoch, isListening]`), and ends in WAITING in wake mode, in OFF in tap mode. LOCKED takes commands with no phrase until `lockEndsAt`, `COOK_LOCK_MS` (15 min) after the tap, never extended; its expiry is a self-re-arming timeout aimed at the wall-clock deadline (so a timer that wakes early re-arms for what is left), settled again by `useOnTabReturn`, and its countdown is drawn by a leaf component and a CSS animation started from the time already elapsed, so the cooking screen does not re-render each second. Waiting turns itself off after `COOK_WAKE_IDLE_MS` (10 min) since it last entered WAITING, so each window that ends restarts the ten minutes; 15 minutes was the old limit and the owner asked for less continuous listening, and a simmer step rarely waits longer than ten minutes unattended. A tap steps down one level (a window to waiting, or off in tap mode; waiting and the lock to off); a tap on the mic from off opens a first window in either mode.

  | Event | OFF | WAITING | WINDOW | LOCKED |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | `TAP_MIC` | WINDOW (start) | OFF (stop) | WAITING (wake) or OFF (tap, stop) | OFF (stop) |
  | `WAKE_HEARD` (the phrase, alone or with a command) | ignored | WINDOW | WINDOW, restarted | ignored |
  | `COMMAND_HEARD` | ignored | ignored | WINDOW, restarted | ignored |
  | `WINDOW_ENDED` (10 s) | ignored | ignored | WAITING (wake) or OFF (tap, stop) | ignored |
  | `WAIT_ENDED` (10 quiet minutes) | ignored | OFF (stop), "wake-idle" | ignored | ignored |
  | `LOCK_ENDED` (15 min) | ignored | ignored | ignored | OFF (stop), "lock-ended" |
  | `STOP` ("stop listening", Finish, leaving) | ignored | OFF (stop) | OFF (stop) | OFF (stop) |
  | `ENGINE_STOPPED` (`onHalt`) | ignored | OFF (stop), the reason | OFF (stop), the reason | OFF (stop), the reason |
  | `CHOOSE TAP` (sheet row) | remembers tap mode | OFF (stop) | stays, ends in OFF | OFF (stop) |
  | `CHOOSE WAKE` | WAITING (start) | stays | stays, ends in WAITING | WAITING |
  | `CHOOSE LOCK` | LOCKED (start) | LOCKED | LOCKED | stays (the deadline is kept) |
  | `MODE` (first-use rows) | remembers the mode, starts nothing | as `CHOOSE` without a start | as `CHOOSE` | as `CHOOSE` |

  `check:pinch-voice` runs that whole table (every event from every state a person can be in, 91 cells, plus named scenarios with the effect each one asks for) and 200 random walks of 60 events that hold its invariants (waiting only in wake mode; `lockEndsAt` set exactly while LOCKED; a stop reason only while OFF; a start asked exactly when the mic opens and a stop exactly when it closes). The **wake phrase** is `readWakeWord(alternatives)`: each guess is put through the grammar's plain-text step, up to two leading fillers ("um", "okay", "well") are dropped, and it counts only when what is left starts with one of `COOK_WAKE_SPELLINGS` (`constants.ts`, the one place: "hey pinch", "hay pinch", "heypinch") followed by a space or nothing, and is not followed by "of" ("hey, pinch of salt"). So it is anchored at the start of the phrase: "add a pinch of salt", "a pinch", "pinch", "okay, pinch the edges", "hi pinch", "hey punch" and "I said hey pinch" never wake it (the checks read eleven recipe steps and each of their sentences aloud, every use of "pinch" among them, and mutations that add "a pinch", drop the "of" rule, unanchor the phrase, or stop a window from restarting each fail the check). Any ranked guess may carry the phrase (waking costs ten seconds of listening and nothing else), but what follows it keeps its rank, so a one-word command in a lower guess ("back" heard as "black") still never runs. What follows goes through the ordinary grammar in the same breath ("hey pinch next step" does the command and opens the window); the phrase alone opens it with nothing run. `planHeard(phase, alternatives, context)` is the one pure place that says what a finished phrase means in each phase (`IGNORE`; `CLEAR`, a non-command while kept open; `COMMAND`; `WAKE` with the command after it or none; `MISSED`, talk in an open window to quote back), checked for 26 phase and phrase cases, and `useCookVoice` only acts on its answer. Waiting ignores the phrase while a dialog is on top, and "stop listening" says the whole thing off from any phase and with a dialog open, "hey pinch, stop listening" included.
- **Voice: what the wake phrase cannot do.** (a) Waiting is continuous recognition: the browser's mic indicator is on and, in Chrome, audio streams to Google for as long as it waits, which the first-use rows and How it works say in plain words; nothing is detected on the device. (b) A recognizer session ends by itself after silence or about a minute (Chrome) and sooner in Safari: `useSpeechCommands` restarts it after 250 ms with no visible change, so a phrase spoken in that gap is missed. (c) A phrase is final only after a pause (about a second), so "Hey Pinch" shows its window about a second after it is said. (d) It listens in the page's English; a mishearing that is not one of the spellings ("hey punch") does nothing and the person says it again or taps. (e) Screens that turn off, backgrounded tabs and locked phones end recognition: the page's wake lock (`useWakeLock`) keeps a cooking screen on, a hidden page aborts and `useOnTabReturn` restarts it only if the mic was on, and a phone that locks anyway loses it until the page is back (the deadline of a lock keeps running). (f) A ringing timer can drown the person out; the Time's up is never delayed or quieted by voice (nothing in this code plays a sound; it only reads text aloud when asked), and "dismiss" within 2 s of it ringing is held back as before. (g) A noisy kitchen misrecognizes: the window quotes what it heard as a non-command so the person sees it, and every command is one tap on the screen. (h) Nothing here is sent anywhere by Pinch's own code; no data model, rule, index or Function changed.

### 5d. Marking a recipe cooked

- **Where it happens.** (1) The recipe drawer's "Mark as cooked" row in its ⋯ More ("Keeps a record you can change later"; disabled until its write settles, so a double tap or a tap during the acknowledgement lag can't make two cooks and two Undo toasts, which offline means it waits for the write to be acknowledged) adds a cook at the recipe's own servings (`getCookServings`: kept inside 0 to 1,000) with `cookedAt` now, and a toast says "Marked Adobo chicken as cooked" with an Undo that deletes exactly that cook's id. (2) Cooking mode's Finish (last step) records the cook as it opens the final panel: `markRecipeCooked(recipe, servings, { isToasted: false })` from a recipe, `markPlannedPlateCooked({ plannedMealId, entries, cookedAt })` (no toast; one entry per recipe of the session, one for a single recipe) from a planned meal, guarded by `isMarking` so it runs once; the panel (`CookRecord`) says "Marking as cooked…", then "Marked as cooked" with an Undo (`removeCooks` over the ids this screen wrote, once, all or none: "Undoing…", then "Not marked as cooked" with "Mark as cooked"; hidden once a box was put away or a note saved from the panel, `hasFollowUps`: each has its own Undo, and removing the cook under them would orphan them) and "Servings made" for each recipe, whose taps write the cook with `updateCook` (field-scoped, reading `cookedAt` from the store at the tap). Timers are not stopped by Finish; Done goes through `leave()` and its "Stop timers and leave?" question. A plan's cook already in the store before this session wrote one makes the card "Already in your cooking history" (a recipe whose `markPlannedPlateCooked` result says `isNew: false` reads the same): Finish writes the session's servings onto it (`updateCook`), there is no Undo for it (Undo never deletes a cook this screen didn't write), and put-away and the note attach to that cook; a plate whose other recipes were marked elsewhere lists a recipe left unmarked as "Not marked", with "Mark as cooked" in the card's title row. A repeat Finish (after "Back to the last step") writes the servings again instead of a second cook. A failed write is a red line in the card with "Mark as cooked" to retry; a full history (500) records nothing and says why. Leaving before Finish writes nothing. Finish only records when `canMark` holds; otherwise it leaves as before. Started from a planned meal (`DayDrawer`'s `onStartCooking` puts `plannedMealId` on the subview state; a recipe's own drawer leaves it out) it is the same transaction and id as the row's "Mark as cooked", so one dinner can never be two cooks, and a meal can be a day ahead (the cook is written at once and the row shows it from that day). Every close that follows an awaited step (Done, a saved note, `leave()`) is `closeSubviewIf(...)`, which closes only if the screen showing is still this recipe's (and this plan's) cooking screen (a functional `setSubview` update in `Pinch.tsx`): the write can sit queued offline and resolve long after the person has left, and a bare `closeSubview()` used to close whatever was open by then (a half-typed recipe form: reproduced with a delayed write, then fixed). The header's back is not disabled while the write is in flight, since offline that would trap the person on the screen: it leaves, the write carries on and a failed one shows its sentence in the panel while it is open. (2b) The recipe drawer's footer primary "Start cooking", shown when the recipe is on today's plan and that planned meal has no cook yet: it opens cooking mode with that item's servings and `plannedMealId`, so Finish writes `{plannedMealId}__{recipeId}` like the planned meal's own Start cooking. The today key is read once when the drawer opens. (3) A planned meal on today or a day that has passed (`meal.date <= getLocalTodayDate()`) has one "Mark as cooked" chip per recipe in it, on that recipe's row of the plate, turning into "(✓) Cooked · Not cooked" once its cook exists (the toast's Undo covers the seconds after a mark, but this standing one can come weeks later and takes a cook out of the history, so it asks first, "Not cooked after all?", naming the recipe and when it was cooked). A recipe listed twice in one meal is one row with the servings added up (`getMarkableRecipes`), because the deterministic id can only hold one cook; an ingredient item has no row. (4) The drawer's Notes and history view starts with "(✓) Cooked 4 times, last Oct 12" ("Cooked once, on Oct 9"), a row that opens the history; the Recipes list and the drawer's front show no cook count.
- **Writes.** `addCook({ uid, recipeId, servings, cookedAt })` is a plain `setDoc` of a new document with a fresh id and returns the id (the drawer toast's Undo, the Finish panel's Undo and the history's "+ Add an earlier cook" use it); `markPlannedCooked(...)` is one `runTransaction` that reads the deterministic document and writes nothing when it exists, resolving `{ id, isNew }` (the decision is the pure `mergePlannedCook`, which `npm run check:pinch-math` covers with the other cook helpers), and a `false` from the planned meal's own row shows an "Already marked" toast; `markPlannedPlateCooked({ plannedMealId, entries, cookedAt })` is the same decision for a plate's recipes in one `runTransaction` (every document read first, then each missing one set), resolving `[{ recipeId, id, isNew }]`: cooking mode's Finish uses it for any planned session, one recipe or several, and treats an `isNew: false` as already marked (the panel says so; no toast); `removeCooks({ ids })` deletes several in one `writeBatch`, so the panel's Undo takes back all of a plate's cooks or none; `updateCook` is a field-scoped `updateDoc` of `servings`, `cookedAt`, `updatedAt` (plus a missing `plannedMealId` as `null`) and never writes a cached cook back; `removeCook` is `deleteDoc` of one id (Undo, and the history's Delete after a destructive confirm). The two adds refuse, with a sentence the screen shows, when the cooks listener hasn't landed or has failed, when the recipe isn't in the loaded recipes, when the servings aren't above zero and up to 1,000, when `cookedAt` is more than a minute ahead, and when the recipe already has `SOFT_MAX_COOKS_PER_RECIPE` (500) cooks. The screens gate the same predicate: the rows are disabled while the history loads, say "This recipe's history is full" at 500, and are left out when the listener failed. A transaction doesn't queue offline, so marking from a planned meal needs a connection: every cook write logs a Firebase error and shows "Check your connection and try again." for `unavailable` or an offline browser (the shopping list's rule), else one plain sentence; the recipe drawer's mark is an ordinary write whose toast arrives when the write is acknowledged, while the Cooked row in Notes and history already shows it from the local cache.
- **The history sheet.** "Cooking history" (opened by the Cooked row in the recipe drawer's Notes and history, shown only when the recipe has a cook, so for a recipe never cooked the way in is to mark it and change the date in its history) closes the drawer and opens `CookingHistorySheet`, a `FormSheet` titled "Cooking history" (a drawer on a phone, a modal from `sm`), from `RecipesScreen`. It lists the recipe's cooks newest first as "Oct 12 · 6 servings" (with "From your plan for Sun, Oct 11" when `plannedMealId` is set), the first `COOK_HISTORY_FIRST_PAGE` (8) and then "Show N more" in pages of 50, but only while more than three would be hidden (so 11 cooks show all of them, 12 show 8 and "Show 4 more"), with "Show fewer" once everything is open. A fixed header (the recipe's name and "+ Add an earlier cook") sits over the one scrolling list, capped with `max-h-[max(12rem,calc(92dvh-14rem))]`. A row, or the add link, swaps the sheet's content in place for `CookEditView` with a "Back" link (never a second overlay; each opening carries a token, and the close after a save or delete is `closeView(token)`, a functional update that does nothing once another view is showing, so a write that resolves late can't close the wrong one): a day and a time (`Input type='date'` with `max` today, stacked below a narrow container), a servings `Stepper`, Cancel and Save, and for an existing cook an icon-only `DeleteIconButton` on the left of the footer whose destructive confirm ("Delete this cook?") names the day and time. Save is disabled until something changed and says "Pick a time that has already passed." for a moment in the future (compared against `useNow()`, the 15-second clock); a save that leaves the day and time untouched keeps the stored `cookedAt` to the millisecond instead of the minute the inputs show. The edited cook is read from the live store by id, so one deleted on another device returns to the list. When the listener failed the sheet says so and offers no add; a full history says so and hides "+ Add an earlier cook". Under each row that has no note a quiet "Add a note" (and an "Add a note" row under Day, Time and Servings in such a cook's own view, disabled with "Save your changes first" while that view is dirty) swaps the same content for `NoteForm` (§5e), carrying a token too, so its close is the same functional update; both rows are left out when the notes listener hasn't landed or failed.
- **Reading.** `selectCooksByRecipe` (a `Map<recipeId, Cook[]>`, newest first by `cookedAt`, then `createdAt`, then id), `selectCookSummaries` (a `Map<recipeId, { count, lastCookedAt }>` built in one pass over every cook), `selectCooksById` and `selectCooksLoadError`, all `createSelector`s over the slice's items. The recipe drawer, the delete confirm, the plan builder's recent picks and cooking mode read the summaries (the count and the last date); a planned meal's row reads its own `Cook` object and whether the recipe's history is full, so every visible row of a meal re-renders when the cooks change, which costs nothing next to the few rows of one meal; a Recipes row reads no cook count. Cooks whose `recipeId` names no recipe are never read by anything, so they are neither shown nor counted.
- **Listener and failure.** `startCooksListener` (a plain collection `onSnapshot`) is started from `usePinchSync` with the other optional collections. Its error is logged (`console.error`), clears the loaded cooks and sets `loadError`; it never reaches the load-error screen (`selectPinchLoadError` leaves it out) and is not part of the loading gate (`selectIsPinchLoaded`). Without cooks the app is unchanged except that the drawer shows no Cooked row or "Mark as cooked" row, and a planned meal shows no cook rows.
- **Archive and delete.** Archiving a recipe (it is on a planned day or in a saved meal) leaves its cooks alone, so the history is still there; a hard delete takes the recipe's cooks with it in `deleteCooks`, which reads them from Firestore (`getDocs` with `where('recipeId', '==', id)`, one equality, so no index) instead of the store, because the store is empty while the cooks load or after the listener fails, and deletes them in `writeBatch`es of `COOK_BATCH_SIZE` (400), after the recipe's own delete has succeeded and as best effort (a failure is logged and the delete still finishes). Anything that survives (a cook written after the read, a failed batch) names a recipe that no longer exists and is ignored by every reader. Cooks made from a planned meal are not removed when the planned meal is: the person did make it.
- **The cap.** `SOFT_MAX_COOKS_PER_RECIPE` (500) is checked by the add actions against the loaded cooks and by the screens, not by the rules, which can't count a collection. It is per recipe, so the whole collection is not bounded.
- **A toast over a drawer.** The Undo toast is raised while the recipe drawer is open, so the toast stack sits above the drawers' overlay (`z-index: 110` on the notifications region in `index.css`; drawers and modals are `z-[100]`): below it the toast showed dimmed behind the overlay and its button couldn't be tapped. It is a central rule and reaches every mini-app's toasts.


### 5e. Recipe notes and tweaks

- **Where a person writes one.** (1) The recipe drawer's "Notes" section, in its Notes and history view under the Cooked row: with no notes it is one tinted `ActionRow`, "Add a note" ("Something to remember next time"); with notes it is the header "Notes" (with the count only past ten entries or while some are folded, `SECTION_COUNT_THRESHOLD`), that row, then the entries newest first. (2) "Add a note" under each time in the cooking history that has no note yet, and in such a cook's edit view (under Day, Time and Servings, a `custom` field of its form). (3) "Add a note" on cooking mode's last screen, once the cook it follows up is recorded (or already was, from the plan); saving shows "Note saved." in the panel, with no toast and without closing the screen. A note on a cook carries that cook's id as `cookId`; any other note writes `null`.
- **The drawer's journal.** `RecipeNotes` reads `selectNotesByRecipe` and `selectNoteSummaries` for its recipe and `selectCooksById`, and draws `NoteEntry` rows: the date (`formatDateShort`, with a year when it isn't this one), written "Changed on Oct 6" with a small "Tweak" tag for a TWEAK so it reads as a point in time, and an **Edit** button (named "Edit the note from Oct 6", so its accessible name holds its visible word) in a 40px band, then "After cooking on Oct 12" when `cookId` names a cook that still exists, a TWEAK's "You changed:" list, the text (`whitespace-pre-wrap`, four lines and "More" only when it overflows, measured by a `ResizeObserver` callback, so the state is set from a subscription and not an effect body) and, for a NOTE written before the recipe's latest tweak, "Written before your Oct 20 tweak"; a NOTE (never a TWEAK) ends with a quiet "Apply to the recipe" (`onApply`, below). The first 5 entries show and "Show N more" (pages of 50) only when more than three would be hidden, with "Show fewer" once everything is open. The drawer's front shows no note: the latest NOTE is cooking mode's (`CookNotes`, below), where next time starts. A failed notes listener turns the section into "Your notes couldn't load just now." with no add row.
- **In cooking mode.** `CookNotes` (`components/cooking`) sits above the step counter, and above the ingredient list (not on the final panel), and reads the same selectors as the drawer (`selectNotesByRecipe`, `selectNoteSummaries`, `selectCooksById`, plus `notes.isLoaded` and `loadError`) in its own component, so the session doesn't re-render for a note. It renders nothing until the notes have loaded without error and the recipe has one. Collapsed it is one tappable line of the newest NOTE (tweaks are skipped: the recipe already reflects them; with no NOTE there is no line), one line (`line-clamp-1`, a 📝 in a 20px emoji column, so it costs the step a single row), from the pure `getNoteSummary` (`utils/notes.ts`, checked by `npm run check:pinch-math`): the date ("Oct 12:", with a visually hidden "Your latest note," before it) and its words starting at a "Next time" sentence when one follows the first sentence ("…Next time double the garlic"), with "You've changed the recipe since." under it when `lastTweakAt` is later than the note, runs of white space squeezed to one so a note's line breaks don't take the line. Tapping it opens the journal in place, newest first through `NoteEntry` with `onEdit={null}` and `onApply` (see "Updating from a note"; no Edit button: editing is a sheet, and no sheet opens over an overlay subview), `RECIPE_NOTES_FIRST_PAGE` (5) at a time with "Show N more" only when more than three would hide. It is a plain muted line with a chevron (no fill and no hairline, 20px tall with a pseudo-element for a 40px tap area), so it never reads as a dish card or the Ingredients button beside it; the open journal sits between two hairlines. A late-landing listener adds it once and a step change never moves it.
- **The note sheet.** `NoteSheet` is a `FormSheet` ("Note": a drawer on a phone, a modal from `sm`) mounted by `RecipesScreen` after the drawer closes, like the cooking history; `NoteForm` is its body and is also what the history sheet swaps in place (with a "Back" link, never a second overlay). One textarea (`NoteTextField`, focused once the sheet has settled, with a counter from 1,800 characters) and, for a new note on a recipe that has cooks and no `initialCookId` (a note started from a cook doesn't ask), "Is this about a time you cooked it?" as a `PillGroup`, starting on a cook from today (local day, labelled "Today") when there is one: "No, just a note" first, then the latest four cooks (`getNoteCookChoices`, plus an older cook that was preselected), each labelled with its day, and with its time when two share a day (`getCookChoiceLabels`). Save is disabled until the text has a character (a blank paste stays disabled) and the footer says why. Editing offers only the words (the date and the cook show as a muted line, a TWEAK's "You changed:" list is read-only), an icon-only `DeleteIconButton` asks "Delete this note?" first. Saving a new note toasts "Added a note to Adobo chicken". A sheet whose notes haven't loaded says so and offers nothing.
- **Writes.** `addNote({ uid, recipeId, text, cookId })` is a plain `setDoc` of a NOTE with a fresh `generateUuid()` id and every key; it refuses, with a sentence the sheet shows, until the notes listener has landed, when it failed, for a recipe that isn't in the store, for text that is blank or over 2,000 characters, for a `cookId` that isn't one of that recipe's loaded cooks and at 300 notes for the recipe. `updateNote({ uid, id, text })` is a field-scoped `updateDoc` of `text` (blank is refused for a NOTE and becomes `null` for a TWEAK) and `updatedAt`, plus any key an older document lacks; it never writes a cached note back. `removeNote` is `deleteDoc` after a destructive confirm. The 300 is the app's cap, not the rules'.
- **A tweak is part of the recipe's save.** `saveRecipe` builds the TWEAK itself and puts it in the recipe's own `writeBatch`, beside the recipe update, any new source and any new ingredients, so the note exists exactly when the recipe write did (a failed save leaves no orphan note, and an offline save is queued whole). It does so only for an edit (never a new recipe), only when `selectTweakTracking` is `'on'` (the recipe has a cook, the cooks and notes listeners have landed without error and the journal has room), and only when `getRecipeChanges(existing, draft, …)` returns a line. A cover-only edit has none, neither has one that changes only the recipe's care (§5f), and neither has one that changes nothing the person could see (stray spaces, an empty step or description). The note's `createdAt` is the recipe's own `updatedAt` for that save, its `text` is the form's "What changed and why?" line trimmed (or `null`) and its `cookId` is `null`.
