The lines are compared with the recipe as this device last had it, so an edit made on another device in between can show in them.
- **`getRecipeChanges(before, after, ingredientsById, sourcesById?)`** (`utils/recipeChanges.ts`, pure, covered by `npm run check:pinch-math`) compares the two recipes after trimming and ignoring blank steps, a blank description and a blank line note, and returns plain-word lines in the form's order: "Renamed to “…”", "Serves 4 → 6", "Prep time 10 → 15 min" (or "Cook time 35 min → 1 h 10 min" past an hour, "set to", "removed"), a source change, then ingredients by line id ("Garlic 4 → 6 each", "Garlic 4 each → 0.5 cup", "Added Lime juice, 2 tbsp", "Removed Salt", "Salt 1 tsp → no amount", "Salt no amount → 2 tsp", "Added Parsley" for a line with no amount (an amountless line's unit is not compared), "Swapped Butter for Olive oil", a line note added, changed or removed, and "Ingredients reordered" when the lines both lists share changed their order), steps ("Step 2 now reads: “…”" quoted to 80 characters, "Added step 4: …", "Removed step 2: …", or "Steps reordered" when the same steps changed order; a shared start and end are skipped, so a step put in the middle is one line, not a rewrite of what follows) and nutrition per typed metric ("Calories 520 → 600", "Fat set to 20 g", "Protein back to the calculated number"). There is no line when nothing changed, and a safety line ("The recipe changed") covers any difference the rest missed, so "no lines" always means "no change". At most 8 lines, the rules' cap: past eight the first seven stay and the eighth says "and N more", and a line over 200 characters is cut at a whole character and ends in "…", counting 16-bit units the way the rules' `size()` does (tried in the emulator: 100 emoji pass, 101 are refused; counting code points would let a line of emoji through the check and fail the save). Amounts are compared as they print (rounded to two places), so 4 → 4.001 is not a line of its own and a save whose only change is one of those reads "The recipe changed". A check runs 600 random pairs of edits (lines exactly when the recipe changed, never more than eight).
- **Updating from a note.** "Apply to the recipe" on a NOTE entry (`NoteEntry`'s `onApply`, not on a tweak) opens the recipe form (`{ kind: 'recipe', id, fromNoteId }`): from the drawer's Notes it closes the drawer first; from cooking mode's open notes it goes through the same discard and "Stop timers and leave?" checks as leaving, then replaces the cooking subview. The form shows the note as a read-only quote at the top ("Updating from your note") and starts "What changed and why?" with the note's words, which the person may change. Saving writes a new TWEAK as above; the NOTE it came from is never written. A recipe with no cooks records nothing, as for any edit, and shows no "What changed and why?" line; the quote is still shown.
- **The form's field.** "What changed and why?" is a one-line input after Nutrition (Enter doesn't submit), with a line under it saying that saving adds a note with today's date and what changed. It shows only while tracking is `'on'`; at `'full'` a muted line says this change won't be noted and to delete an older note; otherwise nothing shows, so a recipe never cooked, a failed listener and a new recipe say nothing false. There is no way to save an edit without its tweak (open question 19).
- **Listener and failure.** `startNotesListener` (a plain collection `onSnapshot`) is started from `usePinchSync` with the other optional collections. Its error is logged (`console.error`), clears the loaded notes and sets `loadError`; it never reaches the load-error screen (`selectPinchLoadError` leaves it out) and is not part of the loading gate (`selectIsPinchLoaded`). Without notes the app is unchanged except that the drawer says "Your notes couldn't load just now.", no "Add a note" row or "changed" date shows, the history and cooking mode offer no note, and a recipe edit saves without a tweak.
- **Archive and delete.** Archiving a recipe leaves its notes alone. A hard delete reads the recipe's notes from Firestore (`getDocs` with `where('recipeId', '==', id)`, one equality, so no index) instead of the store, which is empty while the notes load or after they fail to and would leave them orphaned, and removes them with `deleteNotes`, `writeBatch`es of 400, after the recipe's own delete and as best effort (a failure is logged and the delete still finishes), like cooks; anything that survives names a recipe that no longer exists and is ignored by every reader. Deleting a cook leaves its notes, which then read as plain notes while the dangling `cookId` stays in the document.
- **Reading and performance.** The grouping and the summary are `createSelector`s over the slice's items (the summary in one pass). `RecipeRow` is a `memo` component, so opening a drawer or a sheet, or adding a note, re-renders no row of the list (it re-rendered every mounted row before); a recipe save still re-renders them, as every recipe snapshot does, because each snapshot gives every recipe a new object.


### 5f. Recipe care

- **Where a person writes it.** The recipe form's "Keeping it" row (`RecipeCareField`, a `SummaryEditor`) sits between Steps and Nutrition: a caption, the summary (`getCareSummary`, or "Add how to keep it" in muted text while empty, two lines at most) and a chevron. Its editor is a drawer on a phone or a short screen and a popover from `sm`, ending in Done like the others, and holds two compact number fields, "Fridge" and "Freezer", each with a visible "days" suffix (`type='number'`, 1 to 365, labelled "Fridge in days" and "Freezer in days"), the line "Whole days. A month is 30, so 90 shows as 3 months.", a "Reheat" and a "Tip" textarea (two lines from `sm`, three on a phone, since each can hold 300 characters). Captions are small and inputs 40px (`Field isCompact`, `COMPACT_INPUT_CLASS`); Enter in a days field closes the editor, and in Reheat or Tip it adds a line break (the drawer keeps it) and never saves the recipe. Days are typed as days and stored as one number, not chosen as weeks or months: a unit picker would double the editor's height for the one case that wants it (a freezer measured in months), so the summary does the converting on the way out ("90" reads back as "3 months", as 90 minutes reads back as "1 h 30 min" in Prep and cook). A bad value (days that aren't whole numbers from 1 to 365, a reheating line or tip over 300) shows in red under the row and in the editor, and the pinned footer says "Check the fridge and freezer days to save."
- **Form data.** `RecipeFormData.care` is a `CareText` (four strings), so typing never loses a half-typed number. `getInitialData` builds it from `getRecipeCare(recipe)`, `getDataFromExtracted` from the reading, and the save turns it into `RecipeCare | null` with `parseCareText` (blank parts null, all blank null). The form's dirty check includes it like any field, so Keep editing and Discard behave as before.
- **Writing.** `saveRecipe` puts `care` in the same field-scoped `updateDoc` (or the new recipe's `set`) as every other field the form owns, on every save: a recipe written before care gains the key the first time it is saved from the form, as `null` unless the person filled the row in, and a save that never opens the row can't lose what was there. A change to care never records a tweak (§5e): `getRecipeChanges` leaves it out of what it compares and of the check that guards its "no lines means nothing changed", so a save that touches only care writes no note and leaves the drawer's "changed" date and every older note's "Written before your … tweak" label as they were, and one that also changes something the tweak does cover records only that line (open question 20).
- **The drawer.** `RecipeCareSection` is a `FormSection` titled "Keeping it" between Steps and Notes, drawn only when `getRecipeCare` isn't null. Each part is a line with a fixed 20px emoji column: 🥛 "Keeps 3 days in the fridge", ❄️ "Keeps 3 months in the freezer", ♨️ "Reheat: …" (not 🔥, which means calories everywhere in Pinch) and 💡 the tip in the person's words (pre-wrapped, long words break); a part that is null is left out.
- **Cooking mode.** The Finish panel's put-away pills carry the keeping times ("Fridge · keeps 3 days", "Freezer · keeps 3 months", from `care` through `formatCareDays`); there is no separate leftovers line.
- **The readers.** The text, photo and link readings share one schema and one prompt, extended with `fridgeDays`, `freezerDays`, `reheat` and `tip` (all nullable) and the rule "fill in … only when the recipe itself says how long leftovers keep, how to reheat them or how to store them. Never estimate how long food keeps, and use null when the recipe is silent." The schema tells the model to answer in whole days (a week is 7, a month is 30) and, for a range, with its shorter end: a storage time is a safety figure, so the cautious end is read, where cooking mode's timers take the longer end of a cooking range. `normalizeExtractedRecipe` turns the answer into `ExtractedRecipe.care` with `toCare`: days are rounded down (a storage time is a safety figure, so 36 hours is 1 day and half a day is dropped) and must then be 1 to 365 (anything else is null, so "2 years" is dropped and never cut to a year), text is trimmed and cut to 300, a wrong type is null, and a record with nothing in it is null. A reading that finds no storage lines leaves `care` null, so the form's row stays quiet and nothing is listed under "We couldn't read"; the disclosure lines ("An AI reads it and fills in the form. It isn't saved.") are unchanged and still true. The form is filled for review only, as before, and the storage text a person typed is never sent to the AI.
- **Copy.** Pinch has no copy-out of recipes (the Markdown copy was removed in the redesign), so nothing carries care out of the app. If a copy returns it should add the lines under the steps with the person's text escaped for the format, and add nothing for a recipe without care.
- **Legacy and rules.** `isRecipe` reads `care` with `data.get('care', null)`, so a document without the key is valid on create and on update, and `isRecipeCare` allows null or a map with exactly `fridgeDays`, `freezerDays`, `reheat` and `tip` (days null or an int from 1 to 365, text null or 1 to 300 characters). It doesn't insist that the map holds something (the client writes null for that) and it changes nobody's access: the owner writes recipes as before, another signed-in user and a signed-out visitor are refused as before.
- **No new listener, function, secret or index.** `care` rides on the recipes the app already listens to; `firestore.indexes.json` and `functions/` are unchanged.

### 5f.2. Recipe links

- **Model.** `Recipe.links` is a required key typed `RecipeLink[]`; a recipe written before it has no key and reads as `[]` through `getRecipeLinks` (`utils/recipeLinks.ts`), which runs every stored list through `cleanLinks`: entries without a usable http(s) address are dropped, the first `HOW_TO` wins and a later one reads as `OTHER`, an unknown `kind` reads as `OTHER`, a label is trimmed and cut at 40 characters (blank is null), a missing id reads as `link-<index>` (stable across renders) and no more than `MAX_RECIPE_LINKS` (8) are kept. Default labels and emoji are constants (`RECIPE_LINK_LABELS`, `RECIPE_LINK_EMOJIS`), and a custom label replaces the default (`label: null` means the default). Every save of the form writes `links` (field-scoped `updateDoc`, or the new recipe's `set`), so a recipe saved after links existed gains the key even when it has none (`[]`). Links are not in `getRecipeChanges`: editing them never records a tweak (nothing about the dish changed), and a save that changes only them is not a "recipe changed" for notes.
- **Addresses.** `normalizeLinkUrl` is `toWebLink` (`@/utils/urlUtils`: trim, the one `http(s)://` token among words, a wrapped link with no scheme joined, several links refused, credentials and any other scheme refused) plus a length cap of `MAX_LINK_URL_LENGTH` (2,000). `getYouTubeId` reads `youtu.be/<id>`, `/watch?v=<id>`, `/shorts/`, `/embed/`, `/live/` and `/v/` on `youtube.com`, `m.`, `music.` and `youtube-nocookie.com` (`www.` stripped, exact host match so `evilyoutube.com` and `youtube.com.evil.example` have none), id exactly 11 URL-safe characters; a playlist alone or a channel has none. `scripts/checks/pinchLinks.ts` (chained onto `npm run check:pinch-ingest`) holds the parser, normalizer, cap and one-how-to cases and was mutation-tested (19 mutants of the helpers, all caught).
- **Form.** `RecipeFormData.links` is a `LinksText` (`{ howTo: { id, label, url }, others: LinkDraft[] }`: addresses as typed, so a half-typed one is never lost); `getLinksText` builds it from the recipe and `toRecipeLinks` turns it into `RecipeLink[]` on save (the how-to first, blank slots and rows dropped, a custom label kept only while `isCustom`). A how-to label written by something else is carried through the slot, which has only an address. `RecipeLinksField` is a `SummaryEditor` ("Links") whose editor (`LinksEditor`) keeps the open row in its own state (one at a time, in place: the drawer holds one level) and takes its removals through a local `useUndoRemoval`; its `UndoSnackbar` has `isOverSheet`, which draws the bar through a portal at the page's root at `z-[105]` (drawers are `z-100`, toasts `z-110`) and positions it over the drawer's own footer (`SummaryEditor`'s drawer footer now carries the `form-footer` class `UndoSnackbar` looks for). `SummaryEditor` also gained `leading` (a picture before the text) and `shouldFocus` (off where the first field is already filled in or the editor has none), and its popover caps the editor's height and scrolls inside. A bad address (`getLinkUrlProblem`) shows in red on the row and in the editor and blocks saving; more links than the cap (the how-to slot counts once it holds an address; a blank row never counts, and a row still waiting for its address holds back "Add a link") shows "Take a link out to save".
- **Drawer.** `RecipeLinksSection` is drawn last in `RecipeDrawer`'s recipe view (after the Cost tile, its note and the macros), nothing for `[]`. A `Button href target='_blank' rel='noopener noreferrer'` per link, so the other site opens in a new tab from the tap itself; no embed, no script, no autoplay. The how-to card's thumbnail is `https://i.ytimg.com/vi/<id>/hqdefault.jpg` through `FallbackImage` (`referrerPolicy='no-referrer'` is its default), over the tinted tile that is also the fallback for another video site or a failed load; opening a recipe with a YouTube video therefore asks Google's image host for one picture (an IP address and a user agent reach it; no cookie, no script). No lookup of a page title is made: labels are the defaults or the person's own words.
- **Rules.** `isRecipe` allows the `links` key (`hasOnly` only, never `hasAll`) and requires `data.get('links', []) is list && size() <= 8`. The per-link shape (kind, label, url, at most one how-to) is the client's, like ingredient lines and `group`: checking even one field of eight entries by index would add to a recipe rule that already runs near the evaluator's 1,000-expression budget. A read of a hostile list is safe because every reader goes through `cleanLinks` and an address only ever reaches an `href` after `normalizeLinkUrl` has checked its scheme. No index, Storage rule or Function changed. Proven on the emulator as the owner, another signed-in user and a signed-out visitor, base rules against the branch's (a recipe with links, 8 links, an empty list and a backfill onto a legacy recipe are the only outcomes that differ, denied then allowed; 9 links, a string, null, an unknown key, a changed `createdAt`, and every other user's and signed-out request are denied on both; a legacy recipe still reads, edits and archives on both).
- **Seed.** Alex's Adobo chicken has a how-to video and an article link; every other seeded recipe keeps the older shape (no key) so the legacy path stays exercised.

### 5g. Fridge & freezer boxes

- **Where one is made.** (1) Cooking mode's Finish panel, once the cook is recorded (below); (2) a recipe's or saved meal's drawer, the "Put some away" row in ⋯ More (a `ContainerFormSheet` mounted by `RecipesScreen` after the drawer closes, opened on that recipe or meal with nothing to pick); (3) the Fridge & freezer subview, "Add" (the same sheet, which first asks what it is: a search over the listed recipes and saved meals capped at four rows, ending in "Call it “text”" for a typed name, with a "Putting away" summary row once picked, and its search box focused once the drawer has settled). The sheet's title is "Fridge & freezer" and, once picked, the dish is named above the fields in a `SummaryRow` captioned "Putting away" (its chevron swaps the sheet back to the search, a back step that keeps `DetailsForm` mounted but hidden so its draft survives, with a "‹ Back" link; plain text when the sheet opened on one dish; no "Recipe / Saved meal / Typed name" line under it); its fields are how many servings (a whole-number stepper, 1 to 100), where (Fridge or Freezer, a `PillGroup`), **how many boxes** (a stepper from 1 to `MAX_CONTAINERS_PER_PUT_AWAY` (20), never more than the servings, with a line that says how the servings split: "2 servings in each", or "3 servings in the first, 2 in each of the others", the extra servings going one each to the first) and eat by (a date input with the hint "Your call. …"), inside the repo's `Form` as one `custom` field. It writes through `useContainerActions().putAway(inputs)`, which checks the cap (the soft cap counts containers: what is in stock plus what is added), builds the containers (`buildContainer`, a fresh `generateUuid()` each, one shared `createdAt`) and dispatches `writeContainers`: **one `writeBatch`**, so N containers land whole or not at all. Each document is an ordinary create, so the rules did not change; a batch of 20 was written against the emulator with the real rules. The batch is not awaited: the write is accepted locally at once, the sheet and the Finish panel answer from the store, and only a refusal that arrives later raises a toast ("That didn't save"), so a slow or offline network never holds the screen (offline, the toast adds that it was saved on this phone and will sync). The saved toast ("Saved 4 boxes (4 servings) in the freezer, eat by Jan 12, 2027", or "Saved 2 servings in the freezer, …" for one) carries an Undo (`undoContainerBatch`): one transaction that re-reads each document and deletes the batch only while none of them has changed since (`updatedAt` still equals `createdAt`), else it leaves them and says so. It reads server state, so an Undo tapped within one round trip of a save that is still in flight can find nothing to delete, and the put-away then lands anyway (narrow, accepted).
- **Eat-by defaults (`getEatByDefault`, `getEatByHint`, `getMoveNotes`).** Counted from the viewer's local today. Fridge: the recipe's `care.fridgeDays`, else `CONTAINER_FRIDGE_DAYS`. Freezer: `care.freezerDays`, else `CONTAINER_FREEZER_DAYS`. Thawing out of the freezer: the shorter of `CONTAINER_THAWED_DAYS` and `care.fridgeDays`. **A move starts from what the container already has** (`currentEatBy`): a container already past its date keeps that date through any move (the hint reads "Your call. The date stays as it was." and the move view says "It was past its date before it was moved."); a thaw starts at the earlier of its current eat-by and today plus the thaw days ("Your call. It already had an earlier date, so that stays." when the current date wins); moving an in-date fridge container into the freezer starts fresh from today. A container that has been frozen before says so in the freeze view ("It's been frozen once before.", "twice", "N times"), with "Your call." kept and no claim about safety. The hint otherwise starts "Your call." and says where the number came from ("Taken from the freezer time in your recipe.", "Once thawed, this starts at 2 days.", "A starting point of 4 days; change it to what you trust."; none says how long food keeps or that it is safe, and pinchMath guards that). The sheet's move rows read "Sets a new eat-by date" in every state. Until the person types a date, the date follows the default for the place; a typed date is kept when the place changes and never replaced by a default. A saved meal and a typed name have no care, so they use the named defaults.
- **The list.** `ContainersSubview` (`{ kind: 'containers'; recipeId?; mealId?; place? }`, a `Subview`, so its back button and the back gesture close it, and a sheet over it takes the gesture first) shows one place at a time through a thin `PillGroup` of Fridge and Freezer counted in servings ("Fridge · 10 servings"; the sum of what matches the scope chip and the search; the shown place is derived each render, never mirrored with an effect: the person's pick (a drawer's stock row starts the screen with its place picked: the subview's `place`), or the other place while a search matches only there, and before any pick the place holding what is due first (`dueFirstPlace`: the earliest eat-by among in-scope boxes that are due by tomorrow or past), the fridge when nothing is due, falling to the other place while that one is empty; both places keep their option when empty, so the switch never reshuffles), soonest eat-by first (`selectContainers` sorts once), drawing `CONTAINER_LIST_PAGE_SIZE` rows per place and "Show N more" only when more than three would hide, and a search from `LIST_SEARCH_THRESHOLD` rows. Rows are batches: `groupBatches` (one pass into a `Map` keyed by `getBatchKey`: `createdAt`, place, eat-by, recipe, meal and title, so boxes one put-away wrote stay together and a box moved or re-dated alone leaves) draws one row per batch in the order of its first box, each batch's boxes in take order (fewest servings first); the data is still one document per box, and pagination counts rows. A row says its servings (`getBatchServingsText`: "2 servings", "3 boxes of 2 servings", "3 boxes · 7 servings") and one eat-by item (`getEatByLabel`): plain words ("Eat by Oct 19", read in UTC) or, due today or tomorrow (`CONTAINER_EAT_SOON_DAYS`) or past, the same words in a soft-tinted `EatByBadge` ("Eat today", "Eat by tomorrow", "Past its date"), never a solid fill. A freezer row leads with `getFrozenAt` (the last FREEZE, else `createdAt`: "Frozen Oct 5") and names its eat-by only when `isEatByShown` (within `CONTAINER_FREEZER_DATE_DAYS` or past). Two fridge rows with the same title are told apart by a "Made Oct 14" tag, and a thawed one always says "Thawed". Opened from a drawer it shows the containers of one recipe, or of one saved meal and of the recipes in it (the same roll-up as the Meals row), with an "Only Adobo chicken ×" chip that clears the filter. With nothing in stock the subview shows no switch and says "Nothing in the fridge or freezer right now"; the "What you finished" door (an `EntryTile` under the switch and search) shows only once something is finished, so the unscoped list reads the first page of finished boxes as soon as it is ready (the same `finishedContainersQueryOptions(uid, 25)` the step uses, so opening it is instant, 25 reads once per 30 s), `getFinishedDoor` takes the read's state (`getFinishedRead(isError, hasData)`: LOADING, LOADED or FAILED, so a failure is never folded into "nothing") and whether a door is expected (`hasSeenFinished(uid)`, a `pinch:{uid}:has-finished` flag in localStorage that the finished read rewrites each time it lands, read once on mount): it shows the tile once something is finished or the read FAILED, holds its place while LOADING only when expected (a wrong guess moves the rows once), and draws nothing for a LOADED empty answer; a scoped list, whose read can be 200 containers, reads only while the scope has nothing in stock and shows the door while it has stock. "Clear out the fridge" is a red hairline `ActionChips` chip (`isDestructive`) on its own line above the rows. The header's "Add" is disabled until the listener has landed.
- **Finished.** The "What you finished" door (a step of the subview: `Subview`'s `onBack` returns to the list, so the back gesture does too, and the header has no Add) reads `finishedContainersQueryOptions(uid, count)`: a TanStack Query over `where('status', '==', 'DONE')`, `orderBy('updatedAt', 'desc')`, `limit(count)`, which needs the composite index `containers (status, updatedAt desc)` in `firestore.indexes.json` (the emulator never enforces indexes, so it is unverified until deployed; a Finished view that can't load says so in one line and nothing else is affected). "Show 25 more" raises the limit and keeps the page on screen while the new one loads. The queries are invalidated by every write that finishes, reopens or deletes a container (`refreshFinished`), and persisted nowhere (`meta.persist` is not set: they are Firestore reads). A finished row says how it ended against how much went in: "Ate 4 servings · Oct 12", "Tossed 1 of 4 · Oct 14", "Tossed 4 servings" or "Cleared out 3 of 6" (`getFinishedText`: the end event, plus the EAT events summed, so a corrected count reads true; a history at the cap, which may have lost an EAT, says "Ate it all" or names only what ended it). When the list is scoped ("Only Adobo chicken"), Finished reads that recipe's or meal's containers instead (`scopedContainersQueryOptions`: one equality, no index, at most 200 per recipe or meal, filtered to DONE, merged by id and sorted newest first), so an older finished container of the scope is never hidden behind the newest 25 of everything and "Nothing from … is finished yet" is true. Both reads go through `readContainers` (`queries/containerQueries.ts`), which throws `OfflineEmptyReadError` when the answer is empty and came only from the local cache (`metadata.fromCache`), so offline the step says "Couldn't load what you finished while you're offline." instead of a false "Nothing is finished yet"; a cached non-empty answer still shows. A failed read (that, or `FAILED_PRECONDITION` while the `containers (status, updatedAt desc)` index is still building) keeps the door drawn; `ContainersSubview` refetches the errored active reads of `containerKeys.all(uid)` through `refetchQueries` when `useNetworkStatus` reports a verified reconnect and on `useOnTabReturn` (the shared client sets `refetchOnReconnect: false`, and `retry` stays off), and `readContainers` logs any failure that is not `unavailable` with `console.error`. The cooking history reads `.data` only and treats the error as no data, as before. A meal reads its own containers plus one query per distinct recipe, so only the first 25 are drawn, with "Show 25 more" only when more than three would stay hidden (as the in-stock groups do). Ordering the reads by `updatedAt` would need a composite index (an equality plus an `orderBy` on another field), so they stay unordered and a recipe or meal past 200 containers shows an arbitrary 200, the same limit as the cooks' history below.
- **The sheet (`ContainerSheet`, a `FormSheet`: a drawer on a phone, a modal from `sm`).** Titled with the dish. Details first: where it is and how many are left (a freezer box adds "Frozen Sep 14"), the eat-by once as a tappable line (the tinted pill when due; it swaps in the date view), "Thawed in the fridge on Oct 14 · Frozen twice" when that is so; then one big filled "Ate one" (it closes the sheet; a batch's is "Ate a box", or "Ate one" when each box is one serving, and eats the first box in take order whole); then one `bg-muted/50` `ActionGroup` of three actions and a door: "It's gone" ("Keeps it in What you finished": swaps to Ate it / Tossed, **no confirm**, the Undo toast is the net), "Put in the freezer" or "Thaw in the fridge" ("Sets a new eat-by date"; one box of a batch; it closes the sheet with a toast) and "How many are left?" (no subtitle; `getCountChange`: fewer is `eatContainer` with the difference, 0 is `ALL`, more is `correctContainerCount`, so the history says "Ate 2 servings" or "Count 1 → 3"; a batch counts boxes down with `getBoxCountCopy`); and a last `GroupRow`, "History · 3 entries" (a batch: "Each box"), swaps in the rest; the pinned footer holds the trash (a destructive confirm that says the history is erased and, for an in-stock one, points at "It's gone") and Done as a secondary button. "It's gone", the move, the date, the count, the history and a batch's boxes swap the sheet's content in place with a "Back" link, never a second overlay; each owns its state, so it resets on every open. The record is the live store's, read by id, with a finished one's snapshot only for the Finished view; a container that has left the store shows "This isn't in your fridge or freezer anymore" and Done. `ActionRow`'s subtitle is optional (a row with no consequence to state has none). A write that fails says one plain sentence inside the sheet. **Taps are guarded in one place:** every container action goes through `useContainerActions`, which ignores a second tap on the same container while its first change is in flight (`once`), so two taps 80 ms apart eat one serving in the sheet and in the planned meal's list alike. Each toast's Undo is guarded the same way: Dreamer UI's toast action only calls `onClick` and never dismisses, so the toast gets a known id (`addUndoToast`), is removed on the first tap, and its id is remembered, so a second tap, even one that lands while the first undo is being written or the toast is still fading, does nothing; a later Undo of a different event is a different toast and still works. Done carries `data-modal-action`, which Dreamer UI's modal focuses first, so the sheet never opens with focus on an action and Enter, Enter cannot eat a serving.
- **Changes are transactions over pure plans.** `eatContainer({ id, count: number | 'ALL' })` (`ALL` means whatever is left when the transaction reads it), `tossContainer`, `moveContainer({ id, to, eatBy })`, `changeContainerEatBy`, `correctContainerCount({ id, count })`, `undoContainerEnd({ id, at })` each run `applyPlan`: `runTransaction`, `transaction.get`, the plan for that action over the fresh document, then `transaction.update` of only the fields the plan owns (`servings` and/or `status`, `place`, `eatBy`, always `history` and `updatedAt`). A batch's "It's gone", date and delete are one write over every box: `endContainers` and `changeContainersEatBy` run `applyPlanToAll` (one `runTransaction` re-reading each box and applying `planEat(ALL)`/`planToss`/`planEatBy`; a box that changed or is gone aborts the whole batch with one sentence), `removeContainers` one `writeBatch` of deletes; a batch is at most `MAX_CONTAINERS_PER_PUT_AWAY` (20) boxes. The end's Undo is `undoClearOut` over the ended ids (it reverses any end event while it is still the last one). A plan refuses with one sentence when the container is already finished ("This one is already finished."), when a count is more than is left ("Only 1 serving is left, so nothing was changed."), when it is already in that place, or when an eat-by isn't a whole UTC day; the thunks never let `servings` leave 1 to 100. A transaction needs a connection, so a failed write says "Check your connection and try again." The eat and toss toasts ("Ate 1 · 4 left", from the transaction's own result, "Finished Adobo chicken", "Tossed Adobo chicken", "Ate a box · 2 left", "Ate all 3 boxes") carry an Undo (`planUndoEnd`) that works only while the undone event is still the last one, and gives the portions back (a finished container comes back in stock with the portions it ended with).
- **Clear out a place (`clearOutPlace`, `undoClearOut`).** After a power cut, a clean-out or a holiday the person would otherwise finish every container one by one, so the Fridge & freezer list has one bulk end per place, an eraser button in the header, shown only for the whole place (not while a recipe or meal scope chip or a search narrows the list). It is `runInChunks`: `CONTAINER_CLEAR_CHUNK_SIZE` (200, the action's limit and not the rules', under Firestore's 500) container ids at a time, each chunk its own `runTransaction` that re-reads every container with `transaction.get`, plans it with `planClearOut` (a container that is gone, already finished or in the other place since the tap is skipped) and writes only the fields `planClear` owns (`status` DONE, `history` + one `CLEAR` event carrying what was left, `updatedAt`; `servings` and `place` stay, so Finished still says what was left and where). It resolves to the ids and instants it really cleared, so the toast's number is the number written; a chunk that already committed stays committed when a later one fails (the sentence says how many were cleared before it stopped); nothing cleared is a plain sentence and no toast. `CLEAR` is an end event like `EAT` and `TOSS` (`END_KINDS`): it is the last event, so `appendEvent` never drops it, `planUndoEnd` reverses it only while it is still the last event at that instant, `getFinishedText` reads "Cleared out 3 of 6" and `getEventText` "Cleared out · 3 servings". The Undo toast (once per toast, the in-flight guard keyed on the place so a double confirm cannot clear twice) is `undoClearOut`, chunked the same way, putting back only what is still as the clear-out left it and saying how many came back ("Put back 7 of 9; the rest changed since"). Any finished box's sheet offers "Put it back in the freezer" (`canPutBack`: DONE and its last event an EAT, TOSS or CLEAR with servings) through `putBack`, the same `planUndoEnd`, which respects `SOFT_MAX_CONTAINERS`. The clear-out's message says "out of the fridge" and "What you finished" (`getClearOutMessage(count, servings, place)`). A container another device put away after the tap is not cleared; deleting a container (which erases its history) stays a separate, per-container action. The rules' `kind` allowlist gained `CLEAR`; documents written before it are untouched.
- **History and the cap.** `appendEvent` keeps at most `MAX_CONTAINER_HISTORY` (10) events: the first is never dropped, the oldest EAT goes next, then the oldest EDIT, then the oldest move after the first place event that is not a FREEZE, and only then the event right after the first place event (out of reach while moves alternate between freezing and thawing); the event just added is never the one dropped. The first place event and the FREEZEs go last because they are what `getStartServings` (what went in), `getFreezeLine` ("Frozen twice", "Frozen 3 times", nothing for one) and the sheet's "Thawed in the fridge on …" line (`getLastPlaceEvent`, the last event that says where it went, so the line survives an eat or an edit) read: a cook's container (MADE, FREEZE) with many eat-by edits keeps its FREEZE, and a freeze-heavy history (a thaw and a refreeze past the cap) still counts every freeze. A long run of moves can still drop the oldest thaws after the first place event (`pinchMath` puts away 8, then freezes and thaws it five times: the 8 that went in and all five freezes stay, the oldest thaw goes). A cook's container starts with MADE, which records what the cook made (a whole number from 1 to 100, whatever the cook's half servings), and then where it went with its own servings; a container added by hand starts with where it went alone (no MADE: one event, so it spends no slot and doesn't read twice). What went in is the first place event (`getStartServings`). The cap of 10 is set by the rules evaluator's budget of 1,000 expressions a request, not only by the length checks (criterion 13).
- **Cooking mode (the Finish panel).** `usePutAway` (`hooks/usePutAway.ts`) lives in `CookingSession` beside the note hook, takes one entry per recipe cooked (recipe, servings made, eaten tonight, cook id) with one set of details open at a time (`getEntry(recipeId)` gives a recipe's card), and `CookPutAway` draws one recipe's card on the Finish panel once a cook is recorded (marked now, or already in the history from the plan) and the containers listener has landed without error. **What a bulk meal is** (`isBulkMeal`, no new field on `Recipe`): the session's servings are `BULK_MEAL_SERVINGS` (5) or more, or the recipe says it freezes (`care.freezerDays` set; fridge days alone are not a signal), or something of that recipe is already in stock. **The question is asked only when something is left**: the servings cooked, minus what is eaten tonight (the servings planned when it was started from a planned meal, else `CONTAINER_SITTING_SERVINGS`; started from a plan, `eatenTonight` is the plan's servings and `servings` the recipe's own, so a planned batch has something left and the question appears), minus what this cook already put away (the first place event of its saved containers), is above zero; otherwise only the quiet link shows, so a recipe that says it freezes, cooked at exactly the planned servings, no longer asks about a serving that doesn't exist. A bulk meal with something left asks "Putting any away?" with three answer `Pill`s ("Fridge · keeps 3 days", "Freezer · keeps 3 months", "Not this time"); any other meal, and the panel after "Not this time" or Skip, shows the same card headed "Put some away" with the two place pills; after a save it reads "Put another away" and shows only while something is left (`usePutAway().canOffer`: nothing saved yet, or `left > 0`). A pill opens the details with the place fixed. An answer opens the details in place on the panel (no overlay over the overlay): servings (default `getPutAwayServings(left)`: what is left, whole, from 1 to 100, so "Put another away" never re-offers the whole batch), a line that works the default out (`getPutAwayBasis`: "6 left after the 2 you're eating tonight", "6 left after about 2 for tonight", "Nothing left after … and the 6 already put away"), one quiet line when the total put away from this cook would pass what it made ("That's more than the 8 you made.", never blocking), the place when the link asked for it, how many containers (above) and the eat-by from the recipe's care; the pinned footer swaps to Skip and Save. Save writes the batch described under "Where one is made" with `cookId` set to the cook just made (or, on the "Already marked" panel, that planned meal's own cook id) and history MADE (the cook's servings) then FRIDGE or FREEZE, and the panel says "Saved 3 boxes (6 servings) in the fridge" with "Eat by Oct 17" under it for each put-away still in stock from this cook (`groupSavedBatches` and `getSavedHeadline`, read from the store by `cookId`). **The panel is the confirmation, so this save raises no toast** (`putAway(inputs, { withToast: false, onWriteFailed })`; the drawers and Kitchen still toast): a toast here would stack over "Marked as cooked … Undo". Each line this screen wrote carries its own quiet "Undo" (`undoSaved`, the same guarded `undoContainerBatch` the toast used, run once: the button gives way to "Undoing…", a second tap finds nothing, and it deletes exactly the ids that save wrote, or none if one has changed since); a refused Undo or a rejected write is one plain red line inside the card, and a line from an earlier visit has no Undo. **Layout:** the panel is one centered column (`max-w-md`, never stretched at 1280): the centered title lines, then each stage in one hairline card (`bg-muted/50`, `border-border`, `rounded-2xl`, no card inside it): the question (centered heading, the fridge and freezer pills, then "Not this time"), the details as settings rows (`ContainerFields layout='rows'`: caption left and control right, the helper line under both, "Where" as a full-width `PillGroup` row, the over-note and any error at the card's foot; the drawer form keeps `stack`), the saved lines, and the note box; "Add a note" is an `ActionRow` under the cards. **Nothing here blocks leaving:** Save disables only its own button while the write is accepted (it isn't awaited from the server), and the header's back, Escape and the back gesture all use `leave()`, which asks "Keep editing / Discard" only when something was changed (`hasDraft`). A held write channel was used to check it: the panel showed the save at once and leaving worked. **A note never ends the panel:** saving one returns to the panel with "Note saved." (the question still there while it is unanswered) and Done; the note box and the details are never open together. Nothing here blocks Done, and a failed containers listener leaves the panel exactly as it was. Cooking a plate, a card shows only for a bulk recipe, with the recipe's name above its heading.
- **The cooks' history.** `CookingHistorySheet` shows "Put away 4 servings" under a cook when containers came from it, from the in-stock store merged by id with `scopedContainersQueryOptions(uid, 'recipeId', recipeId)` (one equality, no index, at most `CONTAINER_QUERY_LIMIT` (200) containers) so a container that has since been finished still counts. Finished containers are never purged, so a recipe with more than 200 would sum an arbitrary 200 of them; a purge or a rollup is a follow-up.
- **Lists.** `selectStockByRecipe` and `selectStockByMeal` come from one pass (`getStockMaps`) into `Map<id, { fridge, freezer, containers, nearestEatBy, fridgeEatBy, freezerEatBy }>` servings per place and the nearest eat-by overall and in each place. `selectStockByMealWithRecipes` rolls a saved meal's stock up into whole plates per place (`getMealStockMap`, a `PlaceStock`: `{ fridge, freezer, fridgeEatBy, freezerEatBy }`): the servings put away as that meal plus the fewest servings of any one of its dishes in that place, so 5 and 3 servings of its two recipes and 2 boxed plates are 5 plates, never 10 (a recipe listed twice is one dish, an ingredient is no dish and no stock, and a dish with nothing in a place makes no plate from the dishes there), and each place's eat-by is the nearest of what makes its plates (a dish that makes none there sets no date); a meal with no plate in either place has no entry. The roll-up feeds only displays, never a decision or a write: the Saved meals row and its `kind='meal'` `StockLine`, its In stock filter and counts (a saved meal is "in the fridge" with a whole plate there) and the meal drawer's stock rows (`formatStockAmount`: "5 plates in the fridge"); a planned meal's "Eat from fridge" and its primary come from `getPlannedMealOffer` over the matching boxes (`useMealContainers`) and count boxes, so one dish in the fridge still offers that dish to a plate while the saved meal shows no plate. A row reads its own entry as plain numbers with `shallowEqual`, and both row types are `memo` components (`MealRow` works out its own cost from `selectCostContext` instead of taking it as a prop), so a change re-renders only the rows whose numbers moved: counting renders on Alex's Meals list, a new nachos container re-rendered "Big week" and no other row, where all three rows re-rendered before. `StockLine` draws one group per place, only above zero, in `RecipeRow` and `MealRow`: "🥛 5 in the fridge" (servings for screen readers) with an `EatByBadge` ("Eat today", "Eat by tomorrow" in the soft tint, or "Past its date") beside the place whose nearest eat-by is close, so the date is tied to the place it belongs to; on a phone each place is its own line, from `sm` they sit in a row with a "·" between them that is hidden where they stack, and the words "in the fridge" are read aloud (the drawer says them in plain text). `RecipesScreen`'s height estimate counts a line per place on a phone (20px with a badge, 16 without) and came within a pixel of the real chunk on Alex's list. The Recipes and Meals filters gain an "In stock" question (`MultiPillGroup`: Fridge · n, Freezer · n, only the places that have something plus any already chosen; both chosen is "either"; counts are recipes or meals, not servings; each chosen pill is an active chip "In the freezer"), and the filter button now appears on Meals when anything is in stock. The recipe and meal drawers show `StockRows` in their ⋯ More view, after the actions (so the first two actions are on screen on a short phone), under the caption "In your fridge and freezer" (the row in the list already says it before the drawer opens, so the drawer's front doesn't): a roomy row per place with the place's emoji in its chip and the amount in words ("5 servings in the fridge" for a recipe, "5 plates in the fridge" for a saved meal, `formatStockAmount`), an `EatByBadge` under the words of the place that is due; a row closes the drawer and opens the Fridge & freezer subview scoped to that recipe, or to that meal and its recipes, on the row's place. The drawers' row is "Put some away" ("In the fridge or freezer, with an eat-by date"), the words Finish's card uses.
- **Eating from stock in a plan.** In `PlannedMealView`, on today and yesterday only (a day ahead can't be eaten yet, and an older day is history), one row per place in its ⋯ More that holds a container of one of the meal's recipes or of the saved meal it came from ("Eat from fridge", "2 boxes match this meal"), minus the boxes the meal can't mean: a box whose `cookId` is one of this meal's own cooks (`{plannedMealId}__{recipeId}`), a box of a recipe already cooked for this meal, and a saved meal's box once anything on the plate is cooked. The rules are the pure `getPlannedMealOffer` (`components/calendar/plannedMealOffer.ts`, covered by `check:pinch-math`), which also picks the one primary (today only: "Eat from fridge" when an in-date box is due today or tomorrow and nothing is cooked, else "Start cooking" for an uncooked recipe with steps), the recipes to cook (`cookRecipeIds`, the plate's session), whether "Save to Saved meals" shows (2+ items and no saved meal with the same kind, ref and servings multiset) and `more`, the ordered list ⋯ More draws (`COOK` unless cooking is the primary, an `EAT` per place with its box count unless that place is the primary, `SAVE`), checked per phase; the view adds "Remove from this day" after it, always, red and on its own (`ActionRow` `isDestructive`). The view passes the narrowed list to `EatFromStockView`, which swaps the view in place for a list, soonest eat-by first, each with a button "Ate 2": the servings planned of that recipe (whole, at least one, never more than is left), or one plate of a saved meal's container. With one box the moment has one action, so its "Ate 2" is the one filled button, portaled into the drawer's pinned footer (`footerEl`) and the row keeps none; with several the rows keep their quiet secondary buttons, since the choice is which box. It records an EAT with the same toast and Undo, returns to the meal at once (`onDone`, a functional update that acts only while the eat view still shows, so no second "Ate 2" button is left to double-tap, and a second tap while the write is in flight is ignored; its Back returns to where it was opened from, More or the meal), and writes nothing to the planned meal, its prices or its cooks. Decided over "just open the container sheet": a sheet over the day drawer would be an overlay on an overlay. Nothing is eaten without a tap.
- **Shopping and the plate search.** `AddToShoppingSheet` says "You already have 3 servings in the freezer." under its heading when the recipe, the saved meal (`ShoppingRequest.mealId`, set by the meal drawer and by a planned meal that came from one) or the recipes in the items have stock; the list and its totals never change (the app does not subtract stock). `ItemSearch` shows a recipe's `StockLine` under its title in the plate builder's results; saved meals are results there too (no stock line).
- **Failure.** `startContainersListener` is an optional extra like cooks: its error is logged, clears the loaded containers and sets `loadError`, and never reaches the load-error screen. Kitchen's Fridge & freezer tile says it in its accessible name ("couldn't load just now"), the subview says so, Add is disabled, and the list lines, the filter, the drawer rows and the Finish panel's question all disappear while the rest of the app runs as before; `putAway` refuses with a sentence. Against rules that predate this PR (the preview builds against production, and CI deploys rules only on merge) the listener is denied, which is exactly this path: deploy the rules first, and nothing else in the app is affected in the meantime (checked by loading the previous rules into the emulator).
- **Not in this PR.** No push or reminder for an eat-by date (a scheduled sender would need the containers' index and a quiet-hours rule of its own), no automatic eating, no subtracting from the shopping list, no per-portion tracking, no moving some portions of a container, and no stock counts for ingredients (§6).

### 6. Stock tracking (reserved for Next Steps)

The fridge and freezer list now exists (§5g, decision 23); what stays open is ingredient stock. The data model leaves two things open on purpose: a future `stock` collection (`ingredientId`, `amount`, `unit`, `lastCountedAt`, `isTracked`) where a missing document means "not tracked", and `plannedMeals.madeAt` for mark-as-made. Neither is written in the MVP. Both arrive as new optional-by-default fields (`null`) with the legacy-document defaults the repo already requires.

---

## Security Rules Design Criteria

1. **Everything is owner-only.** Every path under `apps/pinch/kitchens/{uid}` (the kitchen document and all fourteen subcollections) allows read and write only when `request.auth.uid == uid`. There is no member-of, admin or role branch.
2. **`isAdmin()` and `isDevUser()` get no access to kitchen data.** They gate only the app *catalog* document `apps/pinch`, as for every other app. A dev fixture signs in as its own `uid` like anyone else.
3. **The app document** `apps/pinch` follows the existing four-read-predicate shape (admin/dev, unrestricted-public, uid-allowed, email-allowed) with admin-only writes, in alphabetical position among the app blocks.
4. **Shape validation covers every field on every type** (a recipe's lines are the exception, criterion 14), with the `T | null` convention (the key must exist; `null` is legal only where the type says so). Enums use `in [...]` against the same strings as `constants.ts`. Bounds: `priceCents` is an int `0 … 100_000_000`; `sizeAmount`, `amount` and `servings` are numbers `> 0` and `≤ 1_000_000`; nutrition values are null or numbers `0 … 100_000`; `gramsPerMl` and `gramsEach` are null or `> 0`; `date` is an int and a multiple of `86_400_000`; strings have length caps (names 120, titles 200, steps 2,000, notes 200, a source's label and a recipe's `sourceId` 80); arrays are capped at `MAX_RECIPE_INGREDIENTS`, `MAX_RECIPE_STEPS`, `MAX_MEAL_ITEMS` and `refIds` (rules can't loop, so a recipe's ingredient lines and steps are the client's to enforce, while a meal's at most 10 items are each checked by index: keys, kind, string ids and `servings > 0`; adding length caps on those ids or checking the elements of `refIds` exceeds the evaluator's budget, so those stay the client's); `url` is null or an `https://` string; `dismissedNudges` has at most 500 keys. A shopping item (`isShoppingItem`) has its seven required keys and one optional one (`sources`, a list of at most 60 entries read with `get('sources', [])`: what put the line on the list), `ingredientId` of 1 to 80 characters, an `amount` that is null only when the `unit` is `EACH` (a line with only a name is always counted in EACH) or a number above zero and at most 1,000,000, a known `unit`, a boolean `isChecked`, integer timestamps, and its document id equal to both `data.id` and `ingredientId + '__' + unit`. An idea (`isIdea`) has exactly the eleven keys of the type, every one required (there is no older shape), `kind` one of `LINK`, `PHOTO`, `TEXT`, a `title` of 1 to 120 characters, a `note` of 1 to 1,000 or null, a `url` of at most 2,000 characters that starts `http://` or `https://` (lower case, so `JavaScript:` and `//x` fail) and has no user name or password before the host, or is null, a `siteName` of 1 to 120 or null, an `imageUrl` of at most 2,000 characters that starts `https://` with no user name or password, or is null, a `photoPath` that is null or exactly `apps/pinch/kitchens/{uid}/ideas/{id}/photo.webp`, a `recipeId` of 1 to 80 characters or null, integer timestamps, and its document id equal to `data.id`; that `kind` fits the other fields (a link has a `url`) is the client's. A cook's `servings` is a number `> 0` and `≤ 1,000`, its `cookedAt` an int at most a day (`86_400_000` ms) after `request.time` (clock skew, so a moment that has not happened can't be recorded), `recipeId` and a set `plannedMealId` are 1 to 80 characters, and a set `plannedMealId` means the id is `plannedMealId + '__' + recipeId`. A recipe's `care` (`isRecipeCare`) is null or a map with exactly its four keys, `fridgeDays` and `freezerDays` null or ints from 1 to 365 and `reheat` and `tip` null or 1 to 300 characters. A note (`isRecipeNote`) has exactly its eight keys, every one required (there is no older shape), `kind` `NOTE` or `TWEAK` (a NOTE's `text` must be a string), `text` null or 1 to 2,000 characters, `recipeId` 1 to 80, `cookId` null or 1 to 80, integer timestamps, its document id equal to `data.id`, and `changes` a list of at most 8 strings of 1 to 200 characters, empty for a NOTE; rules can't loop, so each of the eight is checked by index (`isNoteChangeAt`), like a meal's items.
5. **Immutable fields only:** `id`, `uid`, `createdAt` (a source's and an idea's too), an idea's `kind`, an ingredient record's `catalogId`, a store's `catalogStoreId`, a product's `ingredientId`, a shopping item's `ingredientId` and `unit` (its id is built from them), a note's `recipeId`, `kind`, `cookId`, `changes` and `createdAt` (a tweak's record of what changed is history while its `text` stays editable; the stored `cookId` and `changes` are read with `.get(key, null)` and `.get(key, [])`, so a document made without them can still be edited with the backfill), a cook's `recipeId` and `plannedMealId` (a planned meal's cook has its id built from them; `plannedMealId` is compared with `resource.data.get('plannedMealId', null)`, so an older document lacking the key can still be edited), a container's `recipeId`, `mealId` and `cookId` (what it was made from never changes; the collection is new, so they are compared directly). Everything the UI edits stays editable, including a planned meal's `date`, `mealType` and `items`.

6. **A snapshot is written once and replaced only by a marked re-price.** A planned meal's `snapshot` may be written while the stored value is `null`; after that an update must leave it equal, or be a re-price (`isRepricedFrom`): the new snapshot carries an integer `repricedAt`, keeps the stored `frozenAt`, and the rest of the meal (`date`, `mealType`, `name`, `sourceMealId`, `items`, `refIds`) is unchanged, so its id and anything that points at it stay (a re-price that also changes any of those, or that writes the snapshot back without an instant `repricedAt`, as the freeze does, is refused). Only the owner can update, as for everything under the kitchen, so access for another signed-in user and a signed-out visitor is unchanged. `frozenAt` and `repricedAt` must each be `≤ request.time.toMillis()` plus five minutes of clock skew, so a phone whose clock runs a little fast can still freeze. The shape is checked (the five original keys required, `repricedAt` allowed and, being new, optional on a stored snapshot: `value.get('repricedAt', null)` is null or an int; bounded totals, at most 10 lines); the elements of `leftOut` and `lines` are the client's, since rules can't loop. (Rule check: `resource.data.get('snapshot', null) == null || request.resource.data.snapshot == resource.data.snapshot || isRepricedFrom(resource.data, request.resource.data)`.) The client writes `repricedAt: null` in every snapshot it freezes, so a rules deploy has to reach the backend before a client that freezes is exercised against it.
7. **Cross-field integrity the client could get wrong:** a planned meal's `refIds` equal the distinct `refId`s of its `items` is **not** enforced in rules (it's a derived index the client writes; a wrong value only affects the "in use" check, and the owner is the only writer), and the rules say so. A recipe has at least one ingredient; a meal and a planned meal have at least one item; `defaultProductId` shape is a string or null.
8. **Deletes:** the owner may delete any document in their kitchen, including the kitchen document. The client archives anything in use (Logic §3a), and the rules deliberately don't, since the data is the owner's own and a stale client must not be locked out of cleaning up.
9. **No cross-document rules.** No `get()`/`exists()`: a product doesn't have to point at an existing ingredient or store at the rules level, and a recipe's `sourceId` doesn't have to name an existing source and an idea's `recipeId` doesn't have to name an existing recipe and neither does a cook's (nor its `plannedMealId` an existing planned meal) or a note's `recipeId` (nor its `cookId` an existing cook); the client resolves a dangling reference to "Removed …" (a source to none, an idea's recipe to "not made yet").
10. **Rules tolerate older documents.** A field added later is validated on the incoming document with `request.resource.data.get('field', default)` and compared against the stored value with `resource.data.get('field', default)`; every edit action backfills the new key with its empty value in the same write. A recipe's `sourceId` is the example in use: `isRecipe` lists it in `hasOnly` but not `hasAll`, reads it with `data.get('sourceId', null)` and accepts it missing, `null` or a string of 1 to 80 characters. `care` is the second: listed in `hasOnly`, not in `hasAll`, read with `data.get('care', null)`, and written by every save of the recipe, so an older recipe gains the key. An idea has no older shape: the collection is new, `isIdea` requires all eleven keys, and `createIdea`, `updateIdea` and the recipe link still write back any key a document lacks (so a document made outside the app can be edited through the client). (Reserved, not yet written: `plannedMeals.madeAt`, the `stock` collection.)
11. **No server-only collections; one new index.** (Containers, criterion 13, add the one index below.) The only queries are a single-field range on `plannedMeals.date` and an `array-contains` on `plannedMeals.refIds`, neither of which needs a composite index. The sources, the shopping items, the ideas, the cooks and the notes are listened to as plain collections (the lists are grouped and sorted in the client), so they add no query and no index (a cook is read with one plain collection listener, and a recipe's cooks and notes are found in the loaded store, not by a `where`); `firestore.indexes.json` is unchanged by everything before containers. A source and a shopping item have no file, so only an idea adds to the Storage rules (criterion 12).

12. **Storage rules.** `apps/pinch/kitchens/{uid}/recipes/{recipeId}/{file}` and `…/stores/{storeId}/{file}` are readable and writable only by `request.auth.uid == uid`, only `image/*` content types, at most 5 MB, and the file name is `cover.webp` or `logo.webp`. Storage paths mirror the Firestore document paths. A recipe idea's photo is `apps/pinch/kitchens/{uid}/ideas/{ideaId}/photo.webp`, the cover's rule over again: owner only, `image/*`, under 5 MB, and the file name must be `photo.webp`; a PHOTO idea's `photoPath` is checked against that path in `firestore.rules`.

13. **Containers (`isContainer`).** Exactly its twelve keys (`data.size() == 12` and `hasOnly`, so a missing or extra key is refused), `id` equal to the path, `title` 1 to 120 characters, `recipeId`, `mealId` and `cookId` null or 1 to 80 characters with `recipeId` and `mealId` never both set, `servings` an int 1 to 100, `status` in `['IN_STOCK', 'DONE']` (a create must be `IN_STOCK`), `place` in `['FRIDGE', 'FREEZER']`, `eatBy` an int that is a multiple of 86,400,000 (UTC midnight) from 946,684,800,000 to 4,102,444,800,000 (2000-01-01 to 2100-01-01), integer timestamps, and `history` a list of 1 to 10 events. **The history is the part that is cheap on purpose.** Rules can't loop and the evaluator stops at 1,000 expressions per request (measured on the emulator, which enforces it: a first draft that shaped each event with `hasOnly`/`hasAll`/`in [...]` failed at 5 events), so each event is checked by index (`n <= i || isContainerEvent(history[i])`, with `let n = history.size()`) with the leanest shape that still holds: `size() == 5`, `kind.matches('MADE|FRIDGE|FREEZE|THAW|EAT|TOSS|EDIT')` (a whole-string match), `at is int`, `servings` null or an int 1 to 100, `place` null or `matches('FRIDGE|FREEZER')`, `note` null or a string of at most 80. Ten events with servings and place on every one (the busiest history the app writes) pass, on create and on update, in the rules matrix, so the cap of 10 is set by this budget and not only by the length checks; an event with servings, place and a note all set at once, which no action writes, can fail at ten (it is denied, not allowed). An update keeps `id` in the path and compares `recipeId`, `mealId`, `cookId` and `createdAt` with the stored ones; everything else the actions change (`servings`, `status`, `place`, `eatBy`, `history`, `updatedAt`, and `title`, which no screen edits yet) stays editable, a reopen (`DONE` back to `IN_STOCK`) is allowed, and nothing locks a history to appends only (the owner is the only writer and the client's plans are the only code that writes it). A key added later raises that count in the same PR and is read with `get(key, default)`, so a document written without it still passes. A put-away of several containers is one `writeBatch` of ordinary creates, each checked by this same rule (a batch of 20, each with a two-event history, was accepted by the emulator with these rules), so batches needed no rule change; a cook's MADE event carries a whole number from 1 to 100 (the client rounds and clamps it), and a container added by hand has a one-event history. The one index is `containers (status ASC, updatedAt DESC)`, for the Finished view; the stock listener is a lone equality and `where('recipeId', '==', id)` is one equality, so neither needs one.

14. **Recipe lines are client-shaped, the group included.** `isRecipe` caps the list at 60 lines and checks the fields around it, never a line's own fields: measured on the emulator, even the cheapest check of one field per line by index (`lines.size() <= i || lines[i].get('group', '') is string`) runs past the 1,000-expression budget at 60 lines and refuses the recipe ("Unable to evaluate the expression as the maximum of 1000 expressions to evaluate has been reached"; a `matches` check of null or 1 to 40 characters failed at 40 lines, a helper call per line at 30), with or without a create or update precondition. So a line's `group` (like its `amount`, `unit` and `note`) is shaped by the client, the 40-character cap is the client's, and the rules matrix proves only that a recipe with groups, one with `group: null` and a legacy recipe with no key are all read, created and edited by the owner and refused to another signed-in user and a signed-out client, exactly as with the rules before groups.

15. **Collections and favorites (`isCollection`, a recipe's `collectionIds`, a meal's `isFavorite`).** A collection has exactly its four keys (`hasOnly` and `hasAll`: a brand-new collection has no legacy shape, so a client that omits a key is refused), `id` equal to the path, `label` 1 to 40 characters, `createdAt` and `updatedAt` ints, `createdAt` unchanged on update; the 50-per-person cap is the client's (a rule can't count documents) and says so in §5a.2. A recipe lists `collectionIds` in `isRecipe`'s `hasOnly` but not its `hasAll`, and reads it with `data.get('collectionIds', [])` (`isCollectionIds`: a list of at most 20; the client shapes the ids, since a rule can't walk a list), so a recipe written before collections still validates and updates, one that sets the key to `null` or a string is refused, and 21 ids are refused. A saved meal lists `isFavorite` in `isMeal`'s `hasOnly` but not its `hasAll` and checks `data.get('isFavorite', false) is bool`, so older meals validate, rename and star. No rule says a `collectionIds` entry names an existing collection (criterion 9: no `get()`); readers ignore an id nothing names. Verified on the emulator as the owner, another user and a signed-out visitor, with the previous rules and these (45 cases, same outcomes everywhere except the intended new allows), including a legacy-shaped recipe and meal.

The shape of the block (helpers declared in the `kitchens/{uid}` match so the nested matches reuse them):

```
// --- App: pinch ---
match /apps/pinch {
  allow read: if isAdmin() || isDevUser();
  allow read: if isUnrestrictedPublicApp(resource.data);
  allow read: if isUidAllowedApp(resource.data);
  allow read: if isEmailAllowedApp(resource.data);
  allow create, update, delete: if isAdmin();
}

match /apps/pinch/kitchens/{uid} {
  function isOwner() { return request.auth != null && request.auth.uid == uid; }
  function isCents(v) { return v is int && v >= 0 && v <= 100000000; }
  function isPositive(v) { return v is number && v > 0 && v <= 1000000; }
  function isNullOrNutrition(v) { return v == null || (v is number && v >= 0 && v <= 100000); }
  function isUnit(v) { return v in ['G','KG','OZ','LB','ML','L','TSP','TBSP','CUP','FL_OZ','PINCH','EACH']; }
  function isMealType(v) { return v in ['BREAKFAST','LUNCH','DINNER','SNACK','DESSERT','DRINK']; }

  allow read: if isOwner();
  allow create, update: if isOwner() && validSettings(request.resource.data);
  allow delete: if isOwner();

  match /ingredients/{id}  { allow read: if isOwner(); allow create, update: if isOwner() && validIngredient(request.resource.data, id); allow delete: if isOwner(); }
  match /families/{id}     { /* same shape */ }
  match /stores/{id}       { /* same shape */ }
  match /products/{id}     { /* same shape */ }
  match /recipes/{id}      { /* same shape */ }
  match /sources/{id}      { /* same shape; id, label 1…80, createdAt, updatedAt */ }
  match /collections/{id}  { /* same shape; id, label 1…40, createdAt, updatedAt; every key required */ }
  match /shoppingItems/{id} { allow read, delete: if isOwner(); allow create: if isOwner() && isShoppingItem(request.resource.data, id);
                              allow update: if isOwner() && isShoppingItem(request.resource.data, id) && ingredientId, unit and createdAt unchanged; }
  match /ideas/{id}        { allow read, delete: if isOwner(); allow create: if isOwner() && isIdea(request.resource.data, id);
                             allow update: if isOwner() && isIdea(request.resource.data, id) && createdAt unchanged; }
  match /cooks/{id}        { allow read, delete: if isOwner(); allow create: if isOwner() && isCook(request.resource.data, id);
                             allow update: if isOwner() && isCook(request.resource.data, id) && recipeId, plannedMealId and createdAt unchanged; }
  match /notes/{id}        { allow read, delete: if isOwner(); allow create: if isOwner() && isRecipeNote(request.resource.data, id);
                             allow update: if isOwner() && isRecipeNote(request.resource.data, id) && recipeId, kind, cookId, changes and createdAt unchanged; }
  match /containers/{id}   { allow read, delete: if isOwner(); allow create: if isOwner() && isContainer(request.resource.data, id) && status == 'IN_STOCK';
                             allow update: if isOwner() && isContainer(request.resource.data, id) && recipeId, mealId, cookId and createdAt unchanged; }
  match /meals/{id}        { /* same shape */ }
  match /plannedMeals/{id} { /* same shape, plus the snapshot rule */ }
}
```

---

## Client State Management (Redux Toolkit)

Per-app store under `src/apps/pinch/store/`, exposing `PinchState` and `pinchReducer`, mounted in the central `src/store/index.ts` as `pinch` (next to `aList`, `nineLives` and `waypoint`).

```typescript
interface SettingsState       { settings: KitchenSettings | null; isLoaded: boolean }
interface IngredientsState    { records: IngredientRecord[];      isLoaded: boolean }
interface FamiliesState       { items: Family[];                  isLoaded: boolean }
interface StoresState         { items: Store[];                   isLoaded: boolean }
interface ProductsState       { items: Product[];                 isLoaded: boolean }
interface RecipesState        { items: Recipe[];                  isLoaded: boolean }
interface SourcesState        { items: Source[];                  isLoaded: boolean; loadError: string | null }
interface ShoppingState       { items: ShoppingItem[];            isLoaded: boolean; loadError: string | null }
interface IdeasState          { items: Idea[];                    isLoaded: boolean; loadError: string | null }
interface CooksState          { items: Cook[];                    isLoaded: boolean; loadError: string | null }
interface NotesState          { items: RecipeNote[];              isLoaded: boolean; loadError: string | null }
interface ContainersState     { items: Container[];               isLoaded: boolean; loadError: string | null }   // only what is in stock
interface CollectionsState    { items: Collection[];              isLoaded: boolean; loadError: string | null }   // an optional extra, like sources
interface MealsState          { items: Meal[];                    isLoaded: boolean }
interface PlannedMealsState   { items: PlannedMeal[]; windowKey: string | null; isLoaded: boolean }
export interface PinchState { settings: SettingsState; ingredients: IngredientsState; families: FamiliesState;
  stores: StoresState; products: ProductsState; recipes: RecipesState; sources: SourcesState; collections: CollectionsState; shopping: ShoppingState; ideas: IdeasState; cooks: CooksState; notes: NotesState; containers: ContainersState; meals: MealsState; plannedMeals: PlannedMealsState }
```

Every slice resets on the shared `resetAllState`, so a user switch never shows the previous person's data.

**Two listener tiers**, both under `store/listeners/` and started from `usePinchSync(uid, shownMonth)` called once from `Pinch.tsx` (the only orchestrator); no tab, panel or drawer has an `onSnapshot` of its own:

1. **Signed-in user:** `startSettingsListener`, `startIngredientsListener`, `startFamiliesListener`, `startStoresListener`, `startProductsListener`, `startRecipesListener`, `startSourcesListener`, `startCollectionsListener`, `startShoppingListener`, `startIdeasListener`, `startCooksListener`, `startNotesListener`, `startContainersListener` (`where('status', '==', 'IN_STOCK')`), `startMealsListener` — `onSnapshot` of the kitchen document and each collection under `kitchens/{uid}`. One effect keyed on `uid`.
2. **Visible window:** `startPlannedMealsListener(uid, windowKey, onChange)` — `onSnapshot` of `plannedMeals` where `date >= windowStart` and `date < windowEnd`, a single-field range covering the window's centre month (`shownMonth`) plus `MONTH_WINDOW_PADDING` (2) months on each side. The effect is keyed on that month (`"2026-10"`). `CalendarScreen` moves the centre only when the view being shown reaches past the window (`isRangeInWindow`), so month paging restarts the listener about every second page and week or day paging seldom does. The slice keeps the previous window's documents until the new snapshot lands, so the grid never blanks while paging.

**Loading gate.** `Pinch.tsx` shows a skeleton until every user-tier slice `isLoaded` and the first window has landed. An empty kitchen (no settings document, no records) is a valid state that renders the empty Calendar; there is no first-run blocking modal. The settings listener is waited for but its failure never shows the error screen (the nudges simply stay open), since it only carries an enhancement, and the sources listener is the same: its first snapshot or error ends the gate (so the filter doesn't pop in after the list), and its error is left out of `selectPinchLoadError`. The shopping listener is left out of the gate as well as of the error (a screen that needs it shows nothing until it lands), and its error is logged and clears its items. The ideas listener is the same: it is left out of the gate and of the error, the Ideas list shows nothing until it lands, and its error is logged and clears its items.

**Actions (thunks)** in `store/actions/`, writing only to the person's own paths:

| Action | Write |
|---|---|
| `saveSettings(fields)` | a field-scoped dotted-path write (`showNutrition.protein`, built with `FieldPath`) in one `runTransaction` that creates the document on first write; a closed nudge is no longer written ("Not now" lasts until the page reloads), and `dismissedNudges` keys already stored are only read and cleared when a price is added |
| `saveIngredient(draft)` | `setDoc` of the record (custom: new id; catalog: id = catalog id) with the full shape; edits are field-scoped `updateDoc` |
| `removeIngredient(id)` | the archive-or-delete check (Logic §3a) |
| `saveFamily` | `setDoc` (no delete in the MVP, so no family is ever archived) |
| `saveStore(draft)` | `setDoc` to create, field-scoped `updateDoc` to edit; a catalog store is created lazily by `ensureCatalogStore(catalogStoreId)` (create-if-absent inside a `runTransaction`, so a double tap makes one); an uploaded image goes to Storage first, then `imagePath` is written |
| `removeStore(id)` | archive when any product points at it, else delete + delete the image |
| `saveProduct(draft)` | one `writeBatch`: the product document and, when it's the ingredient's first live product or "Make this the default" was chosen, the record's `defaultProductId` (an upsert for a catalog ingredient with no record); also clears the matching `dismissedNudges` keys; preceded by `freezePastMeals` |
| `removeProduct(id)` | delete; re-points `defaultProductId` to another live product or `null` in the same batch |
| `setDefaultProduct(ingredientId, productId)` | field-scoped `updateDoc` of the record's `defaultProductId` (a transaction confirms the product still exists, so a stale device can't leave it dangling) |
| `saveRecipe(draft)` | `setDoc`/field-scoped `updateDoc` of what the form owns (never the whole cached object; `care` is one of those fields, written on every save and `null` when empty, so an older recipe gains the key); new custom ingredients from an AI draft are created in the same `writeBatch`; a cover goes to Storage first; preceded by `freezePastMeals`; a Firebase error is logged and shown as one plain sentence ("Check your connection and try again." when offline or `unavailable`, a Storage code's own plain sentence, else "Unable to save this recipe."), never its raw text, while the thunk's own sentences reach the form verbatim |
| `removeRecipe(id)` | the archive-or-delete check, preceded by `freezePastMeals` |
| `renameSource({ id, label })` | field-scoped `updateDoc` of `label` and `updatedAt`; refuses a label another loaded source has ("You already have a source called …") |
| `removeSource(id)` | `writeBatch` (400 writes each) of the source's delete and `sourceId: null` on every loaded recipe that names it |
| `addToShoppingList({ lines })` | one `runTransaction` that reads every target item first, then creates, adds to or restarts each (§5b); at most 200 distinct lines; returns how many it wrote |
| `setShoppingItemChecked({ ids })` / `updateShoppingItem({ mergedIds? })` / `removeShoppingItem({ ids })` | a field-scoped `writeBatch` of `isChecked` over a row's items, `updateDoc` of `amount` or a transaction that moves the item (and folds a row's other items into it), and a `writeBatch` of deletes |
| `clearCheckedShoppingItems()` | takes the checked ids from the store, then one `runTransaction` per chunk of 250 that re-reads each item and deletes only those still checked; returns the items really deleted, for the Undo (chunks are independent, so not atomic together) |
| `restoreShoppingItems({ items })` | one `runTransaction` per chunk of 250 that recreates each item as it was unless it exists again; returns how many it put back |
| `createIdea({ draft })` | refuses while the ideas listener hasn't landed or has failed, then checks the title, note, link (http(s), at most 2,000 characters, not already saved), the 200-idea cap and that a photo idea has a picture, then uploads a photo first (resized to webp; a file the browser couldn't decode is refused with "That picture couldn't be read. Try a JPG, PNG or WebP photo.") and `setDoc`s the idea with every key (a photo is deleted again if the write fails); an empty title on a link becomes the host; `siteName` is the page's or the host; `imageUrl` is kept only if it is https; takes an optional caller-chosen `id`, and its checks are `getNewIdeaProblem`, which the sheets run first; returns the id |
| `updateIdea({ id, title, note, url, photoFile })` | field-scoped `updateDoc` of `title`, `note`, `updatedAt`, a changed link's `url`, `siteName` and `imageUrl: null`, a replaced photo's `photoPath`, and the keys an older document lacks |
| `removeIdea(id)` | `deleteDoc`, then the photo object (best effort); the recipe an idea became is not touched |
| `saveRecipe(draft)` with `fromIdeaId` | the recipe's own batch commits first and unchanged; then, if the idea is still in the loaded store, a separate field-scoped `updateDoc` of `{ …missing keys, recipeId, updatedAt }` on the idea, whose failure is logged and never thrown; returns `{ recipeId, linkedIdea }` |
| `addCook({ recipeId, servings, cookedAt })` | `setDoc` of a cook with a fresh `generateUuid()` id and every key; refuses (with a sentence) until the cooks listener has landed, when it failed, for a recipe not in the store, for servings outside 0 to 1,000, for a `cookedAt` more than a minute ahead and at 500 cooks for the recipe (§5d); returns the id |
| `markPlannedCooked({ plannedMealId, recipeId, servings, cookedAt })` | one `runTransaction` that reads `{plannedMealId}__{recipeId}` and writes the cook only when it doesn't exist; resolves `{ id, isNew }` (needs a connection, like every transaction); the planned meal's row calls it |
| `markPlannedPlateCooked({ plannedMealId, entries: [{ recipeId, servings }], cookedAt })` | one `runTransaction` over `{plannedMealId}__{recipeId}` for each entry, writing only the missing ones; resolves `[{ recipeId, id, isNew }]` (cooking mode's Finish from a plan) |
| `updateCook({ id, servings, cookedAt })` | field-scoped `updateDoc` of `servings`, `cookedAt`, `updatedAt` and a missing `plannedMealId` as `null` |
| `removeCook(id)` | `deleteDoc` (the toast's Undo, a planned meal row's Undo, and the history's Delete after a destructive confirm) |
| `removeCooks({ ids })` | one `writeBatch` of deletes (cooking mode's Undo, all or none) |
| `removeRecipe(id)` (hard delete) | after the recipe's delete, `deleteCooks` and `deleteNotes` each query their collection (`where('recipeId', '==', id)`, one equality, no index) and remove what they find in `writeBatch`es of 400, best effort; an archive leaves them |
| `addNote({ recipeId, text, cookId })` | `setDoc` of a NOTE with a fresh `generateUuid()` id and every key; refuses (with a sentence) until the notes listener has landed, when it failed, for a recipe not in the store, for blank or over-long text, for a cook that isn't the recipe's and at 300 notes for the recipe (§5e); returns the id |
| `updateNote({ id, text })` | field-scoped `updateDoc` of `text` and `updatedAt` plus any key an older document lacks (a TWEAK's blank text is `null`, a NOTE's is refused) |
