| `removeNote(id)` | `deleteDoc` (the note sheet's trash after a destructive confirm) |
| `saveRecipe(draft)` (an edit of a cooked recipe) | the recipe's own `writeBatch` also holds the TWEAK note (`draft.tweakReason` becomes its `text`) when something the form edits changed; never for a new recipe or one with no cook (§5e) |

| `useContainerActions().putAway(inputs)` then `writeContainers({ containers })` | `putAway` refuses (with a sentence) until the containers listener has landed, when it failed, for a missing name, a recipe and a meal together, servings outside 1 to 100, an eat-by that isn't a whole UTC day, and when what is in stock plus what is added would pass `SOFT_MAX_CONTAINERS` (500); then it builds the containers (a fresh `generateUuid()` id each, the full shape, one shared `createdAt`, history MADE with the cook's servings only from a cook, then FRIDGE or FREEZE with the container's own servings) and `writeContainers` commits them in ONE `writeBatch` (1 to 20 ordinary creates, no rules change); the batch is not awaited by the UI, and a refusal arriving later raises a toast |
| `undoContainerBatch({ ids })` | one `runTransaction`: reads every container of a put-away and deletes them only while none has changed since it was written (`updatedAt` still equals `createdAt`), else it leaves them and says so |
| `eatContainer({ id, count })` / `tossContainer({ id })` / `moveContainer({ id, to, eatBy })` / `changeContainerEatBy({ id, eatBy })` / `correctContainerCount({ id, count })` / `undoContainerEnd({ id, at })` | each one `runTransaction` that re-reads the container, applies the pure plan (`planEat`, `planToss`, `planMove`, `planEatBy`, `planCount`, `planUndoEnd`) and updates only the fields it owns; a plan that can't hold stops with one sentence; eating, tossing, undoing and deleting also invalidate the Finished queries |
| `removeContainer({ id })` | `deleteDoc` (the put-away toast's Undo, and the sheet's trash after a destructive confirm that says the history goes too) |
| `saveMeal(draft)` / `removeMeal(id)` | `setDoc`/`updateDoc`; meals are referenced only by `sourceMealId` (never read for display), so a meal is always deletable |
| `planMeal({ date, mealType, items, name, sourceMealId })` | `setDoc` of a new planned meal with `refIds` derived; a past date is frozen in the same write |
| `updatePlannedMeal(id, fields)` | a `runTransaction` that re-reads the meal, refuses a frozen one, then updates what the builder owns (`date`, `mealType`, `items`, `refIds`, `updatedAt`) |
| `removePlannedMeal(id)` | `deleteDoc` after `useActionModal().confirm({ destructive: true })` |
| `savePlannedAsMeal(plannedId, name)` | `setDoc` of a new meal from the planned meal's items; reads the planned meal from the store at tap time |
| `freezePastMeals()` | field-scoped `snapshot` writes for the loaded, unfrozen past meals: one `writeBatch` per 100, falling back to one `updateDoc` per meal when a batch is refused |
| `repriceDay({ uid, dayKey })` | one `runTransaction` that re-reads the day's frozen meals, then replaces each one's `snapshot` (from today's prices, `repricedAt` set) and `updatedAt`; nothing else on the meal changes |

No cached document is ever written back whole. There is no cross-person concurrency; the create-if-absent cases above and the shopping list's add and unit change (two devices of one person can add at once) are the only transactions.

**Selectors** (`store/selectors.ts`): `createSelector` for everything that builds a new array or object, so no `useAppSelector` returns a fresh reference without `shallowEqual`.

- `selectIngredientsById` — catalog merged with records (a `Map`), memoized on the records
- `selectIngredientFamilies` — grouped list for Kitchen (Yours / All), archived hidden
- `selectDefaultProducts` — `Map<ingredientId, Product>`
- `selectRecipeCosts` — `Map<recipeId, RecipeCost>` computed once per change of recipes, products or ingredients
- `selectPlannedByDay` — `Map<dayKey, PlannedMeal[]>` read by the Calendar's `renderCell` and the day drawer, so a cell never filters the list
- `selectPeriodStats(state, startKey, endKey)` — one pass over the planned meals of the shown month or week (`getPeriodStats(range)`): cost, average per fully priced meal, what it leaves out, and the totals by day that the summary sheet lists (`byType` is still computed; nothing reads it now). The sheet adds `getPricedCoverage` (distinct ingredients used and how many are priced) only while open; `getPeriodCostText`, `getDayCostText` and `formatPeriodCost` word the line, the tile and the rows (`getDayCostText` formats through `formatPeriodCost` too, so a day of $1,000 or more reads "$1,235", not "$1,234.56+" in its row and "$1,235" in its drawer tile; checked at 99,999, 100,000 and 123,456 cents); `getPeriodCostTile` words the Total tile from the same figure ("$4.10" over "Total so far", "$4.10+" over "Total", `null` with nothing priced, so the line keeps saying "No price yet"); `getPeriodTotal` is what the summary sheet's tile calls (the front tile's words, or the line's "No price yet" / "Not priced" under "Total"), so the tile and the sheet it opens say one figure in one anatomy; and `getCostAverages` words "Avg per fully priced meal" and "Per g protein, fully priced" (from three such meals, protein only while it is on) once for both the Calendar's stats tiles and the sheet's reference lines (all checked by `check:pinch-math`, including a period of fully priced, partly priced, frozen and priceless meals). The period's total carries one qualifier: "so far" while a left-out ingredient sits on a meal that isn't fixed yet (`openLeftOut`), "+" once every gap is on a fixed day ("$1,805+"), so a frozen number never says "so far"; the averages carry none. The Calendar's header action (Plan and the View options eye) is drawn only once there is a recipe or a saved meal (`selectRecipes`, `selectMeals`), and the eye's two switches (`useListViewOptions(uid, 'calendar')`) reach one place each: "Show stats" (`showStats`, off to start) draws `StatsTiles` under `SummaryLine` and tells the line (`isTotalBelow`, true while `getPeriodCostTile` has a tile) to leave the total to the Total tile, and the sheet (`isStatsOnFront`) to leave its reference lines out (they come from the same functions as the tiles, so each figure shows once, on the front or in the sheet); "Show prices" reaches the Week's meal rows as `showPrice`. Switching a nutrition number on from the Calendar's sheet changes nothing else: the number shows in the tiles while `showStats` is on, and elsewhere either way. `StatsTiles` is `StatTile`s (Total, Avg per fully priced meal, Per g protein, fully priced; `isLabelWrapped`, so a long label wraps under its number, and one `getValueSizeClass` step for every number in the row, set by the longest figure (two characters of slack in a row of one or two tiles, and a phrase like "No price yet" counts for none) and never under 12px, with a three-tile row at 360px or under giving a total of eight characters or more ("$12,345+", which doesn't fit a 91px tile at 12px beside its warning icon; seven in a day's drawer, where the tile is 85px) the whole row and the other two the next) over `MacroStats` tiles for the daily nutrition (the stacked emoji, number and name tile below `sm`, the one-line tile from `sm`); the whole block is one pointer target for the summary sheet (an `aria-hidden`, `tabIndex={-1}` button behind the tiles, which are `pointer-events-none` except for their help buttons), so a keyboard reaches the line above and the warning icon, never a second tab stop for the same sheet. `PeriodLeftOutHelp` is the warning icon's text for the Total tile, the sheet's Total tile and the day drawer's. **A day's stats** are the same functions over one day: `DayDrawer` reads `selectPeriodStats(state, dayKey, dayKey)`, its Total tile is `getPeriodTotal(stats, dayKey < todayKey)` (so it is the figure the summary sheet's row for that day shows, and it says "No price yet" or "Not priced" itself since no summary line does), and `StatsTiles` takes `day` (always a Total tile, the nutrition captioned "That day"), an optional `onOpen` (no target behind the tiles inside the drawer, which has no sheet to open) and `isNutritionHidden` (the cost tiles alone, for the stats view, whose nutrition is already on the front). The nutrition block is `NutritionTiles` (`values`, `caption`: the `MacroStats` tiles for the switches that are on and known, nothing otherwise), which `StatsTiles` renders under "Daily average" or "That day" and the drawer's front renders on its own. `isStatsShown` (the Calendar's `showStats`) comes down as a prop from `CalendarScreen`, with `todayKey`, so the drawer follows the Calendar's clock and option: on, `StatsTiles` replaces the lone Total tile and the fixed-day note drops under it; off, the Total tile stays and `NutritionTiles` draws the day's nutrition under it ("That day": a nutrition switch that is on shows its number on the day's front without a tap, as View options' "Nutrition, everywhere" says), and `getDayStatsOffer(stats, show)` returns `{ subtitle: 'Cost averages' }` only when `getCostAverages` has an average, since the Total and the nutrition are already there; its "Stats for this day" row (`ActionGroup` and `GroupRow`) swaps the cost tiles in place (`StatsTiles` with `isNutritionHidden`; `isStatsOpen`, derived to false when the offer goes away; a `BackLink` with `useBackStep`, so back and Escape step out one layer and focus returns to the row's button). A day with nothing planned renders none of this (one muted line says what can go on the day). The summary sheet's reference lines sit in its fixed head under the Total, never inside the list that scrolls (they were the last child of that list, under the fold of a 190px scroller), so a short phone's first screenful holds them; its column cap is the screen's 92% less 15rem, not the shared 22rem, because its title and footer measure about 13rem and a head with a total, references, a nudge and a shopping row left a tall phone 140px of height unused
- `selectSources`, `selectSourcesById`, `selectSourceCounts` — the sources A to Z, a `Map` of them, and the listed recipes counted per source (and without one) in one pass; the Recipes filter, the Sources list and a source's delete confirm read the counts
- `selectCollections`, `selectCollectionsById`, `selectCollectionCounts`, `selectIsCollectionsReady` — the collections A to Z, a `Map` of them, the listed recipes counted per collection (and in none) in one pass, and whether the listener has landed without error; the form's row, the Recipes filter, the drawer's chips and the Collections sheet read them. `selectMeals` puts starred saved meals first, then newest first
- `selectShoppingItems`, `selectShoppingById`, `selectShoppingOpenCount`, `selectShoppingRows`, `selectShoppingCost` — the items, a `Map` of them, how many are left (the Shopping tab's badge), the groups and "Got it" rows in one pass, and the approximate cost of what is left with what it leaves out
- `selectIdeas`, `selectIdeasById`, `selectIdeaGroups` — the ideas newest first, a `Map` of them, and `{ toTry, made }` (made = its `recipeId` names a listed recipe; built with two filters over the ideas)
- `selectCooksByRecipe`, `selectCookSummaries`, `selectCooksById`, `selectCooksLoadError` — a recipe's cooks newest first, `{ count, lastCookedAt }` per recipe in one pass, a `Map` of the cooks, and the listener's error; the recipe drawer, the delete confirm, the plan builder and cooking mode read the summaries
- `selectNotesByRecipe`, `selectNoteSummaries`, `selectNotesById`, `selectNotesLoadError`, `selectTweakTracking(state, recipeId)` — a recipe's notes newest first, `{ count, lastTweakAt }` per recipe in one pass, a `Map` of the notes, the listener's error, and `'on' | 'full' | 'off'` for whether saving an edit records a tweak (a string, so the recipe form re-renders only when the answer changes)






- `selectContainers` (in stock, soonest eat-by first), `selectContainersById`, `selectStockByRecipe` and `selectStockByMeal` (`Map<id, { fridge, freezer, containers, nearestEatBy, fridgeEatBy, freezerEatBy }>`, servings per place and the nearest eat-by in each, from one pass), `selectStockByMealWithRecipes` (a saved meal's stock in whole plates, a `PlaceStock`), `selectContainersByCookId`, `selectContainersLoadError`, `selectIsContainersReady`; a row reads its own entry with `shallowEqual`
- `selectUsedIngredientIds` — the ingredients a recipe, a saved meal or an unfrozen planned day uses; Kitchen's "Yours" and "Needs a price" read it (rows no longer carry a "No price yet" line)
- `selectGaps(scope)` — scope-parameterized via a small factory; takes `showNutrition` and `dismissedNudges`
- Time-dependent selectors take `todayKey` as an argument (`useNow()` supplies it in the component)

**Tabs.** `PINCH_TABS` is `calendar`, `recipes`, `kitchen`, `shopping`, chosen by `?tab=` (Calendar, the default, has no parameter, so a reload or a deep link keeps the tab) and switched through a transition in `Pinch.tsx`, which mounts only the active tab's screen. `PinchNav` is a floating pill at the bottom of a phone, as wide as its four tabs (never stretched; below `sm` each tab is `min-w-16` with 10px of side padding so it fits 320px), and a rail on the left from `lg` up. The Shopping tab carries the count left to buy as a small `bg-primary` badge on its icon, capped at "99+", read by `ShoppingTabBadge` through its own selector so the nav, Kitchen and Calendar never re-render for a tick.

**Overlay coordination.** The orchestrator owns one discriminated-union state: `subview: null | PinchSubview` (`{ kind: 'products' }` (Stores & prices) `| { kind: 'sources' } | { kind: 'containers'; recipeId?; mealId? } | { kind: 'recipe'; id; method?; fromDrawer?; fromIdeaId?; fromNoteId? } | { kind: 'cook'; recipeId; servings?; plannedMealId?; plate? } | { kind: 'plan'; ... } | { kind: 'meal'; id }`, where `method` is the way a new recipe already chose (on a phone, or the link way for an idea that holds a link), `fromDrawer` says it was picked in the phone's start drawer, `fromIdeaId` is the idea a new recipe is being made from and `fromNoteId` the note an existing recipe is being updated from), exposed through a small context (`usePinchOverlay`) so a full-page screen can never open on top of another. The context also carries `showShoppingTab`, which the add toast's "View" calls (it switches to the Shopping tab only while no subview is open and no dialog is on top, so it never replaces a form holding typed work or closes a sheet under typed prices), and `isStartOpen` / `setIsStartOpen` for the phone's recipe start drawer, which `Pinch.tsx` renders once so that Recipes, Calendar and a way's back button can all raise it. It also carries `showCalendarDay(dayKey)`, which the plan toast's "View" calls: it does nothing while a subview or a drawer or sheet (`hasDialogOnTop`) is open, and otherwise remounts the Calendar on that day (a `key` and `focusDayKey`, cleared on the next tab change), sets `?view=week`, and moves `shownMonth` only when that day's week falls outside the loaded window; `openSubviewFromToast(subview)`, the same guard for a toast that opens a full-page screen (the new recipe's Plan it); and `openIdeaSheet`, which mounts the New idea sheet over any screen. `Pinch.tsx` also holds the tabs' session (`PinchSessionContext`, a `Map` provided around the tab body and cleared when the signed-in person changes): `usePinchSessionState(key, initial, override?)` is `useState` whose value is kept under `recipes:view|query|source|stock`, `kitchen:query|view|filters` and `calendar:anchor`, so a tab that unmounts on a switch opens as it was (a toast's day `focusDayKey` overrides the kept anchor); nothing goes in the URL. A per-tab scroll map is written by one passive scroll listener for the shown tab only (not while a non-overlay subview hides the tabs) and restored in a layout effect when the tab or the tabs' visibility changes. Re-tapping the active tab scrolls to the top, and at the top clears that tab's session keys and remounts it (`key`). `setActiveTab` pushes only when leaving the Calendar (remembering the router's `history.state.idx`), replaces between the other tabs, and steps back with `history.back()` when going to the Calendar from the entry right above it; a toast's "View" (`showShoppingTab`, `showCalendarDay`) pushes only when it leaves the Calendar and replaces when already on a tab, like `setActiveTab`. The other drawers and sheets (day, planned meal, recipe, meal, ingredient, the calendar's summary, fill-in) are opened from the screen that owns them. The recipe form's own editors (the title, prep and cook and description drawers of `SummaryEditor`, and on a phone the ingredient, step and nutrition drawers, one level deep) are the drawers allowed over a subview, and so is a `HelpTip`'s small modal, which is how the meal builder's cost warning opens on a phone or any touch screen. A drawer's action that opens a form closes the drawer first; the in-place "swap" screens (planned meal, save-as-meal) are components inside the drawer, not new overlays.



---

## Component Encapsulation

- **Screens read the store; presentation components take props.** `CalendarScreen`, `RecipesScreen` and `KitchenScreen` use `useAppSelector`; rows, cells and badges are pure.
- **App-aware pieces stay in the app** (`DayCell`, `MonthView`, `WeekView`, `SummaryLine`, `WeekSummarySheet`, `MealChip`, `StoreLogo`, `GapNudge`, `LeftOutTip`, `RecipeStart` (and its phone drawer), `RecipeReader`, `RecipeBasics`, `RecipeCareField`, `RecipeCareSection`, `RecipeIngredients`, `RecipeSteps`, `NutritionReview`, `MealBuilder`, `ListViewControls` with `useListViewOptions`, `CookingSubview` and its step, ingredients and timer pieces with `useCookTimers`, the idea pieces `IdeasView`, `IdeaRow`, `IdeaThumbnail`, `IdeaDrawer`, `IdeaFormSheet`, `IdeaAddSheet`, `IdeaWays` and `IdeaNoteField`, and the note pieces `RecipeNotes`, `NoteEntry`, `ChangeList`, `NoteForm` and `NoteSheet` with `useCookNote`); anything whose props mention no kitchen entity comes from `src/components`, `src/hooks` or Dreamer UI: `SearchInput`, `PillGroup`, `PickOrCreate`, `SectionDivider`, `MoneyInput`, `FormSheet`, `Subview`, `CopyButton`, and the ones added with the redesign: `MacroStats` (with `src/utils/macroUtils.ts`), `HelpTip` (`variant='warning'`), `MethodPicker`, `FormSection`, `forms/Field`, `Stepper`, `FilterBar`, `PeriodSwitcher`, `ViewOptions`, `useLocalStorageState`, `SummaryEditor`, `UndoSnackbar`, `useUndoRemoval`, `useWakeLock` and `durationUtils` (`findDurations`, `formatDuration`, `formatClock`), `TickMark` (round 7), and for voice commands `useSpeechCommands`, `speechUtils` (`speakText`, `pickVoice`, `primeSpeech`) and `Subview`'s `headerAction`.
- **Shared pieces and their rules.** `MethodPicker` is rows only: every `Method` carries its own `onPick`, and what happens next (a screen, a drawer closing) is the caller's (a list of one quiet row is how a reading screen offers "Write it yourself"). `IntroCard` and `FilePickCard` carry no kitchen words: the first is a tinted block with a chip, a lead sentence and a quieter one, the second a place to choose or drop one file (its title is its action, one icon) that shows the file it holds and a line under it; the app supplies the sentences and the picked file's emoji. `Field` wraps its control in a native `<label>`, so a `Select` is not put inside one; `isCompact` gives it small captions for a grid of short fields, and `COMPACT_INPUT_CLASS` makes the input 40 px with a smaller placeholder while typed text stays 16 px so iOS doesn't zoom on focus. `ViewOptions` is the eye button (a drawer on a phone with Done in its footer, and Reset beside it once an option differs from its default; a popover from `sm`), tinted while customized, over `ViewOptionSwitch` rows (Pinch's `ListViewControls` puts "Nutrition, everywhere" first and keeps its macros switch enabled, with a hint while no metric is switched on; `isQuiet` is the Calendar's plain 40px eye, whose `NutritionViewOptions` holds the nutrition section, "On the calendar" (Show stats, Show prices) and About); `useLocalStorageState(key, default, parse)` is a `useSyncExternalStore` read of one `localStorage` key shared by every component using it, with a memory copy that is read first (so a write shows at once even when storage refuses it) and dropped when another tab changes the key (the `storage` event), and `parse` rejecting a stored value of the wrong shape. `SummaryRow` is that row without the editor: caption, value (an optional muted detail line), chevron, in the framed `SummaryEditor`'s hairline frame, for a sheet that swaps its own content to change the value instead of opening a second drawer (Add to shopping's Days, the put-away sheet's Putting away; each registers its swapped view with `useBackStep`); with no `onClick` it is a plain unframed line. `ActionChips` is also the one quiet chip for a state action (Reset, Tick all, Clear what you got; `isDestructive` is the red hairline one), never a link. `SummaryEditor` is one form field as a summary row (caption, one or two lines of value, a chevron, a red problem line) that opens its editor: a `Drawer` over the form below `sm` or on a screen under 600px tall (so a phone held sideways never gets a popover that runs off the bottom), a `Popover` under the row from `sm` up (the row is stretched over the popover's inline-block wrapper, the popover is open-state-only content), each with a full-width Done; the editor's first field is focused after a short timer (350 ms for a drawer, which takes focus while it slides in, 30 ms for the popover, which focuses its own container first), and Enter in a one-line field closes the editor and is swallowed so it never submits the surrounding form. An open `Popover` is a `[role=dialog]`, so `Subview`'s Escape handler and `useSubviewHistory`'s back-gesture handler both leave the form alone while one is open, and the gesture is re-sent to the popover as an Escape. `useUndoRemoval` + `UndoSnackbar` are the removal's undo: `showUndo(message, restore)` keeps one pending removal (a newer call replaces it), counts down from 6 by a deadline (so a throttled tab doesn't stretch it), and `restore` reads the latest rows through a ref because the form's value can change before Undo is pressed; `UndoSnackbar` is always mounted (a persistent `role=status` region says the message once, without the countdown) and draws a fixed bar that measures the nearest visible `.form-footer` and sits 12px above it, or 1rem from the bottom when there is none (it re-measures when the footer resizes). `PillGroup` never reorders what is visible: a chosen option that was hidden behind "Show all" takes the last visible slot, and the fully open list is in its original order; a pick-one group shows no "1 selected" count, a pick-several group still does. `FilterBar`'s apply button on a phone reads `doneLabel` ("Show 12 ingredients") and, at 360px and under, the optional `compactDoneLabel` ("Show 12"). `AddFieldChips` are at least 36px tall (`min-h-9`) and a long label wraps instead of clipping. `DetailSheet` takes two optional, additive props: `actions` (icon buttons drawn on the title's line; the sheet then draws its own heading, which names the dialog itself because Dreamer UI's `aria-labelledby` points at a heading it only draws for a string title, and from `sm` its own 40×40 close beside the actions in place of the modal's corner ✕) and `viewKey` (the content's current view: a new one starts at the top, a view comes back where it was scrolled, and a `ResizeObserver` holds the content at the tallest height it has had, so a short view doesn't drop the drawer under the person's finger). A sheet that passes neither renders as before.
- **The back gesture, one layer at a time (`src/hooks/useBackHistory.ts`).** One module-level stack of layers shares one `popstate` listener, attached when the module loads (so landing on an entry kept from before a reload steps on instead of costing a press, and a reload with layers open no longer leaves extra presses): a full-page subview (`usePageHistory`, behind `useSubviewHistory`), a drawer, modal or panel (`useSheetHistory`, used by `DetailSheet`, `FormSheet`, and the phone drawers of `ViewOptions`, `FilterBar` and `SortControl`), and a swapped-in "‹ Back" step inside one (`useBackStep(isActive, onBack)`: Pinch's planned meal, "Name this meal", Eat from stock, the box sheet's inner views, Add to shopping's Days calendar, the put-away sheet's search, a cook's edit view, a cook's note view, New idea's ways, the recipe and saved meal drawers' views). Escape steps back the same way: one capture-phase `keydown` listener, attached with the `popstate` one, takes Escape when the top layer is a step inside a drawer or modal, with no dialog above that sheet (`getDialogsAbove`), no open select or menu where focus is and no tooltip showing, and calls that step's `onBack` before the sheet's own Escape listener can close it (a step inside a subview's page is left to the subview). Each open layer owns one history entry (`backLayerKey`, plus a clock-based `backLayerSeq` that orders entries across reloads) copied from the router's state, so the router sees the same location. On back, every layer whose entry was popped gets its `onBack` (a sheet's `onClose`) inside `flushSync`; a layer still open afterwards (a subview that stepped back inside itself or asked "Keep editing / Discard", "Stop timers and leave?") gets its entry back. A `HelpTip`'s modal (a phone or any touch screen) is a layer through `useSheetHistory`, so back on a tab closes the help note instead of leaving the tab. When a dialog that isn't a layer (a destructive confirm, a raw Dreamer UI drawer) sits above the top layer, the entry is restored and that dialog gets an Escape instead. Closing a layer any other way (✕, swipe, a button, Escape) steps back off its now-dead entry after a tick; a layer that opens while the current entry is dead (a drawer closing as a subview opens, Strict Mode's remount) reuses it with `replaceState`; a layer that opens while that step back is in flight waits for it; and landing on a dead entry steps back once more, so a stale entry never costs a press. A dialog layer that closes takes its steps with it. An entry left by the page before a reload is made plain on load. `useSheetHistory` also gives the dialog an `onClose` that ignores an Escape meant for a dialog opened above it (`getDialogsAbove` in `src/utils/dialogUtils.ts`, which leaves out a subview's own layer and a drawer sliding shut), so Escape and back both close the confirm first. `Subview`'s optional `onBack` is still what its header back, an overlay subview's Escape and the gesture call (`onBack ?? onClose`). Escape is captured and ignored while a drawer or modal other than the subview's own layer is open (`hasDialogOnTop`).
- **`parseMoneyToCents`, `formatCents` and `centsToInputValue` move to `src/utils/moneyUtils.ts`** in the first Pinch PR that needs them (A-List keeps them in `apps/a-list/utils/money.ts` today); A-List's imports are updated in the same PR, per the "a second mini-app needs it" rule.
- **A quantity/unit select is built once** as `UnitSelect` from `UNIT_LABELS`; the ingredient row, the product form and the fill-in sheet all use it (`triggerClassName` sizes its button where it sits beside a compact input).
- **The shopping sheets stay with their screen.** `AddToShoppingSheet` (a `FormSheet`) takes a `ShoppingRequest` (`{ kind: 'recipe', recipeId }`, `{ kind: 'items', items, label }` or `{ kind: 'range', startKey, endKey }`, a day being a range of one) and is mounted by `RecipesScreen` and `CalendarScreen`, which close the drawer before opening it; the recipe and saved meal drawers open it from an "Add to shopping list" row in ⋯ More and the day drawer and the calendar's summary sheet from `AddToShoppingRow`, a tinted row after the meals (a planned meal shops through its day). `ShoppingAddSheet` renders the same body (`ShoppingPick`) for "From your plan", with no View action on its toast. Its toast's "View" calls `showShoppingTab` from the overlay context, and `AppToast` renders an action button for any toast that gives one (and dismisses the toast when it is tapped). `showShoppingTab` switches to `?tab=shopping` through the same search-param transition the nav uses, but only when no subview is open at tap time (read through a ref the orchestrator keeps current, with the search taken from `window.location`, since the toast holds a function from an older render): a recipe form holding typed work is never replaced, and the tap then only dismisses the toast. A form sheet open over a tab is not checked. The list's row sheet and add sheet are `FormSheet`s opened from `ShoppingScreen` over the tab, and `ItemSearch` takes a `resultsClassName` so the add sheet caps its results and only the list scrolls, and `isDisabled` so a caller can lock the results while a write is in flight.
- **Cooks' pieces.** The recipe and saved meal drawers (`RecipeDrawer`, `MealDrawer`) put nothing between their facts and their subject. Their other content is a view swapped in place (`DrawerView`: `recipe | photo | history | more` in `RecipeDrawer`, `isMoreOpen` in `MealDrawer`), each starting with a local `BackLink` that registers `useBackStep`, so the back gesture and Escape step back first; the main surface stays mounted and hidden (`hidden`), so its own state (an opened description) survives, and `DetailSheet`'s `viewKey` brings each view back where it was scrolled and never lets the sheet shrink between views. The doors are 40×40 icon buttons with `aria-label`, `title` and `aria-expanded`, tinted with `bg-accent` while open and toggling back on a second tap: `DetailSheet`'s `actions` puts 🕘 (Notes and history) on the title's line (on a phone the heading drops 8px so it sits 12px clear of the drawer's own ✕ above it; from `sm` the sheet's Close follows the actions after a 12px gap, so the last door is never next to the button that closes the sheet), and ⋯ More sits in the footer between Modify and the primary (`max-sm:px-3` on both, so "Start cooking" stays one line at 320). Back moves focus to the door that opened the view. Notes and history is the Cooked `GroupRow` (opens the history) and `RecipeNotes`; More owns the drawer, so it is built from the roomy variant of the shared rows (`isRoomy` on `ActionGroup`, `ActionRow` and `GroupRow`: a 40px round `bg-accent` icon chip, a 16px medium title, a 14px muted line, `min-h-16`, a chevron for a row that opens something via `hasChevron`, in an untinted hairline frame; the compact rows stay where rows sit beside other content). A captioned `ActionGroup` ("What would you like to do?") of `ActionRow`s with `isGrouped` (Start cooking or Plan it, Mark as cooked without a chevron since it records at once, `AddToShoppingRow` with `isRoomy`, Put some away), with the reasons a row is off as muted 14px lines under it, comes first; `StockRows` follows it and draws its own captioned `ActionGroup` ("In your fridge and freezer", a roomy `GroupRow` per place with the due badge under the words; a row passes its place, so Fridge & freezer opens on it). A captioned group hides, caption included, while it has no rows (`has-[>[data-action-rows]:empty]:hidden`). In both sizes a row's text sits beside its icon slot on one edge, and keyboard focus draws its ring 2px inside the row (`focus-visible:-outline-offset-2`) because the frame's `overflow-hidden` clipped an outside ring to its bottom edge. `ActionChips` (`src/components`, no app words: a wrapped row of 40px hairline buttons, each an icon, a short label and a `description` that is its `aria-label` and hover title) now carries only a planned recipe item's "Mark as cooked". The planned meal view (`PlannedMealView`) has the same anatomy inside the day drawer: ‹ Back, the meal's name as an `h3` (the same heading on its More view, over the same captioned roomy group and a lone roomy red Remove row), "On the plate", then the Cost tile and macro tiles; its footer (portaled into the day drawer's) is Modify, ⋯ More and the primary, and its views (`PlannedMealSubview`: `meal | more | name | eat`, the eat view remembering whether it came from the meal's primary or from More) are state the day drawer holds, so `DetailSheet`'s `viewKey` (`day`, or `{mealId}:{view}`) keeps each view's scroll and the sheet's height; the meal surface stays mounted and hidden under More and the views reached from it, each view's `BackLink` registers `useBackStep`, and Back focuses what opened the view (the ⋯, the More row, the primary, or the day's row for that meal). `ActionRow` (a tinted row with an icon, a label and a line under it, red with `isDestructive` for the one row that removes something; `ActionGroup` wraps related rows, `ActionRow`s or `GroupRow`s, in one `bg-muted/50` block with `divide-y`) is for a sheet or view whose content is its actions (the container sheet, the note sheet, a drawer's More) and for a lone row at the end of a drawer (the day drawer's "Add to shopping list", "Price this day with today's prices"); `AddToShoppingRow` is the thin wrapper for the day drawer's and the drawers' More rows (`isGrouped` passes through). A planned meal's "Mark as cooked" is `PlannedCookRow`, an `ActionChips` entry on its own recipe's item of the plate (and "(✓) Cooked · Not cooked" once marked). `useCookActions(uid)` holds what marking does and says (the add, the toast with Undo, the "Already marked" notice); `markRecipeCooked` takes `{ isToasted: false }` and `markPlannedRecipeCooked` defaults to no toast, so cooking mode, whose panel confirms itself, raises none. `RepriceDayRow` (calendar) is the tinted "Price this day with today's prices" row in the day drawer (no help icon: its confirm previews the result; it renders nothing when pricing the day again would change nothing). `PlannedCookRow` (calendar) is one recipe's row; the note pieces live in `components/notes/` (`ClampedText` and `NoteTextField` are in `src/components`, since neither names a recipe: the first clamps text at four lines with More / Less, the second is a labelled text box with a counter near its `maxLength` that is focused once a sheet has settled; `ActionRow` is the "Add a note" row; `FormSheet`, `PillGroup`, `DeleteIconButton` and `ModalFooterActions` are the sheet), `NoteForm` serves the sheet and the cooking history's in-place view, and `useCookNote(uid)` holds the note typed on cooking mode's last screen (its text, saving and discarding), whose `hasDraft` is what `leave()` and Cancel ask about before discarding it; `CookingHistorySheet` and `CookEditView` (recipes) are the history, and `RecipesScreen` mounts the sheet after closing the drawer, like the other sheets.



- **Ideas' shared pieces.** `PhotoPicker` gained `uploadLabel`, `changeLabel` and an optional `onRemove` (no `onRemove`, no Remove button) so a photo that is required can say "Replace photo"; `src/lib/pageContent`'s `fetchPageContent` and `pageContentQueryOptions` take an optional `{ timeoutMs }` that sets the callable's timeout and isn't part of the query key; and the link helpers `toWebLink`, `getWebLinkHost` and `getComparableUrl` are in `src/utils/urlUtils.ts`, none of them knowing about ideas (`utils/ideas.ts` in the app holds the idea-shaped ones: `findIdeaByUrl`, `toIdeaUrl`, `toIdeaImageUrl`, `getIdeaDetail`). All are covered by `npm run check:pinch-math`.
- **Closed means unmounted:** drawers' heavy bodies (the recipe drawer's step list, the summary sheet's days, the store list) render only while open.

## UI Component Conventions Applied

- Forms: `FormSheet` for the ingredient, price and store forms and for the recipe ideas' add sheet (the list of ways, then a way's own view in its place) and edit sheet; `Subview` for the recipe form (behind the start list and the reading screens) and meal builder (`MealBuilder` renders its own `Subview`; `MealSubview` and `PlanMealSubview` are thin wrappers) and the fridge and freezer list, Stores & prices and the sources list (the shopping list is a tab, not a subview); the source form and the shopping list's row and add sheets are `FormSheet`s (a drawer on a phone, a modal from `sm`); `DetailSheet` for day, planned meal, recipe, meal and ingredient details; `ModalFooterActions` with Cancel left, a `DeleteIconButton` beside it on edit, the primary alone on the right.
- Titles are plain nouns ("Recipe", "Price", "Store", "Meal"); the verb is on the primary button and the destructive confirm.
- `PickOrCreate` for family, store and a recipe's source (its optional `maxLength` caps the typed label at 80, and `isFocusedOnOpen={false}` keeps the field from taking focus when the form opens with it filled in); `PillGroup` for meal type, the plan builder's day strip and "Your recent recipes and saved meals", the Month / Week switch and the Recipes / Saved meals / Ideas and Yours / All switches (`isThin`); `FilterBar` for the Kitchen ingredient and Stores & prices filters (its `trailing` slot carries the View options button; Kitchen always has it, and Recipes uses `FilterBar` once a source exists or something is put away, with the same button in `trailing`, and otherwise puts the button beside its Recipes / Saved meals pills while the shown list has something in it); `FormSection` and `Field` lay out the recipe form, whose sections are all visible (no "+ Add X" chips) and whose row editors are drawers on a phone and in place from `sm`; `AppToggle` for the four nutrition switches (the top of every View options sheet) and the View options switches. Cooking mode's timers are cards of its own (`CookStepTimer`), so Pinch no longer uses `SuggestionChips`.
- Help and warning icons are `HelpTip`, and the `noModal` prop no longer exists: on a phone (below `sm`) or on any touch screen, whatever its width (`(hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)` doesn't match), the icon always opens a small modal ("Got it", with a hairline border); with a mouse from `sm` up it is a tooltip rendered in a portal on `document.body`, which `HelpTip` places itself (its `placement` side, flipped when that side has no room, clamped to the viewport, the arrow aligned with the icon) and measures again for about 24 animation frames after it opens, so a drawer still sliding in doesn't leave it misplaced. The tooltip opens on mouse hover (pointer events, mouse only) and on keyboard focus (`:focus-visible` with Tab as the key before it, so not for a dialog's initial focus after Enter) and closes on leave and blur; a click opens it and keeps it open instead of toggling. Escape is captured by whatever is open, a tooltip or the modal, so only it closes and the next Escape is the drawer's or the subview's (`Subview` leaves Escape alone while a tooltip is on screen or focus is in an open dropdown). A `linkLabel` opens the modal at every size. The modal's dismiss button says "Got it" unless the caller passes `dismissLabel`; the Shopping tab's cost help says "OK", since "Got it" is the other half of its list.


- Recipes' Source filter is one pick-one `PillGroup` in a `FilterBar` (a drawer on a phone, a popover from `sm`) with no link to the Sources screen (the 📖 icon in the header is its one door); its active chips are `LabelChip`s (`@/components/LabelChip`, also the recipe drawer's source pill, which is a plain label when the source holds no other recipe, and Fridge & freezer's "Only …" chip): the pills are the sources some listed recipe has, with their counts, plus "Any" and, when needed, "No source · n"; the chip reads "From {label}" (or "No source"); an empty result offers "Clear filter". The question shows once any source exists; there is no Sources chip (the header icon opens the list, and a row there shows that source's recipes). Kitchen's two doors are `EntryTile`s (`@/components/EntryTile`: `icon`, `label`, an optional `status` in words and `ariaLabel`, no count) in a two-column grid inside a `nav` named "Also in Kitchen" (no visible caption) under the filter bar, not rendered while a filter or search narrows the list; a tile is a container-query component, stacked (icon chip over the label, chevron top right) while it is narrower than 15rem and lying flat from there, and one alone spans the grid below `lg`.

- Kitchen's price filters read `defaultProducts` and `usedIds` (`applyIngredientFilters`): "Needs a price" is used by something and has no default product, "Has a price" has one, so both only ever match Yours even with All picked. The filter panel says so, the count beside Yours / All is "N of M" with M counting Yours while a price filter is on, typing with Yours picked reads "N found in all ingredients", "Add prices" hands `shown` to `FillInSheet` as `NO_PRODUCT` gaps (it ranks them itself), and the empty state offers "Look through everything" only when All would find something.
- Rows are top-aligned (`items-start`): `RecipeRow`, `MealRow` and the calendar's `MealRow` put the image or tile's top edge level with the title's and keep the chevron on the first line; `IngredientRow` and the Stores & prices rows do the same with the chevron and, on Stores & prices, the price and its unit price.
- A `StatTile` with a help icon keeps `pr-6` clear of it on a phone (the icon sits in the tile's corner); the summary's figures from $1,000 up drop their cents through `formatPeriodCost`, so a long total stays clear of the icon. A row of three (the Calendar's stats) passes `isLabelWrapped`, which lets a label wrap under its number and keeps the `pr-6` on the number's line only, so a long label doesn't shrink to make room for an icon beside the number above it; every other tile keeps its one-line label.
- The recipe step drawer focuses its text box about 350 ms after it opens, once the drawer has finished sliding in, rather than with `autoFocus`.
- Lists search from `LIST_SEARCH_THRESHOLD` rows; Kitchen groups by family with `SectionDivider` and mounts a family group only near the viewport (`LazyMount`); the shopping tab groups by shop section (dividers from 8 rows) and mounts each section's rows in chunks of 12 near the viewport.
- Images from URLs (a recipe cover, a store logo) are `FallbackImage` with a themed fallback (a tinted emoji tile, a letter tile).
- Delete confirmations go through `useActionModal().confirm` with `destructive: true` and say what archiving means when something is in use.

## Theming

- `src/apps/pinch/pinch.css` overrides Dreamer UI's semantic tokens on `html.pinch-theme`: paper-cream (warm black in dark) surfaces with hairline borders, an espresso-ink `--color-primary` for the one filled action, see-through secondary buttons, peach tints (`--color-accent`, `--pinch-drizzle-soft`) for chips, groups and the selected pill, and warm orange (`--pinch-drizzle`) as the accent (today dot and the Week's "Today" pill, selected outline, active tab, the default price's star); `--radius-md` and up raised for a rounded counter feel; field text inset; hairline option pills that take a peach tint and a drizzle outline when selected; tertiary buttons use the normal text colour. `usePinchTheme()` adds the class to `<html>` only while the app is mounted.
- **Round ticks.** Every Pinch checkbox is Dreamer UI's `Checkbox` (a `button[role=checkbox]`), made round by one rule on `html.pinch-theme`: radius 9999px, a 1.5px `--color-muted-foreground` ring, and when ticked or mixed a `--color-primary` fill (with `!important`, because the component sets its background inline) and a `--color-primary-foreground` tick; keyboard focus keeps a primary outline (offset 2px, round); an `::after` (`inset: -11.5px`) makes each 20×20 box a 40×40 target. No other app is touched (the class is only on `<html>` while Pinch is mounted, and stays there under every Pinch overlay: a confirm and a `HelpTip` modal included). A mark that says something is done (a cooked meal or recipe) is the shared `TickMark` (`src/components`: a `bg-primary` circle with a `text-primary-foreground` tick, sized in `em` to its text, `aria-hidden` beside words that say it), never the ✅ emoji, which draws a green square tick box. Round 7's census drove every screen, sheet and state that can hold a tick at 390 and 1280, light and dark: the four `Checkbox` call sites (`ShoppingRow`, `AddToShoppingSheet` with its "From your plan" twin, `ProductFormSheet`'s "Make this the default", `CookIngredientsView`) were already round (radius 9999px, 20×20, a 43×43 target, ticked, unticked and disabled), no `FormFactories.checkbox`, `checkboxGroup`, `input[type=checkbox]` or `menuitemcheckbox` exists in Pinch or the shared pieces it renders (a `Select`'s chosen option is a bare ✓), and the square ones were the ✅ marks in the Week row, the day drawer, the planned meal and the recipe drawer.
- Meal-type colours are tokens, not raw palette classes (`--pinch-meal-breakfast … --pinch-meal-drink`, each with a `-soft` tint for the calendar's chips and pills), checked on a chip and a pill in light and dark.
- The Pinch rules file is written in the same PR as the shell: `.github/instructions/pinch.instructions.md` with an identical `.claude/rules/pinch.md` (metaphor: a warm kitchen; palette roles; shape language; what the emoji mean; calendar chip and pill anatomy; nudge wording; missing details appear only as in-context nudge lines).

---

## Security & Privacy Requirements

- **Private by construction.** A person's kitchen is readable and writable only by them. There is no admin read; Security Rules Design Criteria #1–2 are the whole access model. Nothing in the MVP is shared or public.
- **What leaves the app, and where it goes.** (1) Pasted text or a chosen file (a photo, screenshot or PDF) goes to Firebase AI (Gemini) to be read into a recipe draft; the provider sees the content, not the person's identity beyond Firebase's own request metadata, and the screen says an AI reads it and that it isn't saved. A reading can hand back how long a dish keeps and how to reheat it, but only what the source says, and a recipe's own storage notes are never sent anywhere. (2) A recipe link goes to our `fetchPageContent` function, which fetches the page from Google's servers (signed-in only, http/https only, private networks refused) and returns only what Pinch asks for (the page's text, its title and meta description, and any Recipe data) without storing anything; the client then sends that content, not the link, to the AI reader. (3) Store logos are requested by the person's browser from the logo service by domain only (well-known stores only); a person's own stores never leave Storage. (4) A recipe idea's link goes to the same `fetchPageContent`, asking for the page's `meta` only (its title, site name and picture address); nothing is read by an AI model, and what is stored from the answer is those three. When the idea is shown, the person's browser requests its picture address from that website (through `FallbackImage`, with no referrer). (5) While cooking mode's voice control is on, the person's browser sends what the microphone hears, other people's voices included, to its own speech service to turn it into text (Chrome's goes to Google, Safari's to Apple, Edge's to Microsoft); Pinch only receives the text, matches it against its commands and keeps none of it, and the first tap says so before the browser asks for the microphone. Answers and, when asked, steps are read aloud by the browser's voice, which prefers one on the device; where the browser only has a network voice, the text read goes to that voice's vendor, and the first tap says that too. Nothing else leaves: there is no copy-out of recipes, plans, ingredient lists, ideas or the shopping list.
- **The logo service's key is a publishable browser token**, domain-restricted at the provider, kept in an env var (`VITE_STORE_LOGO_TOKEN`) and sent only in the logo request's URL. It is not a secret and is not a Functions secret; if the chosen provider needs a secret, the logo route moves behind an `onCall` instead.
- **Stored data minimization.** No location, no payment details, no card data, no receipt images are stored. A product stores store, price, size and an optional link; a recipe stores what the person typed or reviewed (its storage notes are days and two lines of their own words, at most 300 characters each), and a source is only a label they typed and a cook only which recipe, when and how many servings, and a note only the person's own words (up to 2,000 characters), the cook it follows and, for a tweak, up to eight short lines naming what they changed in the recipe (ingredient names, amounts, and the first words of a step).
- **Persistence on the device.** Nothing from the person's own documents is persisted beyond Firestore's own cache. No TanStack query in the MVP carries `meta: { persist: true }`; the catalog is code, not a query. The one thing Pinch writes to the browser itself is each list's View options, as small JSON in `localStorage` under `pinch:{uid}:view:{recipes|ingredients|calendar}` (Recipes and Saved meals share the first; the Calendar's holds `showStats`, the tile row under the summary line, and `showPrices`, the price on each Week meal row, through `useListViewOptions(uid, 'calendar')`; a choice stored before `showStats` existed, a non-boolean, and round 7's `showAverages` all read as off, and the old key is dropped by the next write), and the calendar's view under `pinch:{uid}:calendar-view` (`"month"` or `"week"`): display choices and no kitchen data; the tabs' session (scroll, pill, search, period) lives in memory only (`PinchSessionContext`); the key carries the uid, so someone else signing in on the same browser doesn't read it, and nothing clears it on sign-out. Cooking mode writes three booleans for voice commands, `pinch:{uid}:voice:agreed` (the explainer was answered with Turn on), `pinch:{uid}:voice:read-aloud` and `pinch:{uid}:voice:wake` (listen for "Hey Pinch" rather than one window per tap; the fifteen-minute lock is never stored), through `useLocalStoragePreference`; it never stores what was heard.
- **Links are https-only** and shown with `ExternalLinkText`. An idea's link may be http (a page that has no https version still opens) but only http(s), never `javascript:` or `data:` (the field, `createIdea` and the rules all check), and it opens in a new tab with `rel='noopener noreferrer'`; its picture address must be https, in the client and in the rules, and only `FallbackImage` ever loads it.
- **What a recipe idea stores, field by field.** `title`, `note` (the person's own words, shown only to them), `kind`, `url` (the link they pasted, without sign-in details), `siteName`, `imageUrl` and the page's title when the person kept it, `photoPath` (their own picture in their own Storage folder), `recipeId` and the two times. No location, no author, no page text or description. Nothing about an idea is shared, public or copied out.
- **What a note stores, field by field.** `recipeId`, `kind`, `text` (the person's own words, shown only to them), `changes` (lines the app composed from the recipe the person already owns: an ingredient's name, an amount, a time, a step's first 80 characters), `cookId` and the two times. No location, no author, no photo. A note is never shared, sent to an AI or put in a link, and it isn't in any push or email; deleting a recipe deletes its notes.
- **What a container stores, field by field.** `title` (a recipe's or meal's name, or what the person typed), `recipeId`, `mealId` and `cookId` (ids of their own documents), `servings`, `place`, `status`, `eatBy` (a day), `history` (what happened and when: kinds, instants, portions, a place, and an EDIT's note of two dates) and the timestamps. Nothing about money, location, other people or food safety, and nothing leaves the app: there is no copy, export, share or push of a container.
- **Rules are verified against the emulator, not by the UI hiding a control:** the owner allowed and a second signed-in user denied on every path (kitchen document and all thirteen collections, plus Storage), signed-out denied; and one denied write per integrity rule (snapshot overwrite without the re-price marker, a re-price that also changes the meal, `priceCents` negative, `sizeAmount` 0, an unknown `unit`, a `date` that isn't UTC midnight, a 61st recipe ingredient, an `http://` link).

- **Terms and licences are a gate, not a footnote:** the USDA FoodData Central licence (catalog nutrition) and the logo service's terms and attribution are each confirmed in the PR that ships the thing that uses them, with the logo credit in "About the numbers", the last line of every View options sheet. Pinch vendors no other third-party asset since the ingredient icons were removed.

---

## Performance

- **Scale fixture.** `npm run seed:pinch-scale` (`scripts/seeds/pinchScale.ts`, opt-in) fills Taylor's kitchen: 300 custom ingredients across 40 families, 150 products across 12 stores, 200 recipes of 3–8 ingredients (about seven in eight with one of 12 sources, one of them with a 73-character label, the first holding the most), 60 saved meals, about 1,100 planned meals from 45 days back to 45 days ahead (12 a day), a shopping list of 80 items across 25 families (every fifth one checked off; the 48 weighed ones say what they are for, from the planned meals of the next 14 days that use them, a few saved meals and two recipes, one added at twice its servings and one in the older shape with no servings, with amounts that add up to those shares: up to 20 sources a row, 155 on the list, a recipe planned on two days) and 80 recipe ideas (two links in three, a third of the links with a picture address that can't load offline, every seventh title over 100 characters, every ninth with a long note, every tenth already made into a recipe). Every list or per-row feature is opened on it at phone width (390×844) with the dev build throttled 4× (CDP `Emulation.setCPUThrottlingRate`), switching Calendar ↔ Recipes ↔ Kitchen. The numbers from the last pass are in the PR that added it.
- **Collections at scale.** A kitchen of 456 recipes with a collection of 450 of them (Jamie's, on the emulator; Taylor's seed gets twelve collections over its 200 recipes when it is reseeded), 4× CPU throttle: picking the 441-recipe collection in the filter took about 570 ms to show its chip and rows, a 2-recipe one about 240 ms, back to Any about 520 ms, and a tab switch there and back stayed under a second beyond the helper's own wait; 20 rows are mounted when the list opens (the chunks mount near the viewport). The filter's options, the counts and the delete's chunk list are one pass over the recipes each, nothing runs per row, and a recipe row has no collection chip (they are in the drawer). Deleting that collection ran three transactions (200, 200 and 50 recipes) in 15 s on the loaded emulator and reported 450.
- **Never freeze the same meal twice, and freeze in batches.** The load-time freeze of ~540 past meals took the page down twice over: a write per meal raised one snapshot event each, and the list of unfrozen meals shrinking after each event started another freeze of the rest (quadratic writes, most of them refused by the write-once rule). It is now one batch per 100 meals (one event each), each meal is submitted once per visit (`useFreezePastDays`), and a refused batch falls back to a write per meal.
- **One pass, then lookups.** `selectRecipeCosts`, `selectPeriodStats` and `selectSourceCounts` compute in one walk and are memoized; no cell, row or drawer recomputes a recipe's cost. The Calendar's `renderCell` reads `selectPlannedByDay` only.
- **Build once.** The currency `Intl.NumberFormat` and the catalog lookup maps are module-level; the search index is built on first use.
- **Cap what's drawn.** A row is as tall as its lines (no `min-h` under a lone title), so the height estimates follow the lines: `FamilyGroup` adds up 20px for the divider plus 45px per ingredient (44 plus its rule) and 16px for each of the price and nutrition lines that will show, and the recipe list adds a price and a macro line to its cover-sized rows; both are within about 1% of what is measured at 390px. Kitchen mounts a family group near the viewport and the recipe list mounts twenty rows at a time (`LazyMount`); recipe and ingredient pickers show the top matches; the summary sheet lists one period's days. A calendar cell builds a hover popover only on a computer, and the card's content only while it is open; a phone's cell is just its chips. A week's agenda shows six rows a day then "Show N more", and Stores & prices caps its rows at 60 with "Show 60 more". The `LazyMount` height estimates follow the View options (a row with its price, macro or stock line hidden is shorter, and a hidden stock line adds nothing).
- **What is not ours.** A cold first load of the fixture (about 1,900 documents) spends most of its time inside the Firestore SDK ingesting them into its local cache, and so does paging far enough to move the planned-meals window (the listener restarts). Both are measured in the PR and left alone; later visits read the persistent cache.
- **Keep the tap instant.** Each tab renders in a transition and is wrapped in `memo`; the day and recipe drawers read from the store at open, not from their parents.
- **The shopping list at scale.** Groups are built in one pass by a selector, a row is a `memo` component with plain-value props and a group is a `memo` component with a comparison over its rows' ids, names, amounts, units and checked flags, so a tick re-renders the group the row left and its own row and not the other groups, a section's rows mount in chunks of 12 near the viewport (the first chunk of the first three sections at once), and a section draws 100 rows. The count left to buy is read by the tab's badge and by the Shopping screen only, through their own selectors, so Calendar, Recipes and Kitchen never subscribe to a tick (a tab mounts only while it is the active one). The sheets that add a recipe's or a day's lines are measured with the fixture's busiest day (77 lines): one scrolling list, the header and footer fixed. Checked on the fixture at 390×844 with the dev build at 4× CPU: opening the list, a tick, opening the add sheet from a recipe and from a busy day, and the three tab switches; the numbers are in the PR. With the list's sources (round 7 fixes), the names work over three Calendar → Shopping switches at 4× took 42 ms of self time before each row's line was worked out once per change of names and 13 ms after; the tab switch itself is around 300 ms either way at this size, since the names were a small part of it.
- **A tick was slow, and why.** Measured on a kitchen with 300 ingredients, 150 products and an 80-item list (16 ticked) at 390×844 with the dev build at 4× CPU, click event to the next painted frame: a tick took a median of about 595 ms over 24 ticks (435 to 785 ms) while the list was a subview. A count of component renders per tick (a stub React DevTools hook that tallies the fibers that rendered) found 294 to 319: Kitchen re-rendered with its 40 family groups because it read the open count and stays mounted under a subview, and every shopping group re-rendered because the selector builds new group objects on each snapshot, so `memo` on the group never held. The fixes: the count lives in the tab's badge and the list screen only, the list is a tab (so nothing else is mounted while it is open), and a group compares its rows. Now a tick renders 24 to 51 components and takes a median of about 150 ms (87 to 240 ms, 8 ticks). Switching into the Shopping tab takes 360 to 700 ms at 4× and back to Kitchen 660 to 900 ms, which is mounting the 300-ingredient Kitchen and was not changed here.
- **Recipe ideas at scale.** The list draws rows in chunks of twenty that mount near the viewport (`LazyMount` with `defer-offscreen`; the first chunk is eager), a row is as tall as its lines, and each row's picture is a lazy `FallbackImage` (a photo idea adds one cached download-URL query), so 80 ideas mount 20 rows at first and the rest as they are scrolled to (checked on the fixture: 20 at first, all 80 reachable, 8 under "Made into recipes"). Measured on the fixture at 390×844 with the dev build at 4× CPU, loaded unthrottled and throttled only after the page had rendered, median of 5 rounds: the Ideas pill 317 ms until its first rows are drawn, "New idea" 267 ms until the list of ways shows, tapping an idea 204 ms until its drawer shows, back to the Recipes pill 482 ms. The three tab switches, median of 7 rounds each against the same data: Recipes to Kitchen 866 ms (base 922), Kitchen to Calendar 568 ms (base 588), Calendar to Recipes 607 ms (base 661), so they are unchanged within the noise of the throttled run (single rounds reach 1.0 to 1.5 s on the base too). No "Maximum update depth" or render storm in the console.
- **Cooking mode adds nothing to the screens that stay mounted.** It mounts only while open, reads three existing selectors (recipes, ingredients and the per-recipe cook counts) and, through `CookNotes`, the notes selectors and starts no listener (one `store.subscribe` callback runs the memoized recipe selector on a store change, to remember the last recipe); the overlay context value gained no field, so no tab re-renders for it. At 4× CPU on Alex's kitchen with a 40-ingredient recipe, click to the next painted frame took a median of 268 ms to open it, 72 ms for Next, 382 ms for the ingredients view with its 40 rows and 86 ms to come back to the steps (six rounds each), and the three tab switches stayed at 45 to 63 ms. The scale fixture wasn't loaded when this was measured, so the tabs were not checked against it. A plate session reads the cooks and cook-count maps once for all its recipes and memoizes the per-recipe records; on Taylor's scale kitchen at 4× CPU (390×844) Start cooking on a two-recipe plate painted the session in 609 ms and switching recipes took 127 to 185 ms (six switches).
- **Notes at scale.** Taylor's fixture holds about 310 notes across 160 of the 200 recipes (one has twelve, for "Show N more"; a fifth are tweaks, one with the eight-line maximum, and some follow one of the recipe's cooks), generated by their own random sequence so the rest of the fixture is unchanged. The summary and grouping are built once per snapshot, the drawer draws five entries and mounts the rest on "Show N more", `RecipeRow` is now `memo`, and a note's own text measures its clamp once per entry. Measured at 4× CPU on that fixture at 390px, click to the next change on screen (medians): the recipe drawer with twelve notes opened in about 260 ms and the note sheet from it in about 180 ms (five rounds); a save of a cooked recipe that records a tweak took about 1.3 s, and about 1.8 s against 1.5 s for the same edit to a recipe with no cook on another pair of recipes (both include `freezeBeforeEdit` and the write's round trip to the emulator; five rounds); the four tab switches took 0.47 to 0.82 s over eight rounds, the largest single one 1.24 s (0.56 to 0.9 s was normal before). Un-throttled, with the whole list unfiltered, opening and closing a drawer or adding a note re-rendered no `RecipeRow` (40 before the `memo`), and a recipe save re-rendered the 20 mounted rows once or twice, as it does without notes, because each recipe snapshot gives every recipe a new object. No render storm or "Maximum update depth" appeared in any run.
- **Care at scale.** Half of Taylor's 200 fixture recipes carry care (a fifth with all four parts, a fifth fridge-only, the rest in the older shape with no key), generated without a random draw so the rest of the fixture is unchanged. Care adds no listener, selector or per-row work: the drawer reads `getRecipeCare` for its one recipe when it renders, a list row never reads it, and cooking mode derives one string from the recipe it already holds. Measured at 390×844 with the dev build at 4× CPU (throttled only after the page had rendered), click to the next change on screen, medians of 7 rounds, two passes alternating the notes branch (before) and this branch (after) on the same data: opening a recipe drawer took 162 and 226 ms before and 160 and 204 ms after for a recipe with all four parts, 208 and 240 before and 206 and 235 after for a fridge-only one, and 182 and 214 before and 227 and 223 after for one in the older shape; the four tab switches took 415 to 969 ms before and 331 to 883 ms after, so everything is level within the noise of a shared machine (single rounds reach 1.0 to 1.6 s on both builds). No "Maximum update depth" or render storm appeared in any run. The fixture's other agents were using the same emulator while this ran, so absolute numbers are loose and only the before and after of one pass are comparable.
- **Fridge & freezer boxes at scale.** Taylor's fixture holds about 300 containers in stock (a recipe with twelve, to see its row, drawer rows and put-away history with many containers at once; three in ten recipes with none, ten saved meals with one; some fridge ones past their date) and 260 finished ones, from their own random sequence so the rest of the fixture is unchanged. `getStockMaps` is one pass into two `Map`s per snapshot, each row subscribes to its own entry with `shallowEqual` (a container changing re-renders only the rows whose numbers moved), `RecipesScreen`'s chunk height estimate adds a line only for rows that have stock, and the stock subview draws 20 rows per place with "Show N more". Finished containers are never held live: the page is read when the view opens; grouping 300 boxes into batches is one pass (`pinchMath` times it). Measured numbers are in the PR.
- **Cooks at scale.** Taylor's fixture holds about 330 cooks across 200 recipes (one recipe has twelve, for "Show N more"), generated by their own random sequence so the rest of the fixture is unchanged. The summary map is built once per snapshot, a Recipes row reads no cook count, a planned meal's rows re-render with any cook change (a meal has a handful of them), and the history sheet mounts only while open and draws eight rows first. The numbers are in the PR.



---

## Component & File Architecture

```
src/apps/pinch/
├── Pinch.tsx                       # sole orchestrator
├── README.md  UX.md  TECHNICAL.md  ISSUES.md
├── constants.ts  types.ts  pinch.css
├── catalog/
│   ├── index.ts  catalogIngredients.ts  catalogFamilies.ts  catalogStores.ts
├── hooks/
│   ├── usePinchSync.ts  usePinchTheme.ts  usePinchOverlay.ts  usePinchSession.ts  useFreezePastDays.ts  useStorageImageUrl.ts  useIdeaImageUrl.ts  useCookTimers.ts  useCookActions.ts  useCookNote.ts
│   │                                   useContainerActions.ts  useContainerDraft.ts  usePutAway.ts  useEatFromStockPlaces.ts   # put away and eat with their toasts; the three answers a box needs; cooking mode's "Putting any away?"; the places a box can be eaten from
│   │                                   useIdeaSaving.ts  useRecipeAdded.ts  useSourceShown.ts   # one idea save path for the sheet and the drawer; the "added" toast and where it goes; the Sources screen's "show this source's recipes" reaching the mounted Recipes tab
```
